`prepare-link` computes each **completed** group's semantic gap set from its own write receipts (§5.5; D118–D124, #110). A group owns exactly its anchor and the addresses its own `owned-gNN.txt` names: no body text, section number, older Card of the same Literature tree, or inferred address widens that scope. The former `names_section`/`cites_section` inference and the address/outbound-count fallback are gone; a receipt that names a missing Card, or two groups' receipts naming one Card, is an explicit preparation fault (`ownership fault`). A group whose `digest/gNNN@R` unit is not `done` (or, in a folder without a ledger, that has no owned receipt) is reported in `blocked` with an empty allow-list and a link file that names the reason (#92).

The gap set selects exactly three things. (1) A **Card with no valid kinded semantic Link** to another group or relevant earlier material: its `kinds` facts must hold a Link other than a plain `refers`, whose target is not its parent, not a source place (the book Card or any anchor), not register membership, and not a Card of its own group. (2) A **substantive orphan** by the existing authored-Link evidence (`graph::is_orphan` over every authored Link in both Workspaces). (3) The **anchor whose `在全书中的位置` paragraph needs writing**: the paragraph is missing, duplicated, or names no other part. Generated Reverse links are never authored body, so they cannot close a gap. A Card the gap set excludes is `skipped`.

Per group `prepare-link` writes `link-gNN.md` (the gap scope: only gap Cards, their reasons, bodies and candidates), `link-gap-gNN.json` (the frozen scope plus candidates and the archive revision), `allow-gNN.txt` (the gap addresses: the exact write fence) and `cards-all.md` (unchanged). The printed JSON is `{pending, no_gap, blocked, groups, owned_total, gap_total, skipped_total, pending_count, no_gap_count, scheduled, unowned, digest_state, cards_all, archive_revision}`: `pending` names the groups with gaps, `no_gap` the groups whose Link unit will settle without a provider run. Each group carries `owned`, `gaps`, `skipped` and its anchor `position` (`linked`, `anchor_position_missing`, `anchor_position_duplicate`, `anchor_position_unlinked`).

`zt-digest link-start -Out <out> -Group <n> -Token <n>` opens the claimed `link/gNNN@1` unit. It verifies the prepared gap scope against the unit's frozen owned manifest, writes `link-start-gNNN.json` (the job manifest) and `link-job-gNNN.md` (the binding, the gap Cards with their authored fingerprints, the draft schema and the SELF-CHECK command), and freezes the scope in the unit's progress. `expected_jobs` is 1 with gaps and 0 without: a gap-free group's empty checked draft is published by this command itself, so its unit settles with no provider run (AC07). The job file is the only source of the binding; the prepared file cannot widen it.

`zt-digest link-finish -Out <out> -Group <n> -Token <n> -Draft <file> [-CheckOnly]` completes the unit. It checks the current unexpired claim, takes the same per-unit OS guard, re-hashes the unit's frozen inputs and enumerates the owned scope from the group's published owned manifest. The frozen gap scope is the start's retained `link-gap-gNN.json`; for a unit claimed without a start it is the same classifier over the owned Cards, and a retained accepted plan is re-read rather than re-classified, so a lost final reply recovers the same scope instead of classifying Cards that attempt already changed.

The draft envelope is exactly `schema_version: 1`, `unit`, `token`, `input_hash`, `calls` and `dispositions`. `calls` may contain only `edit_card` with `at`, one authored base (`base_fingerprint` or `base_receipt`, never a raw `base`) and `owned_paragraph` (`text`, optional `replaces_receipt`, optional `adopt_legacy`). Every call must name a **gap** Card; a Card outside the frozen gap set is refused, so the job can never widen (AC01/AC02). One Card cannot be written twice in the same plan revision, and a bounded plan holds at most 64 calls and 1 MiB of owned-paragraph text. A gap Card needs exactly one outcome: a written paragraph, `already_linked`, `no_link` (also accepted as `none`), or `linked_from` naming another written Card whose paragraph kindedly links it. An owned Card outside the gap set receives a deterministic `already_linked` outcome that the checker re-verifies. A duplicate, invented, outside-scope or mixed outcome refuses the plan, and a gap Card with no outcome refuses it, so a silent omission is impossible.

A written paragraph must carry a kinded Link to a Card outside its group that is not the parent and not a source place; an anchor's written paragraph must start with `在全书中的位置`, and an anchor already holding that paragraph cannot receive a second one (`already_linked` must then show one linked position paragraph). `linked_from` requires the named source to be written and its paragraph to link the gap Card kindedly. `no_link` records the reason and leaves the unresolved required fault to the card cycle, which reports it (AC06). `-CheckOnly` runs the real transaction preview and rolls everything back, and the Link SELF-CHECK runs `L1,L2` over every owned Card. Normal completion retains `link-accepted-gNNN.json` without replacing an existing plan, applies through the archive receipts, rereads persisted fingerprints, and publishes `link-coverage-gNNN.json`, `link-self-check-gNNN.json` and `link-completion-gNNN.json`. The completion carries `gap`, `skipped`, `counts` (owned, gaps, skipped, linked, linked_from, already_linked, no_link), `quality` (the `L1,L2` faults and checked count) and the changed Cards. One short ledger transaction registers those files, the changed Cards and the archive receipts, marks the unit done and appends its completion event. A lost ledger commit is recovered from the retained plan and its receipts without duplicating the paragraph or publishing a second revision; a changed draft conflicts (`accepted_plan_changed`) instead of rewriting an accepted body (AC08).

Public coverage is `link-finish.tests.ps1` for the transport guards, and `link-gaps.tests.ps1` for the gap behavior: a real `prepare`, two completed groups through `group-start` → draft → `group-finish`, the real `cards-finish` dependency, `prepare-link`, `ledger next`, `link-start`, `link-finish`, an injected lost final reply, and the conflict of a changed body. It proves the exact write fence (the gap set; every new Card of group 1 is in the book's own tree), an older Card of an earlier book's Literature tree and another book's matching `§` Card are not writable, a source-place/parent-only/register Link does not close a gap, an orphan is reported instead of invented, an empty gap set starts no provider job and still settles its unit, one anchor keeps one `在全书中的位置` paragraph, and the older bodies, formulas and the other Cards stay byte-unchanged. No provider or actual digest workflow runs.

`snapshot-topics` calls one fresh `export_titles` per invocation, retaining the existing Workspace headers, type/facet marks and UTF-16 title truncation. `snapshot::read_title_trees` validates completeness/count/revision and adapts those rows to the existing renderer without sharing results between groups. The adapter restores the former binary tree/address input order, including multi-digit Topic ids, before the existing culture-sensitive rendering and candidate-ranking sorts. The API retains numeric ZET order.

`prepare-link` uses that title catalogue, kept to the Literature trees of both Workspaces (`snapshot::read_literature_trees`), one `Zt::cards(..., "text")` read and the archive-wide gap facts (`list_links` and both `get_register` views) in the same MCP process. It compares revisions, keeps existing-link exclusion, and uses the old ranking inputs: full bodies for non-root agent candidates, compact excerpts for human Cards. No Topic-tree Card is a candidate, and `link_gap.rs` (which reads the same Literature catalogue) drops every Link from or to a Topic tree before it classifies, so such a Link closes no gap of a Literature Card. Its global candidate-ranking corpus still requires one bulk text pass over the catalogue; it does not read each tree or calculate a model per Card. Limit errors reduce chunks, and missing required input stops the command. The gap classifier (`link_gap.rs`) uses the same facts as `link-finish`, so the two cannot disagree about which Cards a Link job may touch. The group and whole-book Card cycles, the global audit and every standalone checker mode read their required scope the same way, so #67's read migration is complete.

`prepare-link`'s ownership is receipt-based (#92, refined by #110): a group owns exactly its anchor and the addresses in its `owned-gNN.txt`. The former `names_section`/`cites_section` inference, the address-based Literature-tree inference and the eight-outbound structure guard are all gone. A Card is owned only when the run's own receipt names it (AC03), so no older body, whole older tree or inferred section-number match becomes writable; since a group writes only its book's Literature tree, every owned Card is in that tree. A group is pending only when its `digest/gNNN@R` unit is `done` in the staging ledger; any other state (or, in a folder without a ledger, a missing owned receipt) is reported in `blocked` with an empty allow-list and a link file that names the reason. A receipt naming a missing Card, or one Card named by two groups, is a preparation fault that refuses the whole command. `link-prepare` logs the blocked groups and the scheduled/skipped counts, `link-pass` works only the gap set, and the run report carries `link_blocked` and `link_gaps`.

`call <file|-> -OperationPrefix <run/unit/positive-plan-revision/step>` is the identified write transport. It accepts an immutable, dependency-ordered list of the four `apply_batch` write tools, validates supported calls/earlier references/single-item size before writing, then sends deterministic chunks of at most 64 items and 1 MiB of authored input. Stable item keys use the original accepted-list index. Cross-chunk references resolve through recovered receipt addresses; within-chunk references remain local. It holds one MCP process per command and creates no placeholders for forward references.

Every receipt is historical evidence. Before the identified command reports success, it bulk-rereads the final output per address and compares the authored fingerprint, including type/model/math/BibTeX metadata and excluding generated Reverse links. It also verifies a common archive revision. An independent edit or missing output fails verification without overwriting that Card. A failed chunk leaves earlier committed chunks recoverable; repeat the same accepted input and prefix from the beginning. Only each chunk is atomic. Successful output retains the existing `N ok <address>` lines; failures cannot be resumed with the legacy `-From/-Addresses` pair. This mode explicitly refuses legacy log/move/Topic-gate option combinations rather than silently bypassing their guards. Manual calls retain their existing transport.

`terms -Step apply -Apply -OperationPrefix ...` is the first compiler adoption. It atomically retains an immutable `terms/accepted-<prefix-hash>.json` operation plan with `tempfile::persist_noclobber` before calling the archive. A retry reuses that plan, preserving whether the accepted operation was a create or guarded edit. Changed rendered input under the same prefix is refused; a newly accepted revision gets another retained plan. The file records accepted input, not commit authority: only the archive receipt establishes a commit. The compiler uses the same output reread/fingerprint verification and returns receipt keys with its normal result. Model-compiler adoption, ledger/fencing and manager-process qualification remain unfinished under the implementation plan.

Every identified edit has an authored base: a direct edit provides `base_fingerprint` or `base_receipt`; an edit at an earlier `@@N` result automatically receives that operation's receipt as its base. Raw single-edit bases are refused in identified mode. `calls::authored_fingerprint` reconstructs the archive's standard-JSON projection from a complete lean read, distinct from the legacy PowerShell JSON renderer. The terms compiler retains that fingerprint in an accepted edit plan. An unrelated incoming Link may change generated Reverse text between read and apply without causing a false conflict; authored title/body/type/model/math/BibTeX changes remain conflicts.

The former structure/index guard and outbound-count inference are removed with the rest of the ownership fallback (#110): an unlogged Card now belongs to no group, whatever its body, its section numbers or its outbound count. A receipt is the only ownership evidence.

Normal `card` group cycles (`-Groups` with `-NoOrphans`, without requested book-context rules) read their exact owned manifests/anchors and the required book term table through bounded text batches. Parent context uses fresh catalogue titles. Whole-book cycles, and group cycles requesting book-context checks, read the book plus declared owned Topic Cards and Topic Cards already attributed to the book. One indexed Link list supplies membership/incoming addresses; only necessary external Idea/Finding carriers need authored bodies for kinded inbound checks. Known navigation anchors need no extra body read. No Card cycle invokes the global hub/Bridge report.

The existing `book_index` and offline checker retain category and quality semantics. Explicit ownership keeps an unsourced Topic Card in the checked book scope, where it can receive source/term faults. Human ownership and another Literature tree are refused. An unreadable declared staging folder, missing owned manifest, missing required body or changed revision stops planning. Required external carriers/parents have category `context`, so they cannot become writable work or orphan candidates.

The retained 10,895-Card release comparison in `docs/digest-bulk-command-benchmark.json` verifies exact snapshot, Card-plan state/fault and all 343 link-preparation file projections. Candidate medians are 0.592 s for snapshot, 2.4–2.7 s for the Card steps and 7.963 s for prepare-link, using one MCP process per command. These are local command measurements on preserved archive copies, not workflow throughput. These retained timings precede the shared orphan semantics described above; its actual scope/revision evidence is qualified separately. The 2026-10-18 [requalification](../digest-bulk-command-reverify-2026-10-18.json) on the same fixture measured 0.690 s, 2.6–2.7 s and 10.9 s with unchanged call counts, one MCP process each, and the same output facts apart from the documented #75/#76 rule deltas.

`before`/`after` artifacts label the group/book scope, checked addresses and revision, with archive-global checks `not_run` and book relationship checks labelled separately. The public global `audit` remains the independent report. Fixture language/Link and orphan/Event quality faults match that report for the same attributed Cards; explicit owned Cards with missing attribution are also checked. The retained command comparison and the shared-set tests qualify separate performance and consistency gates under #67. Host execution incidents are recorded in `docs/windows-defender-build-investigation.md` and the delivery status.

### Command line

```text
zt-digest call <file|-> [-Open] [-From N -Addresses a0,a1,...] [-Log f] [-Allow f] [-Beneath f] [-Moves f] [-SrcLog f] [-TopicGate f] [-TopicSimilarity 0.8]
zt-digest call [-Open] -Tool <tool> [-Json '{...}']
zt-digest call <file|-> [-Open] -OperationPrefix <run/unit/plan-revision/step>
zt-digest service-wait [-Wait 0..120] [-Out <folder>]
zt-digest audit [-CitationKey <key>] -Out <folder> [-MaxSentences 12] [-MaxChars 400] [-MaxSteps 6] [-Throttle N] [-Chunk N] [-Sample 15] [-SkipModels]
zt-digest concepts -Step propose|prepare|judge|report|decision -Staging <folder> [-Work <folder>] [-File <json>] [-MaxTopics 10] [-MaxRestatements 10]
zt-digest join -Step plan|apply -Work <folder> [-File <selection.json>] [-Similar 0.5] [-MaxPairs 10] [-MaxOperations 4]

zt-digest topic-home -Step apply -Work <folder> [-File <selection.json>] -OperationPrefix <run/unit/plan-revision/step>

zt-digest register -Step plan|apply|check -Work <folder> [-Review <concept-review.json>[;<another.json>…]] [-Readings <file>]
zt-digest publish -Step plan|apply|check -Work <folder> [-Register <register-plan.json>] [-Hub <hub-plan.json>] [-File <selection.json>]
zt-digest bridge -Step plan|precheck|report|apply -Staging <folder> -CitationKey <key> [-Work <folder>] [-Profile book|math] [-MaxSources 60] [-Candidates 5] [-Books a,b] [-Job br-NNN] [-ReportId r-…] [-Accept ids|all] [-AcceptEarlier ids|all] [-Apply]
zt-digest bridge -Step project -Report <proposals.json> -At <address>
zt-digest math -Step <step> -Staging <folder> [-Out <folder>] [-UnitsPerJob 4] [-Supply examples] [-MainResults 7] [-CoreBudget 3] [-MaxSymbols 80] [-Apply]
zt-digest ledger init|status|gate|next|renew|done|fail|rerun-list|resume|publish|notes|relocate|math|reclaim|job|resource -Out <folder> [-Layer <layer>] [-Key <key>] [-Scope <file>] [-Suggestions <file>] [-Moves <file>] [-Take 1..64 -Owner <id> [-Wait 0..600] [-Lease 1..7200]] [-Unit <id> -Token <n> -Outputs <file>] [-Class retryable|content|operator -Fault <file>] [-Register <file>| -Observe <file>| -Cancel <id>| -List] [-Recover <file>]
zt-digest cleanup-finish -Out <folder> -Unit <cleanup unit> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest cleanup-finish -Out <folder> -Ready [-Owner <label>]
zt-digest cleanup-run -Out <folder> -Step plan|apply [-Owner <label>]
zt-digest finish-batch -Out <folder> -Units "<unit>:<token>,…"
zt-digest finish-batch -Out <folder> -Layer cards|events|findings|models|explain -Owner <label> [-Take 1..8] [-Wait 0..600] [-Lease <s>]
zt-digest group-start -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n>
zt-digest group-finish -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n> -Draft <file> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest link-start -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n>
zt-digest link-finish -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n> -Draft <file> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest cards-finish -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest book-cards-finish -Out <folder> -Work <cards work> [-Unit cards-book/book@N] -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest events-finish -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest findings-finish -Out <folder> -Group <n> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest model-finish -Out <folder> -Model <address> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest explain-finish -Out <folder> -Model <address> -Token <n> [-CheckOnly]
zt-digest model-deps -Out <folder> -Model <address> -Token <n>
zt-digest model-deps -Out <folder> -Units "<models unit>:<token>,…"
zt-digest event -Step plan|precheck|apply|report -Staging <folder> [-Work <folder>] [-Job <id>] [-GroupsPerJob 3] [-Apply]
zt-digest finding -Step plan|precheck|apply|report -Staging <folder> [-Work <folder>] [-Job <id>] [-GroupsPerJob 3] [-MaxCandidates 16] [-Apply]
zt-digest terms -Step plan|apply -Staging <folder> [-MaxTerms 60] [-MaxCandidates 160] [-Apply] [-OperationPrefix <run/unit/plan-revision/step>]
zt-digest skeleton -Step plan|apply -Staging <folder> -Work <folder> [-Audit <audit.json>] [-Thesis <text>] [-Decisions <file>] [-Apply]
zt-digest organize-survey (-Staging <folder> | -Topics <n,m,…>) -Work <folder> [-Notes <file>] [-Similar 0.5] [-CrossLinks 4] [-Small 3]
zt-digest organize-apply -Work <folder> [-DryRun] [-Open]
zt-digest figures -CitationKey <key> [-FileId 0] [-Staging <folder>] [-Source <md>] [-MinFigureBytes 3000] [-Report <file>] [-Batch 40] [-FiguresFile <json>] [-NoDocuments] [-Apply]
zt-digest prepare [-Source <md>] -Out <folder> -CitationKey <key> [-BibTeX <bibtex>] [-Book <work id|title>] [-BookTitle ''] [-StartHeading 导论] [-EndHeading 参考] [-SkipHeadings ''] [-MaxChars 20000] [-MaxUnits 3] [-MinUnitChars 1000] [-FileId 0] [-MinFigureBytes 3000] [-MaxEventCandidates 12] [-Instructions dsh-digest-base.zh.md,dsh-model-cards.zh.md] [-NoEventCandidates] [-NoFigures] [-Apply]
zt-digest prepare-link -Staging <folder> [-TitleChars 40]
zt-digest snapshot-topics -Out <file> [-TitleChars 40]
zt-digest version
```

Parameter names ignore case. `-Name:value` works. A switch is `-Name` alone (or `-Name:$false`). An unknown name fails, as in PowerShell. A value that does not follow a name is positional: the file of `call`; for the other tools it binds to the next parameter, in the script's `param(...)` order, that was not named, as in PowerShell (`util::bind_positional`), and a value left over fails.

### `call`

Input: a UTF-8 JSON file, or `-` for stdin, holding one call or an array of calls (a BOM is ignored). Output, one line per call in order: `N ok <address>` for a create, edit, append, move, insert, or delete call that returned an address; `N <compact JSON>` for any other call (the reply text as is when it is not JSON). On the first failure: `N error <message>` (white space around line breaks collapsed), then `resume: -From N -Addresses a0,a1,...`, and exit code 1. A typed refusal also prints one `<code> <compact JSON>` line immediately before the resume line (`card_locked {"error":"card_locked","tree":"K","reason":"create-inside",...}`), so a Lock refusal keeps the archive's own tree, Lock id, reason and hint across this helper boundary; the message reads `<code>: <the archive's sentence>`. An identified chunk (`-OperationPrefix`) that the archive refuses answers the same JSON in its error and writes nothing, while its earlier committed chunks stay committed.

| Option | Effect |
|---|---|
| `-Open` | run in the open scope. Without it the helper runs in the Literature scope, as every digest step does (see Concepts): a `create_topic`, a write to a Topic-tree Card, a Hub Card or authored text that links a Topic-tree Card fails as `N error literature_only: <tool>: …` before it reaches the archive, and the calls after it do not run. zt-idea-grow, the hub and register work, the tests' fixtures (`zt-calls.ps1`) and hand Topic work pass it; `skills/zt/scripts/zt-mcp.ps1` forwards it |
| `-From N -Addresses a0,...` | skip the calls before N; their addresses still resolve `@@` |
| `-Log f` | append the address of every Card a `create*` call makes |
| `-Allow f` | only these Cards (one per line) and Cards made in this run may be the `at` of `edit_card`, `append_card`, `create_successor`; `create_topic`, `move_card`, `insert_card`, `delete_card`, `create_literature`, `edit_literature_metadata` fail |
| `-Moves f` | append `{"i":N,"moved":{...}}` per call whose answer has a non-empty `moved` map |
| `-SrcLog f` | append `<address>\t<src>` per Card made from a call with `src`; `src` never reaches zt |
| `-Beneath f` | the listed Cards (one per line) may take one `create_successor` with `kind: side` and nothing else; a Direct successor, an edit, an unlisted Card and a human parent are refused. A helper option for hand work: the digest offers no place-beside Card (see "Filing a standalone claim beside earlier thought") |
| `-TopicGate f` | with `-Open` (a Literature-scope helper refuses `create_topic` first): lock `f` (no sharing; wait up to 120 s) around `create_topic`; refuse a title equal to or similar to (character-bigram Dice ≥ `-TopicSimilarity`) an agent Topic, naming it: ``topic exists: `<root>` <title>. File the Card under that Topic instead of creating one.`` |

`append_card { at, text }` reads the Card and appends `text` through `edit_card { at, body: <body without trailing space> + "\n\n" + <text trimmed> }`.

### The box pin

`main` applies `zt_check::mcp::boxpin::apply` to the tool's arguments before anything else (see [zt-check](zt-check.md), "The box pin"): a `zt-box.txt` in the staging folder points the whole invocation, and every `zt mcp` and `zt scholar` child, at a copy of the box. An error (an empty pin, two boxes, a `ZT_CONFIG_DIR` naming another box) prints `zt-digest <tool>: …` and exits 1 before any archive call. `service-wait -Out <staging>` shows which box a pinned run reaches (`archive_root`).

### `service-wait`

`zt-digest service-wait [-Wait 0..120] [-Out <folder>]` is the explicit stage health check (R320). `-Wait` defaults to 120 and is validated as a whole number of seconds from 0 through 120 before any work. It polls `zt status` at 500 ms intervals through `zt_check::mcp::ensure_service`, never launching a daemon itself; after a valid restart it opens one client (the reconnection) and prints one compact JSON line with `ok`, `state` (`up` or `unobserved`), `waited_ms`, the daemon `pid`, `archive_root`, `archive_revision`, `last_exit_abnormal`, `reconnected` and the `stage` when `-Out` was given. A service that stays down prints `{ok:false, code:"infrastructure_fault", error:…}` and exits 4, leaving every unit and file as it was. `-Out` also records the active-run marker for that staging folder.

### `audit`

The port of `digest-audit.ps1`; [Digest scripts](digest-scripts.md) describes what it reports. It makes only read calls: one fresh title export, bounded lean Card batches, `get_model` for required scoped models unless `-SkipModels`, `list_hubs` and `list_broken_links`. Its archive-wide category, attribution, Bridge and orphan report requires the complete graph; the attribution (`audit/book_of.rs`) gives a Card to a book only when it is in that book's Literature tree, so a Topic-tree Card belongs to no book however it links one (the audit still counts the box's Topic trees and hubs as information, never as a digest fault); the indexed lean facts supply outgoing relations and complete inbound counts. Bodies are reused from that read, without a second per-Card pass. Model contexts are read only for the selected report scope.

Missing required input or a revision change is an explicit error, so it cannot produce a successful zero-fault report. A completed report records `complete: true` and `archive_revision`, after checking that the graph, model and relationship inputs share the revision. `-Throttle` and `-Chunk` remain accepted integer compatibility options; server limits control adaptive batches and the command always uses one client. It runs zt-check `--json` in-process, once per model and once per book. Those `--json` calls carry a bundle whose `context` is the same complete title projection the audit already read, so I6 compares each checked Idea title with its own Workspace without a second read, and every entry names its `workspace`, `tree`, `relations` (I7), and `book`. Each book's style block splits the standalone R20/R21 advice from blocking language faults (`advisory`, `advice_by_rule`), and its layer block splits every published `(advisory)` line — I1, I6, E5, L3, and I7's unknown-source line — from blocking layer faults, so `layer.faults` holds only lines that are hard by rule and by text. A `-CitationKey` that is not an agent Literature tree fails with ``no agent Literature tree `<key>` ``.

| Output | Content |
|---|---|
| stdout | `read N Cards in M trees (S s)`, `read N bodies, M models (S s)`, `Card language: N faults (S s)`, then the summary as compact JSON |
| `audit.json` | the report: `generated`, `complete`, `archive_revision`, `scope`, `limits`, `seconds`, `workspace`, `books[]`, and `baseline` (#94 S01) |
| `audit.md` | the report for people |
| `audit-faults.txt` | every Card language and Card layer fault line, sorted by address |
| `audit-cards.json` | every Card checked outside a model, with body, Links, kinds, their `relations`, inbound Links, category, tree, book, parent |
| `audit-addresses.txt` | every agent Card address |
| `audit-sources.json` | every Card's explicit source evidence: the `direct` books, the `derived` ones and their `derived_from` heads, `unknown`, `multiple`, beside the inferred attribution, which is the Card's Literature tree or none (D068–D074, #94 S01) |

#### `baseline`: the reproducible Luhmann baseline (#94 S01)

`src/audit/measures.rs` adds one section, `report.baseline` (schema 1), and writes `audit-sources.json`. Nothing here starts a monitoring service, adds telemetry storage, or writes a Card. It reads five optional named inputs and reports each one it did not read as missing evidence:

| Argument | What it supplies |
|---|---|
| `-Baseline <folder\|audit.json>` | an earlier report; the `audit-addresses.txt` beside it defines the new Cards of the interval |
| `-ConceptReport <folder\|concept-review.json>` | a reviewed concept report (#97); a `decision.json` beside it, or `-Decisions`, counts the resolved restatement pairs |
| `-RunReport <folder\|run-report.json>` | the run's Link jobs (`counts.per_layer.link`) and its exact per-run usage |
| `-TaskSheet <sheet.json>` | the fixed manual task sheet |
| `-SampleSeed <int>` (default 1), `-Sample <int>` (default 15) | the annotation sample: the scope's Idea Cards, ordinal, shuffled by .NET's seeded `System.Random`, cut to `-Sample` |

`evidence` records what a reader needs to reproduce the numbers: `archive {id, path, revision}` (from `Zt::archive_view`), `software {zt_digest, zt_check, zt, rules, advisory_rules}` (the zt version comes from the server's own `initialize` answer, `Zt::server_version`), `thresholds`, `scope {kind: workspace|book, name, workspace, books, cards, trees}`, `sample {kind, rule, seed, population, drawn, addresses}`, `inputs`, and `missing[]` with the measures each gap leaves unknown. `baseline.complete` is true only when every named input was read and valid; the report's top-level `complete` keeps its older meaning (the archive graph read is consistent).

`sources` keeps the explicit evidence set apart from the existing inferred attribution (D067–D074): the checker's own `source_evidence` (a `来源` line's Citation keys, else the Card's Literature tree) plus D071's inheritance for a Model part with no direct source (`derived`, `derived_from` the head). `unknown` and `multiple` stay as they are, a semantic Link to another book is no evidence, and `differences[]` shows the Cards where the inference and the evidence disagree.

`measures` holds P1, P2, P3_P5, P4, P7, P8, P9, P11, P12_P13, P14 and P15 (design §14.3). Each is `{known, measure, values{value, denominator, …}, rate{per_100, of, unit}, note, reason}`; a measure whose evidence is missing is `known: false` with its `reason`, never a zero. `comparison` carries an earlier report's value with the scope it was measured on and gives a `delta` only when both scopes are the same; otherwise every row is `scope_mismatch` with both scopes named. `proposal` retains the accepted proposal's own counts with their original scope (the whole live agent Workspace, 10,887 Cards) and is never a delta. `task_sheet` is the validated fixed sheet: schema 1, kinds `first-useful-card`, `kept-spread`, `distant-card`, plus `removed_cards`, `human_replies`, and `annotation` of the sampled addresses. A sheet outside the schema is refused before the report is published; an observation the archive contradicts (a `useful_address` that is no Card, a removed Card that still exists) is a reported fault that leaves its measures unknown.

`audit.md` renders all of it under `## Baseline measures (#94 S01)`, including `No measure below is reported as a zero` when every input was read.

### `finalize`

The finite finalization branch of a prepared run (#86, R299/R300). It works from the run's own ledger and its published audit revision; it writes no Card prose and never regenerates the book. Four steps, one immutable plan:

- `-Step plan -Out <out> [-Report <audit.json>]` publishes the scheduling audit revision when it is still open (from the audit stage's report, which becomes the revision's checked `report` artifact) and derives `<out>/finalize/audit-plan.json` and `audit-plan.md`. Every fault line of `audit-faults.txt` gets one disposition: `fix` (a writable Card — idea, event, finding, anchor — whose audited authored projection still matches the current archive), `conflict` (the Card changed after the audit it scheduled, so a rewrite from that stale base is refused), `blocked` (a Model, Model part, Hub, structure or term-table Card: correcting it would reopen a done build), `stale` (the audited Card no longer exists) or `advisory` (I1, I6, E5, L3 and `N1`, which never block a run). The branch is `no_fix`, `fix`, `conflict` or `unresolved`, and the plan declares `fix_cycle {permitted: 1, used: 0, rules, cards, card_list}`: the exact Cards and rules the one correction cycle may touch, written to `<out>/finalize/fix-cards.txt` for `card -Step plan -Cards`. The plan also declares the refresh layers with their applicable reason. A recorded cycle (`fix-cycle.json`) makes the plan immutable and refuses a second plan or scope.
- `-Step scope -Out <out> [-FixWork <dir>]` records the cycle's exact changed-Card/touched-tree manifest once, into `<out>/finalize/changes.json`: per changed Card its kinds (`structure`, `links`, `figures`, `new`), category, tree and before/after authored projection, the touched trees, and a `change_hash`. It compares the audited projection (title, body, facet) with the current archive, so a projection difference is never mistaken for a real edit. A second scope is refused: the one cycle is spent.
- `-Step refresh -Out <out> -Layer <layer> [-Changes <file>] -Report <path> [-Scope <hub-scope.json>] [-Coverage <file>]` publishes and completes one affected aggregate publication at a new revision whose declared `scope` is that manifest, through the same `publication_finish` boundary (`consumed`, `unresolved`, `disposition`, exact artifacts, coverage). The identical manifest reuses the registered revision. Only the publications this branch re-derives are refreshed — the Link check (from the cycle's own report) and the final audit; the layers whose own stage generates their content (Organize's Opus decisions, the skeleton job, the Hubs plan, the Bridge judges, the mathematics theory summary) are reported `stage_owned` with that reason and are never republished from an artifact this branch did not re-derive.
- `-Step final -Out <out> [-Report <audit.json>]` runs the read-only audit again into `<out>/finalize/final-audit` (or verifies a given report): a report at or before the scheduling revision is refused, so the final audit is always after the corrections and refreshes. It publishes the next `audit/book@N` revision with the change scope and writes `<out>/finalize/final-report.json` and `final-report.md`: the new `graph_revision`, the scheduling and final audit revisions, the change facts, every refresh fact, every residual fault by class (`hard`, `source`, `infrastructure`) and the status — `done` only when no hard/source/infrastructure fault remains, otherwise `partial`.

`finalize` never loops: the plan permits exactly one cycle, `scope` records it, and every later step is idempotent on the same manifest. Its public coverage is `finalize.tests.ps1` (a scratch archive, a real `prepare -Variant econ -Apply`, the real `zt-digest audit`, a real `card` cycle scoped by the plan, and all four branches) plus the unit tests for the dispositions, the branches, the refresh applicability and the change kinds.

### `gate`

The concept-meaning Topic gate of a managed digest (#100, S07; design §7.1, §7.2; D139–D154, D302, D337). The existing exact/near-title check stays the fast first check; beyond it the gate compares the proposed Topic's **question** with every agent Topic's title and question, the accepted register terms and the run's book term rows, shortlists at most ten plausible Topics, reads representative Cards for that shortlist, and freezes the evidence. `gate.rs` takes no lock while preparing or judging; only creation takes the run's **own Topic gate file** — `<Staging>\topic-gate.lock` (`gate::run_gate`; `prepare` names it in no group's write command and `group-finish` does not guard with it, because a digest creates no Topic) — revalidates the decision against the live concept inputs and holds the lock until the receipt is known. It is that one file and not a second one beside it: two files would let a managed group writer and this gate create the same Topic twice (D148, D154).

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Staging>\gate`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prepare -Title <t> -Question <q>` | `export_titles`, bounded `get_cards` text batches for every agent Topic and the shortlist's representatives, `get_register`; the run's `terms.md` from disk | `evidence.json` (frozen, id = content hash), `prepare.json` | `{schema_version, step, outcome: new\|judge\|incomplete, complete, evidence, evidence_id, inputs_fingerprint, proposed, fast, shortlist, missing, lock}` |
| `decide -Answer <file>` | the frozen `evidence.json` and the answer file (duplicate JSON keys refused) | `decision.json` (frozen, `id` = content hash) | the decision: `{verdict, candidates, reason, evidence, selected_topic?, selected_entry?, missing?, evidence_id, inputs_fingerprint, lock, id}` |
| `create [-Decision <file>] -OperationPrefix <run/unit/rev/step>` | first a `apply_batch` preview of the one `create_topic` item (a committed operation answers `replayed`), then the same thing the decision read: Topics, register and term rows | `create.json`, `gate-report.json`/`.md` | `{created, address, receipt, verdict, title, competing}` or `{created:false, reason, detail, missing?}` |
| `report` | the three frozen files only | `gate-report.json`/`.md` | the report with its prepared outcome, shortlist size, evidence completeness, `missing`, verdict, reason, result and bounded guarantee (both files) |

The **fast check** normalises titles (`calls::title_key`: case, spaces and punctuation dropped) and reports exact and near (Dice over character bigrams ≥ 0.8) matches. Its exact branch is a hard refusal at creation: an agent Topic whose normalised title equals the proposal is the same Topic whatever a judge said about the shortlist. The **shortlist** adds the TF-IDF order (`Relatedness::rank_text_scored`, keeping only a cosine ≥ 0.15 and ≥ 0.35 of the best match so a card sharing one common bigram is not a candidate) and any Topic that names a term the proposal also names through the book table or the register. A Topic's representatives are its own Topic Card and up to three Regular Cards of its tree, read in one bounded batch with their source evidence; a matched term row is carried as a labelled definition (`terms.md`, its meaning and words not to use) when the meaning differs.

`prepare` reports `outcome: new` only when the shortlist is empty and the concept inputs are complete; `outcome: judge` when a shortlist needs a decision; and `outcome: incomplete` when the register or term table could not be compared. An absent register (`status: absent`) or absent term table (`status: absent`) is a fact — there are no accepted terms to compare — and is reported in `optional_missing`; an unreadable term table is `failed` and two registers are `ambiguous`, both of which leave `complete: false`, because an unknown is not a clearance. `create` then refuses with `incomplete_evidence` (exit 3) and names the missing inputs, so nothing becomes a new Topic from evidence the gate could not read (D302). `evidence.inputs_fingerprint` is the content hash of the proposed title and question, every agent Topic's title and question, the accepted register terms and the book term rows — nothing else, so an unrelated Card edit does not stale a decision while a new or reworded Topic does (D150).

`decide` validates one answer against the frozen evidence: the same proposed title, question and fingerprint; a `verdict` of `same`/`different`/`uncertain`; one candidate line per shortlisted Topic (no unknown address, no repeat); a `different` decision that accounts for **every** shortlisted Topic, marks none of them `same`, and selects no existing Topic; a `same` decision with an existing shortlisted Topic and a read Regular Card that is also marked `same`; an `uncertain` decision with a non-empty `missing` list; a `reason`; cited `evidence` limited to addresses the gate read (empty only for an empty shortlist); and no unknown field. A refused answer writes nothing (D302: a failure is never a create).

`create` is receipt-owned (D153). It first previews the one `create_topic` operation under `<OperationPrefix>/item-000000`: a committed operation answers `replayed` and the step returns that address before any new concept decision, so a lost response can never create a second Topic; a replay whose payload changed is refused by the archive's own operation key (`operation key conflict`, exit 1), so a committed key is never reinterpreted. It then answers a `same` or `uncertain` decision from its frozen verdict (a `same` answer names the Topic and its Regular entry; an `uncertain` one names the missing context) after rechecking the fingerprint, and for a `different` decision takes the run's Topic gate file, re-reads the concept inputs, refuses `stale_decision` (exit 3) when they changed, refuses an exact-title Topic, and writes through `calls::invoke_identified` while holding the lock. A second managed writer that arrives while the first holds the file waits for it, then reports `stale_decision` (the Topics changed) or the exact-title refusal; its regenerated decision names the committed Topic in `fast.exact` and cannot create a second one. After the receipt it reads the created Card back, verifies the agent Workspace and Topic kind, and reports any other agent Topic with the same normalised title as `competing`: the gate coordinates managed digest processes only, and a raw Owner or GUI creation is outside it (D154).

`zt-digest call … -OperationPrefix <prefix> -ConceptGate <decision.json>` is the identified helper's own form of the same gate: the one combination the legacy options cannot express, so it is permitted there and nowhere else. A batch with an uncommitted `create_topic` takes the lock and revalidates through `gate::acquire`, holds it across the chunk's `apply_batch`, and refuses a Topic title the decision did not clear; a committed operation is replayed by that same call, before the gate. `-ConceptGate` without `-OperationPrefix`, and the legacy `-TopicGate`/`-TopicSimilarity` beside `-OperationPrefix`, stay refused.

The public outcomes are fixed. `zt-digest gate` exits **0** for an answered step (`prepare` with `outcome: new|judge|incomplete`, `decide`, `report`, or a creation that returns `replayed`, `same`, `uncertain` or an exact-title refusal), **3** when the decision is stale or the concept evidence incomplete, and **1** for a refused or malformed input (an unknown verdict or field, an unread citation, a repeated JSON key, an operation-key conflict). The helper's `-ConceptGate` form exits **0** for an applied or replayed batch and **1** for any refusal, naming it (a stale decision, an exact title, a `same`/`uncertain` decision, or a title the decision did not clear). A refusal always names the reason in `detail` (or `missing`), and no refusal and no failure writes a Card or a Topic.

Public coverage: `gate.tests.ps1` (69 assertions) drives `prepare`/`decide`/`create`/`report` and the `-ConceptGate` form of `call` on a scratch archive with two spellings of equilibrium multiplicity, two senses of learning, an unrelated Topic, an accepted register term and a book term table. It checks the shortlist and its ten-Topic bound (twelve term-named Topics fill it), that the title check alone misses the second spelling while the concept stage finds it, the TF-IDF order reaching a Topic that names no term row, the exact-title refusal, the register term and the labelled definition, the representative reads, that preparing and judging never wait for the run gate while a creation waits for it and writes nothing until released, the answer refusals (partial `different`, contradiction, unknown verdict, unread citation, repeated JSON key), the `same` and `uncertain` answers, the locked creation with its receipt, the replay that creates no second Topic, a changed-payload replay refused by its operation key, the stale decision, an unrelated Card edit that does not stale one, a third learning sense kept apart and created, the empty complete shortlist clearing `new` beside a bare one that reports the absent register and term table, an unreadable term table and two registers that both report `incomplete` and refuse a creation by `incomplete_evidence`, the helper's gate form with its own replay and its refusal of a title the decision did not clear, two concurrent managed writers that leave one Topic, the legacy refusals, and the report's evidence completeness, missing/ambiguous input and guarantee in both files. The module's unit tests cover the answer rules, the fingerprint, the question text, the term-table rows (absent, read and unreadable), the shortlist rules and the competing-creation report.

### `concepts`

The concept, restatement and filing review of one prepared run (#97, design §4.2, §4.4, §12; D063–D066, D076–D082, D287, D288, D300, D302). `concepts.rs` is a compiler with five explicit steps and no query language, and it has **no Card-write path at all**: `propose`, `prepare` and `judge` touch only staging files (and `prepare` reads the archive), `report` and `decision` touch only staging files.

`-Staging` is the run's staging folder (`manifest.json` names the book's Citation key). The one input the tool does not derive is `<Work>\proposals.json` (default `<Staging>\concepts`, and `<Work>` defaults to it), which the caller freezes: per proposed concept a `title`, a `question`, and optionally `statement`, `assumptions`, `formulas`, `sources` and the `terms` names the book's term table defines. Unknown fields, a duplicate title, a term the book's table does not define, more than 20 proposals, or a `book` other than the manifest's are refused. `propose` derives that file from the run's own evidence (see below), so a digest run never asks an agent to invent a concept.

| Step | Reads | Writes | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `propose` | `<Staging>\terms.md` through zt-check's own row parser; no archive call | `<Work>\proposals.json` | `{ok, step, book, term_rows, proposals, omitted, path, terms[]}`, or `{…, proposals: 0, path: null, reason}` when the run has no term row |
| `prepare` | one `zt mcp`: `archive_view`, `export_titles`, bounded `get_cards` text batches | `evidence.json`, `endpoints\<hash>.md` (one retained body per endpoint), `cj-01.md`, `dsh-concept-instructions.zh.md` | `{ok, evidence_id, archive_id, archive_revision, proposals, term_rows, topic_candidates, topic_selected, topic_unselected, restatement_candidates, restatement_selected, restatement_unselected, unread, attempts, evidence, jobs[]}` |
| `judge -File f` | the frozen evidence and the answer file | `judged.json` | `{ok, evidence_id, judged_id, judged, pairs, unjudged, file}` |
| `report` | the frozen `evidence.json` and `judged.json` only — it starts no client | `<Staging>\concept-review.json`, `<Staging>\concept-review.md` | `{ok, report_id, status, complete, findings, judged, unjudged, unread, written, paths[]}` |
| `decision -File f` | `<Staging>\concept-review.json` and the reviewed decision | `decision.json` | `{ok, report_id, decision_id, accepted, file}` |

`propose` (S23 / #115) freezes the run's proposed concepts from the book term table the Terms stage wrote: one proposal per row, its `title` the book's preferred word, its `question` the book's own meaning (`本书的「术语」：意思`, the same kind of text a Topic stores as its question), and its `terms` the accepted and the rejected spellings. The hashes of a repeated term collapse to one proposal, at most 20 are written, and `omitted` counts the rest. A run without a term table proposes nothing: it prints `proposals: 0` with its reason and writes no file, so the workflow reports the review as skipped instead of comparing an invented concept. The step makes no archive call and writes no Card, so the proposal set is exactly what the run's own source declares.

`prepare` reads the whole term table of the run's book through zt-check's own parser (`zt_check::check::term_table`, the `术语 | 不用 | 意思 | 首次定义` columns; D063), so R6 and the concept review never disagree about a row. It then shorts candidates with the cheap comparisons of §7.1 (character-bigram Dice over titles, questions and statements plus a shared formula), not with a semantic claim: at most ten Topic candidates per proposed concept (D287) and at most ten restatement candidates per proposed statement, deduplicated by unordered pair (D288), with the unselected counts in `coverage`. A Topic's representative Cards are its own numbered entries, at most three. `不用` words are matching evidence only — they are the book's local naming constraint, never a synonym table — and the evidence keeps every matched row beside the candidates so the judge sees the preferred word, the rejected word, the meaning, the book, the definition location, the Topic question and the candidate Cards together (D064).

The frozen evidence binds `schema_version`, `created_at`, `archive {id, path, revision}`, the book, `bounds`, the catalogue counts, `coverage`, `term_table`, one `endpoints` entry per address (title, tree, Workspace, type, authored `fingerprint`, the retained `.md` path and its `sha256`), `unread`, the proposals with their selected candidates, and `evidence_id = "e-" + content_hash(payload, [evidence_id, created_at])` through `canon`. The evidence carries no binary float: a "similarity" is a four-decimal string and the order the shortlist was made in is a `rank`, so another JSON reader's parse cannot land on a neighbouring value and change the id.

All archive reads of `prepare` are one view (D300). The initial `archive_view`, the title export, every Card batch and a closing `archive_view` must report one revision; a different one retries the whole set once and then fails with `incomplete concept evidence: changing read view across archive revisions`, writing nothing. A Card the archive omits from its answer (`missing`/`complete: false`) is an `unread` endpoint, never a silent absence; the step still finishes and names it.

`judge` validates one canned answer file (`canon::read_json`, so a repeated JSON key is refused) against the frozen evidence: `schema_version`, the evidence id, the `judgments` bound, each judgment's allowed fields, its `kind` (`topic-concept`/`restatement`), a proposal and a candidate the evidence prepared, a `verdict` of `same`/`different`/`uncertain`, a reason, a non-empty `evidence` list naming only addresses the review read for that pair, a `scope` for `same`, `needs` (what must be read) for `uncertain`, an `entry` that is one of the candidate's representative Cards, and no verdict over an unread endpoint. A second judgment for the same pair is refused unless it is identical, so a contradiction is an error rather than a silent pick. A refused or absent answer writes nothing and leaves those pairs unjudged: it never becomes a create or merge decision (D302).

`report` renders both files from the frozen evidence and, when present, the accepted answers, and it **starts no `zt mcp`** — the trace test proves a report step makes no tool call, so report generation cannot write a Card. The report holds `report_id = "r-" + content_hash(payload, [report_id, created_at])`, the schema, the archive identity and revision, the book, `bounds`, `coverage` (proposals, pairs, catalogued Topics and Idea Cards, prefiltered, selected and unselected counts), `completeness {complete, judged, unjudged, unread, reasons}`, `term_table`, `endpoints`, the `judged_id` and the accepted judgments, a per-proposal summary (`same`/`different`/`uncertain`/`clears_shortlist`), `unresolved`, `status`, and the `findings`. `completeness.unread` is the frozen evidence's own unread record plus every unread endpoint of a judged pair: a Card the review could not read is named there even when it never became a candidate (its read failed before the shortlist existed), so no report over incomplete reads can claim `complete`. A finding has a report-local id (`c001`, … in kind order), one of the three kinds of D076 (`topic-concept`, `restatement`, `topic-home`), its verdict, reason, `scope`, assumptions, `needs`, `evidence`, affected `addresses`, `formula_match` (a candidate signal, never a verdict), `unjudged`, and the `remedies` the evidence supports. An unjudged pair or an unread endpoint becomes an `uncertain` finding that names what must be read and makes the report `partial`: a shortlist is never presented as an exhaustive semantic comparison (D082). `report` is immutable for one evidence set and one set of answers — a second run publishes the same id and changes no byte, and the Markdown is rendered from the published report, not from a new clock. An `evidence.json` whose content no longer matches its `evidence_id` is refused, so a report can never be built on an edited set.

`decision` re-computes the report's own id (a report that does not match it is refused), then validates the reviewed decision: the exact `report_id`, known `finding` ids, each decision's `verdict` equal to the finding's, one of the remedies that finding offers (`uncertain` findings offer only `defer`), a note, and any `fingerprints` equal to the reviewed endpoint's authored hash. It writes `decision.json` with `decision_id = "d-" + content_hash(...)` and no Card (D303's receipt path is not needed because there is no write).

Public coverage: `concepts.tests.ps1` drives the four steps through `concept-compiler.ps1` on a scratch archive (26 canned judgments over the complete set, the capped shortlist, the two `学习` senses, the shared formula under different assumptions, an unread endpoint via the seam's `ZT_BULK_MISSING`, a hidden Topic root whose read the archive answered as missing although no pair names it, a settled and an unsettled `ZT_DRIFT_REVISION`, an edited evidence file, and the `ZT_TRACE_TOOLS` proof that `report` makes no call), and the module's unit tests cover the answer and decision validation, the report ids, the retained endpoint body's bound, the partial state, and the incomplete read that never reached a candidate. The `propose` step is covered by `concepts::tests::the_proposals_are_the_books_own_term_rows` (one proposal per row, both spellings, a repeated term counted once, the frozen field set `prepare` accepts) and `concepts::tests::no_term_row_proposes_nothing_and_the_set_is_bounded`, plus the S23 harness checks that the digest stage proposes from the run's own term table before the first group writer.

### `join`

The existing-Topic pair review and the report-bound application of one approved join (#109, S22; design §7.3, §11.2, §11.3, §12.3; D155–D159, D269–D274, D301, D305). `topic_join.rs` has two explicit steps. `plan` reads the agent Workspace and changes no Card: its only move calls are `move_card` previews, which answer `applied: false`. `apply` executes only the exact plans a reviewed selection names, one `move_card` at a time. Historical live execution is a separate gate (S29): this step's evidence scope is a scratch archive.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan` | `archive_view`, one `export_titles`, bounded `get_cards` text batches, and one `move_card` preview per enumerated entry chain | `topic-join-review.json`, `topic-join-review.md`, `endpoints\<hash>.md` (one retained body per read Card) | `{ok, step, report_id, status, archive_id, archive_revision, agent_topics, pairs_matched, pairs_selected, pairs_unselected, moves_offered, unread, attempts, written, paths[]}` |
| `apply -File f` | the reviewed report, the selection, `archive_view`, the affected `get_cards` text rows, `get_tree` of the two trees, the fresh `move_card` preview and confirmation, `list_broken_links`, `get_model` | `join-result.json`, `join-result.md` | `{ok, step, replayed, report_id, result_id, status, complete, counts, broken_links, paths[]}` |

`plan` compares every agent Topic pair with the same cheap comparison the concept review uses (character-bigram Dice over the title and the question, and one title inside the other; `-Similar 0.5`), keeps the ten strongest pairs (`-MaxPairs`), and reads both Topics' questions and at most three entries each — the same three-entry bound the concept review reads. For each pair it lists the full meanings and questions, the source mix (the books its entries' `来源` lines name), the proposed keeper, the best joining Card and the concrete alternatives of D155–D158. The keeper comes from evidence: more distinct source books first, then more entries that state a source; a tie is `keeper_reason: unresolved`, the crossing is recommended and no move is offered. The Card count is never the reason (D159). The joining Card is the keeper entry closest in words to the other Topic's question and entries; with no match, the keeper's first entry anchors the join.

The alternatives are `keep_distinct` (D156), `keep_both_with_crossing` (D157) and `move_subtree` (D158). The crossing uses an existing Hub level and invents no type (§8.1): the keeper tree's topic hub under `邻近` when it has one, otherwise the keeper Topic Card's `## 入口` section, otherwise a field hub under the register; the hub stage owns that write, this review writes no Card.

A move plan is one entry chain: each entry with its Direct successors and Side subtrees, enumerated from the first entry up to `-MaxOperations`, plus the first entry alone. A Topic root is never an operation. Each plan is one `move_card` preview against today's archive, so the review carries the actual plan: the moved Cards, every mapped address (the Direct-successor continuation included), the Link rewrite count, and the finished-model effect. The destination is the next free Side successor of the joining Card. The finished-model effect compares each moving Model member's home Model Card identity before and after: a whole model that moves with its parts is preserved, and a Model part that would leave its model (or a Model Card that would leave a part behind) is a change. A changed model, a plan over the 120-Card bound, a Zone or Workspace boundary, or a human Link rewrite is a refused plan with a code and its nonmoving alternative — the refusal is policy, never worked around (D305).

The review is immutable for one archive revision: `report_id = "tj-" + content_hash(payload, [report_id, created_at])`, and a second run over the same archive and bounds publishes the same id and changes no file. Its `status` is `partial` when any read was missed, and the unread address is named in `unread` and in the pair's `reasons`, so a shortlist is never presented as a clean comparison. Bounds, the matched and unselected pair counts and the effective constants are in the report, and an edited report or one over another archive is refused.

`apply` first validates the selection (§11.3): schema 1, the exact `report_id` recomputed from the report, one to twenty decisions, each naming a known finding, an action the finding offers, a nonempty note, and for `move_subtree` an offered plan of the finding (a refused plan may not be selected) with no Topic-root operation; any `fingerprints` must equal the reviewed endpoint's authored fingerprint. The service's archive id must be the reviewed one (D272), so a report from another archive is refused.

Then the step applies one selected plan at a time (D271). Before each move it re-reads the moving Cards and compares their authored fingerprints with the review (`endpoint_changed`); re-derives the destination and requires it to still be the next free slot (`destination_taken`); fetches the fresh `move_card` plan and compares its mapping and Link rewrite count with the reviewed plan (`plan_changed` — the confirmation word replans, so no immutable plan id is promised, D269); checks the finished-model boundary on that fresh plan (`model_boundary`); and reads the archive revision again immediately before confirming. A revision change in that interval leaves the item `pending` with `quiet_window_required` and stops every further automated move: a quiet migration window is requested, never weakened (D270). The confirmation's returned mapping must equal the reviewed one, and only after a read-back of the persisted addresses, authored fingerprints, Broken-link count and the affected `get_model` reports does the item become `applied-and-verified`; a confirmation that does not answer is `pending`, and a lost or resumed run detects an already-applied plan by readback (`already_applied`) instead of moving twice. Every applied mapping refreshes the pending operations' addresses, so a later finding moves a Card from where the earlier one put it. The result keeps each item's status (`applied-and-verified`, `unchanged`, `conflict`, `refused`, `pending`) with its reason and refreshed-versus-reviewed addresses, the applied mappings and their inverse as rollback evidence, the pending operations, and the before/after revision and Broken-link count. The same selection replays its recorded result and writes nothing.

Public outcomes: exit 0 for an answered step (`plan`, or an `apply` whose per-item outcomes are any of the five statuses), and exit 1 for a refused input (a missing or edited report, a selection that binds another report or an unsupported action, an archive mismatch).

Operator guidance. A review is evidence, not an instruction: the recommendation is a starting point an owner or an operator reviews, and no move runs until a selection names it. The selection is an ordinary file the caller writes beside the report:

```json
{"schema_version":1,"report_id":"tj-…","decisions":[
  {"finding":"t002","action":"keep_both_with_crossing","note":"三条线各有价值，保留并以主题入口相连。"},
  {"finding":"t003","action":"move_subtree","plan":"t003/p001","note":"把这条孤立的入口链并入已有的多重均衡主题。",
   "fingerprints":{"108/1":"<the reviewed authored fingerprint>"}}
]}
```

A `keep_distinct` or `keep_both_with_crossing` decision writes no Card: it records the chosen nonmoving alternative in the result. A `move_subtree` decision names one offered plan; the fingerprints are optional and make the reviewed authored base explicit. Run `apply` once per batch, read the result's per-item statuses, and regenerate the review after a `conflict` or a `refused` plan before selecting it again — an old report endpoint is never applied as stale text (D301). The bounds (`-MaxPairs`, `-MaxOperations`, the entry and plan-Card bounds) are the report's own record of what it did not compare.

Public coverage: `topic-join.tests.ps1` (79 assertions) drives both steps on a scratch archive with five near-duplicate Topic pairs, a Model Card with its part, an existing topic hub, an agent Card that links the moving chain, a human-linked Topic, a human Topic created through the GUI and a Table Spread. It checks the review's identity and immutability, that `plan` makes no write call and leaves the revision unchanged while it previews real move plans, the keeper evidence with a wider source mix, the crossing's existing hub, the full questions and meanings, every moved Card and mapped address (the Direct-successor continuation included), that no Topic root is an operation, each entry chain as its own plan, the Link rewrite count and the preserved model, the refused human-Link plan beside its nonmoving alternative, the refused `model_boundary` lone move beside the offered whole-model move, the bounds and the unselected pair count, an unread endpoint making the review partial, and the apply-side refusals (a refused plan, an unknown finding, an edited report). Then it applies: a keep-distinct decision that writes nothing, a destination another writer took (`destination_taken`), an edited endpoint (`endpoint_changed`), a new Card inside the moving subtree (`plan_changed`), a writer inside the migration interval (`quiet_window_required`, both selected moves pending with refreshed pending addresses), two sequential joins whose second move uses the refreshed source address, the stale first review refused after another move, a regenerated review applying the A/B join with its persisted addresses, titles, Model part, model report, rewritten agent Link with its unchanged Relation kind and the followed Spread, the same selection replaying its result byte for byte, and rollback evidence for every applied mapping. The module's unit tests cover the Location rules, the pair scan and its bound, the keeper and joining choices, the crossing levels, the plan-line parser, the model effect, the address refresh and fingerprint lookup, the selection validation, the worst-status order and the rendering.

### `topic-home`

The report-bound Topic-home transfer (#113, S21; design §11.1, §11.3, §12.3; D262–D274, D301, D305). `topic_home.rs` has two explicit steps. The current `move` operation refuses the Topic-to-Literature Zone boundary and that rule is untouched: this path never moves a Card. It creates one plain Literature statement and rewrites the old Card at its own address, so an existing inbound Link keeps reaching it and no address breaks.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan` | the reviewed concept report, `archive_view`, one `export_titles`, bounded `get_cards` text batches for the Topic's entries and their own-book source targets, and one bounded `get_cards` batch per target Literature tree for the earlier-carrier scan | `topic-home-review.json`, `topic-home-review.md`, `endpoints\<hash>.md` (one retained body per read Card) | `{ok, step, report_id, concept_report_id, status, archive_id, archive_revision, findings, items, offered, refused, blocked, pending_findings, unread, written, paths[]}` |
| `apply -File f` | the reviewed report, the selection, `archive_view`, the affected `get_cards` text rows, `get_card` for the anchor and the inbound count, `get_model` for the named models, two identified `apply_batch` writes, `list_broken_links` | `topic-home-result.json`, `topic-home-result.md`, `endpoints\…` (the retained pre-edit body) | `{ok, step, replayed, report_id, result_id, status, complete, counts, broken_links, paths[]}` |

`plan` consumes a concept review (`-Report`, default `<Work>\concept-review.json`) and verifies its own content id, so a file that no longer matches is stale text and never this review's parent. It takes that report's `topic-home` findings and, for each, the Topic's Direct-successor entries (up to the bound) — the same Cards the report's filing evidence rests on. A judged finding whose entries still rest only on their own book yields one transfer item per entry, and every item binds the concept report id and finding id, the archive identity, the entry's authored fingerprint and exact statement, the target Literature anchor with its authored fingerprint, the predicted Side slot, the model context it must not change and the treatment of an earlier carrier (D272).

Which entry may be transferred is policy, decided before any text is proposed (D263–D267). An entry that is not an agent Regular Card, is already a restatement entry, names no source Link inside its own book or names another book's Card, or states no statement, is `refused` with a code and its nonmoving alternative. A Model Card or Model part is `blocked` on a named separate model design decision, because a transfer may not create a Model part, take a part into a model or change a finished model; a plain Literature Idea Card beside the model is what this path offers instead.

The target anchor is a plain agent Idea Card of the book's Literature tree: the entry's own source Link when it names one, else the book's first plain anchor. A tree root takes no Side successor, so the Literature root itself is never the parent, and a book without a plain anchor is refused beside the digest's own anchors. The new statement is one `create_successor` with `type: idea` beside the anchor — the exact reviewed title and body, source lines included — so the archive's own `statement_fingerprint` proves the claim survived and every reviewed formula and source Link is checked verbatim (D265).

The old Card's edit is an explicit entry by default: §4.5's `## 同述入口` shape, one Link naming the terminal statement and a source line, so the navigation entry is not a second copy (D264, D266). The authored alternative — a distinct interpretation — is offered too, and the selection must supply that exact text; the review names what a draft has to satisfy (a sentence that links the statement, the source line, a different statement).

An earlier carrier is treated explicitly (D272): when an Idea Card of the target tree already carries the exact statement fingerprint, the item is `reuse` — no second copy is created and the old Card becomes that statement's entry. The carrier scan is bounded, and a bounded scan marks the review `partial`, so "no carrier" is never claimed from an unfinished read.

The review is immutable for one archive revision and one concept report: `report_id = "th-" + content_hash(payload, [report_id, created_at])`, and a second run over the same evidence publishes the same id and changes no file. A Card that could not be read is named in `unread`; an unreviewed finding is `pending` with its reason. An edited report, one over another archive, and a selection that binds another report are refused.

`apply` first validates the selection (§11.3): schema 1, the exact recomputed `report_id`, one to fifty decisions, each naming a known item the review offered (a `refused` or `blocked` item may not be selected), one of the two remedies, a nonempty note, and for the distinct remedy the exact `{title, body}` draft. `-OperationPrefix` is required and becomes each write's receipt key — `…/create-t<id>` and `…/rewrite-t<id>` — so a retry replays the same result instead of writing twice.

Each item is then two identified writes, deliberately not one atomic move (D268). The Literature statement is created first — a retry replays its receipt, and a lost reply is read back from the anchor rather than repeated — and it is verified by read-back: the statement fingerprint, every reviewed source Link and formula, the Broken-link count and one substantive home. Only then is the old Card edited, with the reviewed authored fingerprint as its base guard (D273). A changed old Card is a conflict, not permission to overwrite it: the created statement is preserved, the edit is not applied, and the result carries a `revised_review_item` naming the observed fingerprint. A changed endpoint, a stale finding, another writer's Card beside the anchor, a slot this run itself spent, an archive change between two reads (`quiet_window_required`) and a lost reply are reported distinctly, and the item ends `applied-and-verified`, `unchanged` (a resumed run whose writes are already there), `conflict`, `refused` or `pending` (D274).

After the writes the step reads back the persisted old Card, its Links and their actual Relation kinds, the inbound Link count at the old address, the Broken-link count, the one substantive home and the `get_model` reports of the models the statement names — a changed membership is a verification fault, not a note. No item is marked applied because a tool returned without an error. The result keeps each item's reason, the rollback evidence for both writes (the created address; the old Card's exact previous title and body with its retained endpoint file), the applied mappings and the before/after revision and Broken-link count. The same selection replays its recorded result and writes nothing.

Public outcomes: exit 0 for an answered step (`plan`, or an `apply` whose per-item outcomes are any of the five statuses), and exit 1 for a refused input (a missing or edited report, a selection that binds another report or a blocked item, a missing `-OperationPrefix`, an archive mismatch).

Operator guidance. Nothing moves on real historical Cards by this slice: the review is evidence for a separately authorized selection (S29). A selection is an ordinary file beside the review:

```json
{"schema_version":1,"report_id":"th-…","decisions":[
  {"finding":"c003","entry":"12/1","remedy":"entry","note":"把本书的陈述转成文献陈述，旧卡改成入口。"},
  {"finding":"c003","entry":"12/2","remedy":"distinct","note":"这个主题自己的解释与本书不同。",
   "draft":{"title":"…","body":"…\n\n来源：[[book2020]] §2"}}
]}
```

Run `apply` once, read the per-item statuses, and regenerate the review after a `conflict` before selecting the item again — an old report endpoint is never applied as stale text (D301). A `pending` item is retried with the same selection and the same prefix: the creation receipt is replayed, never repeated.

Public coverage: `topic-home.tests.ps1` (77 assertions) drives a real concept review (`concepts` prepare/judge/report) and both steps on a scratch archive with two books, a Literature tree with five anchors, an existing statement (the earlier carrier), a Model Card with its part, and a Topic whose nine entries cover every item outcome. It checks that the review binds its own id, the concept report and finding ids, the archive revision and the reviewed entries; that `plan` makes no write call and changes no revision; the offered item's anchor, predicted Side slot, source Links, formula, model context and exact entry text; the blocked model item, the refused items, and the reuse of the existing statement; the rendered review; and the immutability of the same evidence. Then it applies: the created statement's fingerprint, source Links and formula; the old Card as an entry that is not a second copy; the unchanged inbound Link count and the untouched human Card; one substantive home; unchanged model reports; rollback evidence for both writes; the byte-identical replay; an authored distinct interpretation whose Link kind is read back; the reuse item that creates no Card; a lost creation reply that leaves one statement and resumes through the receipt; a writer between the two writes that preserves the created statement and produces a revised review item; a change during the item's own reads that stays `pending` with a quiet window; a stale finding, a taken destination, and an edited report refused. The module's unit tests cover the Location rules, the book-bound evidence, the statement text and formulas, the entry remedy's shape, the anchor and model context, the bounded carrier scan, the selection validation and the review id.

### `grow`

The idea-grow compiler (S15; design `docs/idea-grow-design.md`; the workflow itself is the [idea-grow-workflow](idea-grow-workflow.md) block). `grow.rs` runs one cycle: `plan` reads the box and writes the section and job files, `precheck` reports G1–G13 and zt-check for one answer, `topics` makes the run's Topics, `apply` writes the Topic Cards, `check` turns zt-check faults into fix jobs, `report` writes `grow-report.json` and `grow-report.md`. It writes agent Topic trees only: never a Literature-tree Card, a move, a delete, or a human Card. `grow` is an open-scope tool (`OPEN_TOOLS`): the digests build only Literature trees, and this tool (idea-grow 3.0.0) owns every Topic tree, Topic Card, hub and the register. The hubs round (`hubs-plan`, `hubs-apply`, `hubs-check`, `hubs-report`) also runs alone (the workflow's mode hubs) and takes the hub options the digests had, and the run's new Topic Cards get the restatement review (`zt-digest restate`, fed by `restate-cards`). Since 0.11.0 (idea-grow 3.1.0) it also stages the concept review of each source book's term table (`concept-books`, which `zt-digest concepts` then reviews and `hubs-report -Concepts` hands to the register plan) and runs the Topic maintenance round (`organize-plan`, the `go-NNN` precheck, `organize-apply`): the only `grow` steps that move agent Topic Cards and delete an emptied agent Topic root, and only when the workflow asks for the round.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan [-Book\|-Books] [-From/-To] [-Tool/-ZtConfig]` | `get_tree` of each book, `list_trees`, one `export_titles` | `snapshot.md`, `titles.json` (each Card with its `lock`), `topics.md`, `sections\sNNN.md`, `read-only.allow.txt`, `baseline.json`, `instructions.zh.md`, `plan.json`, `jobs\gt-001.md` | `{topic_jobs, pending, left, books, sections, cards, box, topics, locked, baseline}` |
| `precheck -Job <id>` | nothing (offline; zt-check on the drafts) | `drafts-<job>.json` | one line per fault, then `faults: N` |
| `topics -Apply` | the Topic gate's `create_topic`, a reused Topic's fresh `get_cards` Lock | `state.json` (`run_topics`), `plan.json`, `jobs\gw-NNN.md` | `{jobs, topics, created, refused}` |
| `apply [-Apply]` | `get_card` for the parent and base guards, the gate, one write per Card | `state.json` (`sections`, `cards`, `fixes`, `links`, and a `stopped` fact) | `{applied, created, topics, same, refused, duplicates, edited, unanswered, linked, stopped}`; a Lock adds the typed refusal as the last line |
| `check -Round R` | zt-check `--cards` on the run's Cards | `state.json`, `jobs\fxR-NNN.md` | `{fix, round, faults, cards}` |
| `report` | `get_tree` of each book, zt-check and `get_cards` on the run's Cards | `grow-report.json`, `grow-report.md` | `{created, topics, linked, same, refused, duplicates, stopped, deeper, faults_left, share_before, share_after, books, bridges, report}` |
| `hubs-plan [-Limits <json>] [-Trees <list>] [-Rebuild]` | the hub survey and plan (`zt-digest hub` as a child process), the box's field hubs (`seed_fields`) | `hubs\` (the hub state, survey and plan), `state.json` (`hubs`: jobs, limits, survey counts), `jobs\gh-NNN.md` (one grow job per hub job) | `{hub_jobs, trees, topics, fields_kept, limits}` |
| `restate-cards` | nothing (reads `state.json`) | `restate\rNN\manifest.json` (the source book's Citation key, `source: zt-idea-grow`) and `restate\rNN\cards.txt` (the run's new Topic Cards from that book) | `{books, reviews: [{id, book, staging, cards}]}` |
| `concept-books [-Book\|-Books]` | one `export_titles` (agent), `get_cards` of each term-table Card | removes `concepts\`, then `concepts\cNN\manifest.json` (`citation_key`, `book_title`, `source: zt-idea-grow`) and `concepts\cNN\terms.md` (the term-table Card's body); `state.json` (`concepts`) | `{books, reviews: [{id, book, staging, table}], skipped: [{book, reason}]}` |
| `hubs-report [-Register apply\|plan\|off] [-Concepts]` | the hub report, the register plan (and with `apply` its publication and check) as child processes | `register\`, `state.json` (`hubs.report`) | `{hubs, faults, missing, mirror, register: {status, plan_id, entries, unresolved, faults, before, reviews, missing}}` |
| `organize-plan [-Trees <list>]` | `organize-survey -Topics` as a child process | `organize\survey.md`, `organize\survey.json`, `jobs\go-001.md` (when the survey lists a candidate), `state.json` (`organize`); removes a stale `decisions.json`, `applied.json` and `out\go-001.json` | `{organize_jobs, topics, pairs, small, moves, locked}` |
| `precheck -Job go-NNN` | nothing (offline: `out\go-NNN.json` against `organize\survey.json`) | — | one line per fault (O1–O8), then `faults: N` |
| `organize-apply` | `organize-apply -Work <Work>\organize -Open` as a child process | `organize\decisions.json` (the decisions that passed the check), `organize\applied.json` (the tool's), `state.json` (renamed Card addresses, `organize.applied`) | `{applied, merges, moves, refused, blocked, renamed, complete, invalid, run_cards_renamed}`; a Lock stop prints the typed refusal and exits 2 |
