**The concept review's input (0.11.0).** `concept-books` picks the books (`concept_books_of`): the named ones, else the books the run grew Cards from (`grown_by_book`), else every agent book with a term table, at most `MAX_CONCEPT_BOOKS` (24); a book whose agent Literature tree has no term-table Card is skipped with its reason. A book's term table is the first Card by address whose title starts with `术语表` (`term_tables`), the one `concepts -Step prepare` reads too. `hubs-report -Concepts` names every `concept-review.json` the run rendered (`concept_reports`) to `register -Step plan` as `-Review a.json;b.json`; without `-Concepts` the plan reads no review, so a stale report of an earlier run is never evidence.

**Topic maintenance (0.11.0).** `organize_topics` names the surveyed Topics: `-Trees`, else the trees of the run's Cards and the run's Topics. `validate_organize` checks a go answer against the survey: O1 shape, bounds (`MAX_MERGES` 10, `MAX_MOVES` 30) and a reason for each decision; O2 a Card the survey does not list; O3 `drop`/`keep` not two agent Topic roots, neither surveyed, or a moved Card outside the surveyed Topics; O4 a Card that never moves (a root, a Hub, a Model Card or part); O5 a Locked Topic on either side; O6 a merge's `under` outside the kept Topic, or a move's `under` inside the moved Card; O7 `notes`; O8 a Topic dropped twice or both dropped and kept. `organize-apply` drops each faulty decision (`invalid`), runs the rest, and renames the run's own addresses through the tool's `moved` map (`renamed`, longest old address first; `rename_state`: the Card keys and each section's created Cards and their parents), also after a Lock stop, since `applied.json` keeps the moves already made.

**Hub options (3.0.0).** `hubs-plan -Limits <json>` overrides the survey's limits key by key (`merge_limits`): each named key replaces the survey's value, the hub compiler clamps every value again, and an unknown or non-numeric key is refused (`hub limit `<k>` is not one of …`, `hub limit `<k>` must be a number`). `-Trees <list>` plans only the named Topic trees (a pilot), and `-Rebuild` rewrites every hub; both are passed through to `hub -Step plan`.

**The restatement review's input (3.0.0).** `restate-cards` groups the run's new Topic Cards of `state.json` by their source book (`grown_by_book`): books and Cards sorted, at most 200 Cards a book (the review's bound), a Card with no book left out. Each book gets one staging folder `<Work>\restate\rNN` (`r01` …), which one `zt-digest restate` chain then reviews against that book's anchors and models and the earlier Topic Idea Cards.

**Locked destinations (S15, R330/R335).** A Lock on the source book never stops a run: a Locked Literature tree is read like any other. A Locked Topic tree is related context and a Link target only. The plan keeps each Card's Lock facts in `titles.json`, prints `locked` (the number of Locked Topic trees), marks the tree in `topics.md` (`已锁定：只读，可链接，不能写入`), and lists its Cards apart in the section file (`## 用词最接近的已锁定主题卡（只读：可作相关语境，可链接，不能写入）`). The precheck and `topics` report G12 when an answer names a Locked destination — `at`, `same`, an `existing` Topic, or a new Topic whose name is near a Locked one — so the answer must name another destination: the tool never substitutes a tree or a placement. The storage gate stays authoritative: a Topic locked after its answers stops the `apply` stage, which prints the totals line, then the archive's own refusal as `{ok:false, code:"card_locked", error, message, reason, tree, lock_id, locks, mode:"grow", step, at, hint}`, the sentence on stderr and exit 2. The stopped job keeps its answers and the receipts of the Cards it already made, `grow-report.md` lists it under `## 已锁定而停下的写入`, and the same `out` resumes after the owner unlocks. A gate that reuses a Topic reads that Topic's fresh Lock before filing under it.

**Lines beside the doorway (0.9.1).** A section opens at most one line per Topic: `validate` reports G13 for a second idea of one answer at the same Topic root (`n/0` or `T1`), with a message that names the first idea to chain from. In `apply`, a root placement whose doorway already has `MAX_LINES` (7) Side successors goes beside the doorway's last line instead (`line_parent`, which descends again while that line is full too); its receipt in `state.json` carries `full` (the doorway), and `report` counts these Cards as `deeper` and lists them under `## 入口卡已满`.

Public coverage: `grow.tests.ps1` (the concept-review and Topic-maintenance phases added in 0.11.0), the 26 `grow` unit tests of `cargo test --bin zt-digest grow` (among them `the_hub_limit_override_replaces_only_the_keys_it_names`, `the_grown_cards_are_grouped_by_book_within_the_review_bound`, `each_agent_book_has_its_first_term_table_card`, `the_reviewed_books_are_the_named_then_the_grown_then_every_book_with_a_table`, `the_surveyed_topics_are_the_named_or_the_run_s_own`, `a_good_maintenance_answer_has_no_fault`, `the_maintenance_checks_name_each_fault` and `the_run_s_addresses_follow_the_maintenance_moves`), and the design doc's G1–G13 table.

### `resolve`

The separately selected historical resolution batch (#122, S29; design §15-phase-6; D377, D378; S29-AC01–AC07). There is no automatic archive-wide migration. The reports the two qualified paths already produced for one archive — `topic-home`'s transfer review and `join`'s Topic-pair review — name the old restatements, misfiled Cards and Topic pairs a person may act on; this gate turns the exact items a person selected out of them into one batch, executes only those items through those same qualified paths, and verifies the batch afterwards. `resolve.rs` has three steps and changes no Card until `apply`.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp` per report at apply) | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan [-File <batch-selection.json>]` | `topic-home-review.json` and `topic-join-review.json` when the work folder holds them (each against its own content id and this archive's identity), `archive_view`, both `export_titles`, one bounded `get_cards` read of every reviewed address, `list_broken_links`, the verified backup | `resolution-batch.json` (`rb-` content id), `resolution-batch.md`, `resolve-endpoints\<hash>.md` (one retained body per read Card) | `{ok, step, batch_id, status, deferred, archive_id, archive_revision, reports, counts, backup, paths[]}` |
| `apply` | the batch (its own id recomputed), the approved backup, `archive_view`, the protected scope, the two reports again, the selected items' Cards, `list_broken_links` | `resolution-batch-result.json`, `resolution-batch-result.md`; the Cards the selected items identify | `{ok, step, batch_id, result_id, status, complete, done, counts, protected, broken_links, unresolved, paths[]}` |
| `check [-File <result>]` | the batch, the result, `archive_view`, the applied addresses, `get_model`, the protected scope, `list_broken_links` | nothing | `{ok, step, batch_id, result_id, status, complete, done, items[], faults, protected, broken_links, archive, claim, path}`; exit 1 when a fault is found |

S29-AC01 is the batch. Every offered report item becomes one candidate bound to its report id, finding id and item or plan id, the exact texts or operations the qualified path would write (the statement's title, body, fingerprint, source Links and formulas; the old Card's exact entry text; a move's operations, mapping, Link-rewrite count and model effect), the **current** address and authored fingerprint of every Card it touches beside the reviewed one (`addresses[]`; `stale` names every address that moved), the archive identity and revision the plan read, and the explicit treatment of an earlier carrier (`create` or `reuse` of the named Card). The bodies it read are retained under `resolve-endpoints\`. A selected batch also records the approved backup — path, `sha256`, bytes and the verification that it is this archive's own readable SQLite backup holding Cards — or a batch that selects nothing approves none.

S29-AC02 is the selection. `-File` names the batch selection: schema 1, a nonempty note, the verified `backup`, and the `topic_home` and `join` blocks in **exactly** the shapes those two steps' own selections take, so each block is decided by that step's own validator and a batch can never accept something `topic-home` or `join` would refuse. An item the report itself did not offer (a blocked Model Card, a refused plan) is listed as `ineligible` with its reason and can never be selected. Silence is never permission: with no selection file the batch is `deferred` (`no-selection`) and with a selection that accepts nothing it is `deferred` (`declined`); in both cases every eligible item keeps the visible disposition `deferred` with `awaiting-selection` or `not-selected`. `apply` refuses a deferred batch outright and `check` reports the deferral instead of claiming a correction.

S29-AC03 and S29-AC04 are the execution. `apply` refuses a batch that selected nothing, re-verifies the approved backup (the recorded bytes and the archive identity) and the protected scope (every human Card and every agent Model Card) and then executes the accepted items one at a time through the qualified paths: `topic_home::run_selected` for the nonmoving retained-entry transfers **first**, because they change no address, and `topic_join::run_selected` for the reviewed `move_card` joins after them, so no selected move invalidates an address a later item reads and every mapping a move returns is current for the items after it. The transfer's receipt prefix is derived from the batch id (`resolve/<batch>/1/transfer`), so a retry of the same batch replays its own receipts instead of writing twice. A stale base, a changed reviewed endpoint, a taken destination, a changed earlier carrier and a protected Card that moved since the selection are the qualified paths' own conflict or pending outcomes, named per item; the gate weakens none of them.

S29-AC05 is the verification, in two layers that the result keeps apart. The qualified path verifies its own item (persisted text, source Links, formulas, target validity, actual Relation kinds, Broken links, one substantive home, model reports) and that evidence is retained in the item's `evidence`. `check` then re-reads the archive: each applied item's addresses, its statement fingerprint, the old Card's `## 同述入口` entry and the Link it carries, every moved Card at its mapped address with the reviewed authored text, the model reports the statement names, the protected scope digest and the Broken-link count. A fault is named and nothing is repaired.

S29-AC06 is the outcome. Each item ends `applied-and-verified`, `unchanged`, `conflict`, `refused` or `pending`; the batch's `status` is the worst of them, `complete` is true only when every item is applied or unchanged, and `done` is true when every item is either verified or explicitly unresolved with a named reason and the protected scope did not change — D378's finish condition. `unresolved` lists each item that is not verified with its reason, and the result's `scope` says plainly that no other historical fault was rewritten. `rollback` keeps the transfers' created address and the old Cards' previous title and body, and the moves' and their inverse mappings; it is evidence, not an undo command.

S29-AC07 is the boundary this tool keeps: it names only the reports and items a caller selected, writes only those Cards, closes and modifies no issue, and retains the whole record for a later decision.

Public outcomes: exit 0 for an answered step (`plan`, an `apply` whose per-item outcomes are any of the five statuses, or a `check` that finds no fault), and exit 1 for a refused input (a missing or edited batch, a deferred batch at `apply`, a selection naming an unknown field or an item the report did not offer, a selected batch without the verified backup, a backup that is not there or is no longer the recorded bytes, a missing result at `check`) or a `check` that finds a fault.

Operator guidance. Nothing is repaired by running the gate: two steps come first, and both are read-only.

```powershell
# 1. The reports already produced for this run (each writes no Card).
zt-digest topic-home -Step plan -Work <work> -Report <concept-review.json>
zt-digest join       -Step plan -Work <work>

# 2. The batch, deferred until a person selects items out of those reports.
zt-digest resolve -Step plan -Work <work>

# 3. A selection: the accepted items, the note, and the verified backup of this archive.
# 4. The selected batch, then its current readback.
zt-digest resolve -Step plan  -Work <work> -File <selection.json>
zt-digest resolve -Step apply -Work <work>
zt-digest resolve -Step check -Work <work>
```

```json
{"schema_version":1,"note":"只处理这两条。",
 "backup":{"path":"D:\\zt\\backup\\zt.sqlite3.20261020.bak","sha256":"…","bytes":33366016},
 "topic_home":{"schema_version":1,"report_id":"th-…","decisions":[
   {"finding":"c003","entry":"12/1","remedy":"entry","note":"把本书的陈述转成文献陈述，旧卡改成入口。"}]},
 "join":{"schema_version":1,"report_id":"tj-…","decisions":[
   {"finding":"t003","action":"move_subtree","plan":"t003/p001","note":"把重复主题的入口链并入多重主题。"}]}}
```

A `keep_distinct` or `keep_both_with_crossing` decision writes no Card and resolves to `unchanged`. A `conflict`, `refused` or `pending` item is unresolved, not fixed: regenerate the report that named it (D301) or restore the reviewed state, plan the batch again, and select it again. No batch is implied by a report existing, by this tool being installed, or by an earlier issue's authorization.

Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/resolve.tests.ps1` (93 assertions) drives both qualified reports into one batch on a scratch archive: `plan` with no selection (deferred, every candidate, both reports, the current fingerprint of each address beside the reviewed one, the free statement slot, the earlier-carrier treatment, no write call, unchanged revision, retained bodies, rendered view), the refused `apply` on that deferred batch, a declining selection (`declined` plus the visible deferral at `check`), the selection refusals (an unknown field, an item the report did not offer, no backup, a backup that is not there), the approved batch of one transfer and one move (both applied and verified, the created statement's fingerprint, source Link and formula, the old Card as an entry that is not a second copy, its unchanged inbound count, every moved Card at its mapped address with the source addresses vacated, the untouched human Card, unchanged model reports, no new Broken link, the rollback evidence of both writes and of the mapping), `check` passing and then failing on a changed entry and passing again after the restore, the same batch applied again (both items `unchanged`, no second statement, the mapping it had already applied), a stale base that leaves the item `conflict` with the other writer's text preserved, a changed protected Model Card that leaves every item `pending` with a quiet window and no write, and an edited batch refused by its own id. The module's unit tests cover the batch id, both candidate builders with their ineligible items and the reviewed fingerprints of the moving Cards, the landing slot as the only free address, the disposition of every item with no selection, a declining selection and a selection that accepts exactly what it names, the refusal of an unknown field, a missing note, a wrong schema and an item the report did not offer, the staleness of a changed address, the verified backup rules, and the counts and scope of the batch document.

### `pilot`

The one-book pilot gate (#117, S26; design §15-phase-2, D369/D370). `pilot.rs` has three read-only steps over one prepared run: they write only under `-Work`, and they never write a Card, a register, a hub or an archive row. The gate is the pre-launch and post-run seam that S26's human decision rests on; the run itself is launched by the authorized operator, never by this tool.

| Step | Reads | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan -File <authorization.json>` | the prepared contract, one `archive_view` and one complete Card read (`export_titles` + bounded `get_cards` text batches), the installed frozen registry `.claude/workflow-framework/zt-<variant>-digest/lock.json`, the named binary files, the backup, the baseline report and its addresses, the preserved builds | `pilot-plan.json`, `pilot-plan.md`, `pilot-protected.json` | `{contract, step, plan_id, created, decision, identity, binaries, approved_scope, backup, baseline, preserved_builds, comparison, protected, fresh, checks, missing}` |
| `preflight` | the plan, the prepared ledger (`meta`, `units`, `deps`, `events`), one complete Card read, the backup, the preserved builds, the installed script | `pilot-preflight.json` | `{contract, step, plan_id, created, ok, problems, archive, run, link, checks}`; exit 1 when `ok` is false |
| `qualify [-RunReport f] [-Audit f] [-Cards f] [-Sample n]` | the plan and its protected scope, the run's verified `run-report.json`, one complete Card read, the staging folder's `owned-g*.txt`, `link-completion-gNNN.json` and group job starts, the retained Card-cycle report, the audit's `baseline` section, the ledger's events | `pilot-qualification.json`, `pilot-qualification.md` | `{contract, step, plan_id, created, decision, run, scope, cards, measures, interruption, old_data, review, archive, checks, verdict}` |

S26-AC01 is the plan's content, and a plan is refused (nothing written) when any named fact is missing or does not verify: the source and its hash, the Citation key and book title, the staging and the frozen contract versions, every group and its file hash, the archive identity and revision, all three installed binaries with their SHA-256 and own version (the named `zt-digest` must be this process), the installed frozen Workflow's version and script hash, the caps (`maxAttempts`, `dshRunCap` equal to the prepared settings), the baseline report and its `audit-addresses.txt`, the verified backup with its `integrity: ok`, the preserved builds, the earlier run kept for comparison, the approved new-Card statement, and an empty old-Card list. The protected scope is every old Card's authored fingerprint (`pilot-protected.json`); the prepared Literature root and group anchors are this run's own placeholders and are named in `approved_scope.prepared_cards` instead. `plan_id` is `p-` and the first 16 hex digits of a hash over the contract, the preparation hash, the archive id, the old scope's digest and the approved statement, so an edited plan is a different plan.

S26-AC02 is the preflight: it refuses a spent or moved run (`invocation` other than 1, `resume_count` other than 0, any content unit outside `registered`/`ready`, an `owned-g*.txt` already present), a live foreign claim (`claimed` with an unexpired lease), a registered `link/gNNN@1` unit without its group's `digest/gNNN@R` **success** prerequisite, a `link-check/book@1` that covers fewer groups than the run prepared, a changed binary or frozen script, a changed backup or preserved build, another archive, an archive whose revision moved since the plan, an edited plan, and a changed protected scope. It writes its record with `ok: false` and the named problems before exiting 1, so a refused launch keeps its evidence.

S26-AC03–S26-AC07 is the qualification, and it reads only what the run itself retained: the verified report's identity, counts, four completeness flags, gaps, usage and timing; every Card the run's scope now holds, with its owning group, parent, earlier-filing flag (the parent existed before the launch) and recorded review outcome; the group's own Link completion and its L1/L2 quality; the audit's `baseline` measures (P1 own-source Topic dependence with `unknown_source` counted apart and `under_target` against the 30 target, P2 candidates/judged/resolved/pending, P3_P5 cross-book filing, P4 appended tails and the exact per-run usage) and the pilot book's Card-language/layer/orphan counts; the interruption facts from the ledger's append-only events and the recovered group starts; and the old-data proposals (the run report's existing-Card/Bridge/register/hub gaps and every changed old Card) with `applied: []`. A missing audit is stated as an unknown measure with its reason, never zero. Its own checks (`scope.no_old_change`, `cards.reviewed`, `cards.owned`, `link.ownership`, `groups.done`, `measures.known`) set `verdict` to `evidence_complete` or `partial`, and `decision` is always `pending-human` with a deterministic representative sample for the human review (S26-AC08).

Public outcomes: exit 0 for a written plan, a passing preflight or a qualification; exit 1 for a refused plan, a failed preflight or a refused qualification (an edited plan, a missing protected scope, a missing run report, an audit that cannot be read). The runbook is [`docs/luhmann-comparison-pilot.md`](../../docs/luhmann-comparison-pilot.md).

Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/pilot.tests.ps1` (53 assertions) drives the three steps on a scratch prepared run: the plan's refusals and its record (binaries, frozen version and script hash, caps, launch line, backup, baseline, protected scope), preflight passing then refusing a moved run, an archive that moved and a claimed unit, qualify refusing without the run report, then reading the run's own group completion, earlier filing, review gap, Link-completion gap, unknown baseline and unexercised interruption, a retained Card-cycle review and Link completion per Card and group, a changed protected Card named as one old-data proposal with `scope.no_old_change` failing, and the pending decision in every run. The module's unit tests cover the plan id, the protected digest and diff, the protected view, the launch/fresh/lease predicates, the Link prerequisite, the review sample and the refusal message.

### `publish`

The review and live publication gate for changes to existing Cards (#119, S27; design §8.5, §11.3, §12.3, §15-phase-3, D371/D372). `publish.rs` presents the exact changes an accepted register plan and an accepted hub plan propose to Cards that already exist, records the reviewer's decision on each item, and publishes only the accepted ones through the same identified, receipt-bound operations the qualified compilers use. It exists because a register rebuild and an old hub's rewrite are changes to existing Cards and therefore stay review-first (D191, S12-AC08, S26-AC06): a new-book scope authorizes a book's own new Cards and never these.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Work>`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan [-Register <f>] [-Hub <f>]` | the register plan (`out\register-plan.json` by default, `rp-` id), the hub plan (`hub-plan.json` by default, `hp-` id, optional), one `archive_view`, both `export_titles`, the register's `get_register` pages and root, the current Hub Cards and Topic Cards the hub plan would change, the protected scope (`get_cards` text batches for every human Card and every agent Model Card); a staged hub plan whose reviewed authored base has moved is refused as stale | `publish-review.json` (`xp-` content id), `publish-review.md`, one body per read Card under `publish-endpoints\` | `{ok, step, review_id, contract, archive, register, hubs, items, checks_failed, protected, partial, path, markdown}` |
| `apply [-File <selection.json>]` | the review (whose content must still hash to its id), the selection, the archive identity, the protected scope, the staged hub plan (its SHA-256 and id), the current register, every changed Card back | `publish-result.json`; `<Work>\state.json` when a hub item was accepted; the Cards the accepted items identify | `{ok, step, review_id, archive, summary, register, hubs, protected, backup, requires_revision, path}`; exit 1 when an item conflicted or a revised plan is required |
| `check [-File <publish-result.json>]` | the result, the register's entries and layout, each applied item's Card, the protected scope | nothing | `{ok, step, review_id, applied, missing, diverged, complete, faults, entries, protected_digest, protected_cards, protected_unchanged, revision, published_revision, changed_since, path}` |

S27-AC01 is the review. It binds the exact register plan id and hub plan id, the archive identity and the revision it read, and one item per change: every planned term (its exact row, the row the register carries today, each target with its Workspace, Zone, type, facet, title, whether it is a substantive Card and the first 200 characters of its own body with its size, the term-table source rows, the sense evidence, the ambiguity fault), the accepted pinyin bands with their readings and the unresolved terms and readings, every part's address, authored fingerprint, old and planned body, and every staged hub change (the exact old body and authored fingerprint it replaces, the new body, whether an existing hub is reused and why, its crossing lines and every linked Card's membership). It records the policy checks §8.2 and §15-phase-3 ask for — substantive Topic and Literature destinations, mirror, `one-book`, anchor title, register-cycle, link membership — the protected scope (every human Card and every agent Model Card with its authored fingerprint), and `publish-endpoints\` with every body it read, so the reviewer compares against what was read, not a later archive. The review id is `xp-` and the SHA-256 of the content without its own id and creation time: an edited review is refused, not trusted. `plan` changes no Card.

S27-AC02 is the selection: `schema_version: 1`, the review id, the archive id, a nonempty note, the verified backup (`path`, `sha256`, `bytes`), `readings_reviewed: true` when a register row is accepted (the pinyin and grouping evidence), and the item ids to `accept` and `decline`. An item the selection does not name is `declined`, recorded as `not-selected`: no implementation ticket, no new-book scope, and no silence is permission. A selection is refused for another review id, another archive, an unknown or repeated item, an empty note, a missing or mismatched backup, and an accepted register row without the reviewed readings. The gate verifies the backup itself: a readable SQLite file whose `metadata` names this archive and whose `cards` table holds Cards.

S27-AC04 and S27-AC06 are the publication. `apply` re-reads the archive and refuses a moved identity, a changed protected human/Model Card (every item `pending`, a quiet window requested) and a changed old base (`conflict`, `requires_revision: true`). The accepted hub changes publish first, in plan order, each under `publish/<review>/1/hubNNNN` receipts with its reviewed `base_fingerprint`, its `@@N` references resolved to the addresses its own calls returned, every created Card's address recorded in `<Work>\state.json` per tree, and the persisted body read back against the reviewed one; a declined creation leaves its place in the state as it was, and the state is an overlay on the file, so every tree, field, job and counter the plan does not name stays recorded. A later plan therefore proposes a declined creation again instead of duplicating it. The register publishes second, through `register::restage` and `Register::publish_plan` with the same receipt discipline, and only when the staged plan is still the reviewed one (its `rp-` id is compared; a plan re-staged since the review makes every accepted row a conflict, never an unreviewed write): the gate re-stages the accepted term rows against the current register body — the reviewed targets are pinned, the parts, bands and root body are re-rendered by the register compiler's own code — publishes the parts first, verifies them, publishes the root last, and marks the item applied only after a fresh `get_register` agrees. Two decisions are refused rather than weakened: declining a register row the register already serves (a total rebuild would move or drop it) is a `conflict` and publishes no register row, and a register whose own frame (its preamble and the groups it does not own, such as `## 领域`) changed since the review is a `conflict` that asks for a new plan. A retry of the same selection recognises each item its own earlier result already published (its Card still carries the reviewed body) and writes no second Card. Every item ends `applied-and-verified`, `declined`, `conflict`, `pending` or `refused`; a tool that returned without an error is never enough.

S27-AC07 is `check`: it reads the archive again and reports the post-publication revision beside the published one, the register's completeness and faults, every accepted row and hub body against the result, and the protected scope. The result file keeps the accepted scope, the backup record, the per-item outcomes and the revision, which is the evidence reader qualification (#121, S28) asks for.

The register's term label is one row before `：`, so a label that itself holds `：` cannot be a term: `register -Step plan` excludes such a model-head name (`excluded` with `reason: term_separator`) instead of publishing a row whose term is a prefix and whose targets never read back.

The hub plan is `hub -Step stage`: the same computation `apply` runs, with every write recorded instead of sent. A created Card has no address yet, so its calls name the N-th call's own result (`@@N`) and the gate resolves them when it publishes that item; every changed existing Card carries the authored body and fingerprint it replaces, so the review shows exactly what it would overwrite.

Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/publish.tests.ps1` (110 assertions) drives both tools on a scratch archive: the hub stage recording its changes without a write, a review over a hub plan whose authored base moved being refused as stale, the review's ids, rows, targets, sources, readings, retained bodies, part fingerprints, protected scope and policy checks, every refused selection (no selection, another review, no note, an unknown item, no reviewed readings, no backup, a backup whose bytes are not the recorded ones) leaving the revision unchanged, a full publication with its read-back register and hub bodies and advanced hub state, `check`, a retry that creates no second Card, a register plan re-staged after the review that publishes nothing (every accepted row a conflict), a stale hub base that is a conflict with the other writer's body preserved, a changed protected Model Card that makes every item wait, a register with a declared part whose review names the part's address, body and authored fingerprint, and a declined row the register already serves that publishes nothing. The module's unit tests cover the review id, the selection validation, the retained frame, the `@@N` resolution, the state merge, the band evidence, the row grammar, and the line endings.

### `restate`

Resolving a new restatement without rewriting a finished model (#107, S08; design §4.5, §5.4; D083-D087, D109-D116). `restate.rs` has four explicit steps and writes no Card until `apply`.

`restate` is an open-scope tool and runs for zt-idea-grow, which writes the Topic Idea Cards: `grow -Step restate-cards` makes one staging folder per source book (`<Work>\restate\rNN`, its `manifest.json` naming the book and its `cards.txt` the run's new Topic Cards from that book), and one `restate` chain reviews each. `-Staging` is the run's staging folder (`manifest.json` names the book's Citation key) and `-Cards` names the run's new Topic Idea Cards, one address per line: they are the only Cards the review may rewrite. A listed Card that is not an agent Topic Idea claim is reported in `skipped` with its reason, and a list of no such claim is refused rather than reported as a clean comparison.

**The run's own population (S23 / #115).** When `-Cards` is absent, `prepare` reads the run's staged `owned-g*.txt` manifests — the receipts-backed owned-Card lists the Card cycle enumerates — instead of a caller's file. That population may be far larger than one review takes, so it is narrowed from the title catalogue (agent Workspace, Topic tree, `idea`, not a tree root, not a restatement entry) and bounded at `MAX_NEW` in address order; the evidence carries `population {owned, reviewable, selected, omitted, skipped, skipped_total}`, so a review over a subset names what it did not compare. A caller's own `-Cards` list keeps the delivered bound (over `MAX_NEW` input addresses is a refusal). A population in which no listed Card is reviewable is refused by name, exactly like an empty caller list.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes (under `<Staging>\restate`) | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `prepare [-Cards <f>]` | `get_cards` for the new Cards, `export_titles`, bounded `get_cards` text batches for the book's claim Cards and the earlier Topic Cards, `get_register` for the entries' terminals | `evidence.json`, `endpoints\<hash>.md`, `rj-01.md`, `dsh-restate-instructions.zh.md` | `{ok, evidence_id, archive_id, archive_revision, cards, reviewable, skipped, population, candidates, unread, attempts, evidence, jobs[]}` |
| `judge -File <f>` | the frozen evidence and the answer file | `judged.json` | `{ok, evidence_id, judged_id, judged, unjudged, file}` |
| `apply -OperationPrefix <run/unit/rev/step> [-Merges <decisions.json>]` | the frozen files, then the rewritten Cards and the watched existing Cards back | `resolution.json` and its staging copy `restate-resolution.json` | `{ok, evidence_id, judged_id, prefix, written, counts, complete, resolution}` |
| `report` | the frozen files only — it starts no client | `restate-report.json`, `restate-report.md` | `{ok, report_id, status, complete, counts, written[]}` |

`prepare` compares each new Card with its own book's anchors and Model Cards/parts and with earlier Topic Idea Cards (D109). Earlier Topic Cards are prefiltered on their titles (character-bigram Dice, the cheap §7.1 comparison) and a bounded set is read in full; the current relatedness (`Relatedness`) is built over that read corpus, so each candidate carries the TF-IDF score, its normalized title similarity, and whether it shares an exact formula string. None of the three is a verdict, and the pair list is bounded to ten per new Card, ordered by shared formula, then relatedness, then title similarity, then address. A candidate that is itself an entry records its `terminal` (D085). The frozen evidence binds the schema, `created_at`, the archive identity and revision, the book, the bounds, the context counts, one `endpoints` record per retained body (with its SHA-256), the read errors, the new Cards with their statements, assumptions, formulas, sources, the archive's exact `statement_fingerprint` and its authored `fingerprint` (the base an identified edit uses), and the selected candidates with the same evidence. `evidence_id = "e-" + content_hash(payload, [evidence_id, created_at])` through `canon`. All reads are one view: a changed revision retries the whole set once and then fails with the changing-view fault (D300).

`judge` validates one answer against the frozen evidence (whose content must still hash to its own `evidence_id`, so no step reads an edited set): the schema, the evidence id, at most 2,000 judgments, each pair prepared exactly once, a verdict of `same`/`different`/`uncertain`, cited evidence only from addresses the pair read, and a reason. A `same` verdict names the canonical statement (one of the two Cards), the `scope` in which assumptions coincide, and a `remedy` of `distinct-implication`, `entry` or `existing-card-change`. The first two carry the accepted `title` and complete `body` of the **new** Card's rewrite: an `entry` body has a `## 同述入口` section naming exactly one Link, and that Link is the candidate's terminal substantive Card (a book anchor is a comparison candidate, never a terminal: such a pair takes a distinct implication); a `distinct-implication` body has no entry section and links the statement's home — the terminal, or the anchor itself — with a reason in the same sentence; both keep every source Link the new Card had and neither repeats the title in the body (the archive refuses a duplicate leading title). `existing-card-change` carries no text and stays pending. A `different` verdict states the distinction and names no remedy; an `uncertain` one names what is missing in `needs`. One new Card has at most one accepted rewrite. A refused answer writes nothing and resolves nothing (D302).

`apply` carries the accepted rewrite through the archive's identified receipt path: `edit_card` with the frozen authored `base_fingerprint`, only the listed new Cards allowed, one operation key per rewrite under the prefix. It reads every output back, compares the authored fingerprint with the receipt's `after_fingerprint` and the exact accepted text, runs zt-check on the rewritten Card (a hard fault leaves it `unverified`), and confirms that every canonical statement it watched still has its authored fingerprint, so the existing statement keeps its home (D114). Each finding gets one explicit status: `resolved-by-rewrite` only after that readback and check, `pending-existing-card-change` for a resolution that would need the existing Card changed, `recommended` while no rewrite is applied, and `different`/`unresolved` for the other verdicts. `-Merges` names the Card cycle's `decisions.json`: a recorded merge verdict counts as `resolved-by-rewrite` only when a rewrite covers its pair, and otherwise stays `pending-existing-card-change`; a recorded verdict is never counted as an applied merge (D115, D116). The staging copy `restate-resolution.json` is what the Card cycle's report reads.

`report` renders the explicit statuses from the frozen files alone, with one pending and one verified example, the counts (`pairs`, `recommended`, `resolved_by_rewrite`, `pending_existing_card_change`, `unverified`, `different`, `unresolved`, `unjudged`), `completeness`, and `report_id = "rr-" + content_hash(...)`. Its `status` is `partial` while a pair is unjudged or a rewrite unverified, `resolved-with-pending` while an existing-Card change or a recorded merge verdict stays pending, `recommended` while accepted rewrites were not applied, and `resolved` otherwise. The public outcomes are fixed: exit **0** for an answered step, **1** for a refused input (a missing `-Cards`, a lost authored base, an invalid answer, a non-terminal entry, a dropped source, a second rewrite of one Card, a missing prefix).

The `card` report of the same staging folder adds `restate`: the counts and completeness of `restate-resolution.json` when that file exists, and `null` when the cycle reviewed no restatement — never a clean measurement it did not make.

Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/restate.tests.ps1` and the module's unit tests (below). The population mode is covered by `restate::tests::the_run_population_is_narrowed_bounded_and_explained` (the title-catalogue narrowing, the omission count and every exclusion reason).
### `register`

The concept register's plan and publication (#101, design §4.1 and §8.3-8.5, D058-D062, D170-D175, D276, D289, D296, D297, D299). The register is an ordinary Hub Card of level `register` ([Hub Cards](hub-cards.md)); `register.rs` stages its body and publishes it through the MCP tools. It judges no meaning: the concept review (#97, `concepts`) does that, and a reviewed report is read as evidence.

| Step | Reads (one `zt mcp`) | Writes | Prints (last line) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan [-Review <file>]` | `get_cards` (body-free identity), `export_titles` (agent and human), bounded `get_cards` text batches for the term-table bodies, `get_register`, `get_card` for the current register body | `out\register-plan.json` | `{ok, plan_id, archive, register, terms, entries, unresolved, faults, excluded, read_only, review, body_lines, path}` |
| `apply` | the staged plan; `get_cards` identity, `export_titles` (both Workspaces), `get_register`, `get_card`; then `create_topic`/`create_successor`/`edit_card` and a verifying read of every `get_register` page | the Topic `索引`, the register Hub Card, or that one register body | `{ok, plan_id, register, created, changed, entries, read_only, unresolved, review, complete, archive_revision, verified}` |
| `check` | the staged plan, when present, and every `get_register` page | nothing | `{ok, plan_id, register, register_missing, entries, read_only, unresolved, complete, faults, missing, diverged, extra, archive_revision}` |

`plan` reads every agent book term table through zt-check's own row parser (`zt_check::check::term_table`, the `术语 | 不用 | 意思 | 首次定义` columns; D063) and every model-head name, and builds one **concept** per source row: its preferred word and the book's `不用` words are its accepted spellings, and each spelling gets the same candidate set, so two spellings of one term point at the same entry Cards without an alias table. A title **names** a term when it contains it, except a Topic Card, whose title must be the term itself (D173-D175). Candidates are ranked by three preferences, not quotas (D170-D173): 1 a clear definition or statement, including a Model part that is the definition and a reviewed `entry`; 2 a Model Card for a whole model; 3 a standalone Topic claim. The three best distinct addresses become the row, at most three; any further acceptable one is counted in `overflow`. A term with no defensible target is `unresolved` with its source row (one finding per row, naming its spellings), and no address is manufactured (D174). Two rows that share a spelling are combined; two different meanings for one spelling are an `ambiguous` fault that keeps every destination (D299).

`-Review` names a reviewed `concept-review.json` (S04). It is read-only evidence: its `archive.id` must match the current archive, its `report_id` must have the reviewed shape (`r-` and 64 lowercase hex; the review tool verifies the content hash), and the plan records `report_id`, `evidence_id`, `status`, `complete`, and the finding count. A `topic-concept` finding with verdict `same` adds the finding's `entry` as a tier-1 target for the proposal title's spelling; a `different` finding keeps its `candidate` out of that spelling's targets. An `entry` in the human Workspace is an **explicitly selected read-only target** (D174): both Workspaces' titles are read, the card is named in the row and listed in the term's `read_only_targets`, and no agent write ever touches it. An `entry` in neither Workspace is `excluded` with reason `reviewed_entry_missing` — a stale review needs reviewing again, and no address is invented.

The plan id is `rp-` plus the content hash (`canon::content_hash`) of the schema, archive identity, terms, unresolved rows, faults, excluded rows, review summary, readings, parts, obsolete parts, and the root body: re-planning the same acceptance gives the same id, a publication in between does not change it, and a changed reading is an explicit new plan (D184). A register over 120 term rows, or one already split, is planned as `词条分册` parts: the accepted readings group the terms into the `A–F`, `G–L`, `M–R`, `S–Z` bands, then `0–9`, then `其他`, each ordered by its accepted reading and cut into contiguous parts of at most 120 rows (`A–F(2)`, …). The plan holds each part's exact body, the root body naming each part by `{{part:N}}`, and the identified calls that publish them. A part address is reused by its accepted label, then by its position; a declared part the plan no longer needs keeps its address and stays declared, emptied, pending a separate deletion plan; a register whose declared parts are moved, missing, a Hub, listed twice, nested, or unlisted is refused before any write (D182, AC05). The root keeps its preamble and every group it does not own (`## 领域`, `## 未归入领域的主题`) and replaces its term group and its `词条分册` declaration. Term, part, and target order are deterministic (`canon::ordinal_cmp`, and the base letters of an accepted reading); no other dependency or service is involved (D184).

A rebuild of an existing register is a change to existing Cards, so it is published through the review gate `publish` (#119, S27; D191): that gate re-stages the accepted rows against the current body and calls the same `publish_plan` path. A term label is the text before the row's `：`, so a model-head name that itself holds `：` is excluded (`excluded`, `reason: term_separator`) rather than published as a row whose term is a prefix.

`apply` refuses a plan staged for another archive, a plan that names a Card in neither Workspace (a reviewed human target is a valid target), a Workspace with two registers, a plan that keeps more than 120 term rows on the root, and an obstructed `词条分册` layout; a revision change alone is not a refusal, because the plan's own read bound the revision it was drafted from. It then publishes the staged publication in two identified batches (D189, D190). The parts batch creates the Topic `索引` (`本主题收放代理工作区的入口：索引卡与领域枢纽。`), the register shell when the Workspace has none, and each new part, edits a reused part in place, empties an obsolete one (`## 词条`), and verifies every part's exact body and the untouched root shell; it runs through `invoke_identified_staged`, because the root shell it creates is edited by the next batch. The root batch publishes the root last with the addresses the receipts returned. Retrying the same accepted plan replays every committed call instead of creating a second Card, and a plan whose root body is already published skips the root call. It finishes by reading **every page** of the register back through `get_register` (`entry_next_offset` until null, `entry_limit` 1,000) and fails unless every page declares the same revision and register, the pages are contiguous from offset 0, their entries add up to `entry_total`, the published terms and targets are exactly the planned ones with the view `complete` and no duplicate row, the root and part bodies are exactly the staged ones, and the declared labels and addresses are the accepted layout. A revision that moves between pages is retried once; a read set that keeps moving is one changing-view fault (§12.3). `check` reads every page the same way and compares the staged plan with the published entries (`missing`, `diverged`, `extra`), the declared parts (`plan_parts`), the readings, and the duplicate rows, without writing.

Public coverage is `register-compiler.tests.ps1` (a scratch archive; a book term table with an accepted rejected word and an unresolvable row; model-head names; a reviewed `same` entry and `different` sense; the accepted readings with a polyphonic term, an uncertain one, a malformed initial and a term the file never mentions; one, two and three targets; apply, an idempotent second apply, check, and a re-plan with the same id — 38 assertions), `register-human-target.tests.ps1` (a reviewed human entry as a read-only target, the human Workspace never written, a stale reviewed entry excluded), `register-paging.tests.ps1` (1,005 rows published as nine parts, checked, re-applied and paged through `get_register`), `register-parts.tests.ps1` (128 rows in four bands; parts published before the root and verified; each part's label, row grammar, and membership; `zt-check` classifying a part as navigation; the readings report; a retry after an interrupted publication replaying six receipts without a duplicate Card; the refused obstructed layout; the refused large root), and the module's unit tests for the readings, the buckets and their split, the part body and title, the root declaration and its `{{part:N}}` fill, the staging (shell, reuse, obsolete, new part, unchanged plan), the obstruction gate, the published-layout verification, the plan id, the selection order, the unresolved row, the ambiguity, the read-only human target, the page merge, the kept field navigation, and the report-id shape.

### `card`

The port of `card-compiler.ps1`; [Digest scripts](digest-scripts.md) describes the cycle, the job files, and the JSON formats. One `Zt` serves the scoped quality reads and the writes. `invoke_audit` selects the group/book scope and calls `invoke_scoped_audit`; the former `audit::Params::for_card`/global-report path is removed. The scoped read's catalogue is the Literature trees only (`snapshot::read_literature_trees`), so a Topic-tree Card is never reviewed, rewritten or linked by the cycle; `attach_book_links` attaches each catalogue Card's outgoing Links and kinds from the box's `list_links`, skips a Link from or to a Topic-tree Card, and still refuses any other Card missing from the catalogue as an incomplete read (`incomplete book relationship source catalogue`, `… target catalogue`). Writes go through `calls::run` in one batch; after a failed call, the rest go again from the next call.

`-Sample` is the deterministic Idea sample of R297: the eligible population is every fault-free Idea Card in scope, each group of the staged `owned-gNN.txt` manifests gives one Card first (taken in the groups' own hash order), the rest of the budget takes the lowest `sha256(seed:group:address)` keys, and a Card the retained `source-faults.json` names for this book is always included. `state.sampled` (and the report's `sample`) records the fraction, seed, population, eligible count, target, size, per-group coverage, the exact addresses, their source spans and `proves: "sample"`. `-ModelReview` extends a whole-book cycle to the Model drafts no model chain owns: the Model Cards are style-checked with the rest, added as review entries (their `src-gNN.txt` span is the reviewed span), and each outcome lands in `state.reviews` and `report.reviews` with the authored fingerprint. A rewrite whose body carries a `（补…` form other than the two established markers is refused by `test_rewrite`, so the precheck and apply agree. `-Cards <file>` restricts the cycle to the exact addresses a finalization plan lists (one per line, category still writable), so the one permitted correction cycle cannot widen into the book; the plan output carries `cards_scope`. The report also records `kind: "cards_cycle"`, `unit_kind`, the cycle's `work` folder, the audit folder with its checked revision, and `quality_complete`.

| Step | Last line | Files |
|---|---|---|
| `plan` | `{book, picked, with_faults, review, orphans, inbound_repair_cards, faults, jobs}`; with `-Compact` also `dir`, `lead`, `prompts`, and short `jobs` | `before/` (the audit), `state.json`, `fix-NN.md`, `orphans-NN.md`, the two instruction files with `{{KEY}}` replaced |
| `apply` | `{applied_jobs, pending_jobs, retried_jobs, waiting_jobs, counts}` | `state.json` after every job; `owned-gNN.txt` gains each split Card |
| `check` | `{jobs, faults, orphans, inbound_repair_cards}` (and the `-Compact` fields) | `after/`, `state.json`, `fix-NN-r2.md`, `fix-NN.md`, `orphans-NN.md` |
| `report` | the report with `refused` as a count and `restate` when the staging folder holds a restatement resolution | `after/`, `report.json`, `decisions.json` |
| `precheck` | fault lines, then `faults: N` | none |

Each job file holds `SELF-CHECK: zt-digest card -Step precheck -Work "<absolute work folder>" -Job <id>`, so the writer's self-check starts no pwsh. The tool reads `dsh-card-instructions.zh.md` and `dsh-orphan-instructions.zh.md` from `ZT_DIGEST_SCRIPTS`, which the forwarder sets to its own folder, or else from the `scripts/book-digest` folder of the repository the binary was built from. The explain job files of `model` name `SELF-CHECK: zt-digest model -Step explain-check …` the same way.

### `model`

The model compiler of `model-compiler.ps1` (`model::compiler`). Parameters, with defaults: `-Step`* (one of the steps below, case ignored), `-Work`*, `-Staging`, `-Models` (`a,b,…`), `-Plan`, `-Need` (`a=N;b=M`), `-Changed`, `-Rebuild`, `-Level`, `-MaxSentences` (12), `-MaxChars` (400), `-MaxSteps` (6), `-MaxExplainSentences` (16), `-MaxExplainChars` (600), `-MaxParts` (0), `-BriefDoc`, `-FillDoc`, `-ReviewDoc` (default: the shared `dsh-brief`, `dsh-fill`, `dsh-review` instructions), `-Job`, `-File`. `*` marks a mandatory parameter; an empty `-Work` fails.

| Step | Reads | Writes | Prints |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `brief` | `get_model` per model | the model's entry in `state.json` (status `brief`, `steps.brief` with the context/job hashes); `<slug>/context.md` (in-process `model-context`), `read-only.allow.txt`, `brief-job.md` | `{"jobs":[…],"skipped":[…]}` |
| `validate` | `-Plan`, `get_model`, `figures.json`, unit forms, the prepared source the plan's `source_faults` name and the plan's reviewed `src` spans | the run's `source-faults.json` and `source-faults.md` (one entry per source location, attempts merged); `<slug>/source-review.json` (R247: one entry per written part with its resolved spans and outcome, plus the accepted plan hash and the brief's context-pack hash; `complete` only when every written part has a reviewed span) | `source-faults: N recorded, M source faults in <report>` when the plan carries any, `source-review: complete\|incomplete N parts in <file>`, then one fault per line, then `faults: N` |
| `apply` | each `<slug>/plan.json` with status `brief` or `planned` | Cards (level, skeleton, moves, merges, 结构); `setting.md`; `job-fNN.md` and `job-fNN.allow.txt`; `moves.tmp`; the accepted plan's source faults and its source-review artifact (`state.source_review`, `steps.source_review`); a plan whose written part has no reviewed span fails the model instead of building it; `steps.plan`/`steps.apply` with the plan hash | `{"jobs":[…],"models":[{"model","status","error"}]}` |
| `derive` | `plan.json`, premise Cards | `job-dNN.md`, allow files; status `derived`; `steps.derive` | `{"jobs":[…]}` |
| `review` | `get_model` | `review-NN.md`; status `reviewed`; clears `rereview`; `steps.review` | `{"jobs":[…]}` |
| `split` | new `review-*.json`, `-Need` | Cards (new parts, cut 结果, re-pointed next part, 结构, chain lines); allow files; `follow-NN.md` or a new `job-dNN.md`; `steps.split` | `{"jobs":[…],"orphans":[…]}` |
| `close` | `get_model` (each model's `build`: closure, forward, reach_in, unanchored; each Card's supplied-step markers); the plan's taken parts (共用 lines rebuilt first; each 结构 list recorded for `report`) | `follow-NN.md` per conversation (one 修正 note per fault, in the Card's own job: the Model Card's fill job or the part's job) | `{"jobs":[…],"faults":N,"unowned":[…]}` |
| `report` | `get_model`, zt-check `--model … --skip-shared` | `state.json` (`steps.report`: parts, todo, derivations, style, build faults) | `{"models":[…]}` |
| `resume` | `-Models`, `state.json`, `plan.json`, the mapped Cards, `get_model` | Cards: none; the real fill/derive job files of the first missing step; `steps.resume`; status `derived` when a derivation round starts | `{"models":[{"model","status","plan_hash","from","missing","fill","derive","reused","complete","round","jobs"}],"jobs":[…]}` |
| `explain` | `get_model`, the setting Card, the Model Card | `setting.md`, `context.md` (with `-Staging`), `explain-NN.md`, `out/`; `state.explain` | `{"jobs":[…],"parts":N.0}` (a double, as `Measure-Object -Sum` gave it) |
| `explain-check` | `-Job`, its result file (or `-File`), the Cards, zt-check `--json` | nothing | fault lines, `(blocked, …)`, `(kept: …)`, `<address> ok`, then `faults: N` |
| `explain-apply` | every explain job with parts left | Cards (25 edits per helper call), `explain-follow-NN.md`; `state.explain` | `{"applied","refused","blocked","kept","jobs"}` |
| `explain-report` | `get_model` per explained model | nothing | `{"models":[…]}` |

`state.json` is written as `ConvertTo-Json` wrote it: two-space indent, CRLF line ends, keys in first-assignment order. Job files keep the script's HEADER lines (`HELPER:`, `CHECK:`, `ALLOW:`, `LIMITS:`, `SELF-CHECK:`), and the lines a StringBuilder wrote keep their CRLF ends. Writes go through `calls::run` with `-Moves <Work>\moves.tmp`, one renumbering call at a time; every address in `state.json` follows each moved map. Reads use `model::read_calls`, which fails with `zt-mcp failed: <i> error <message>` as zt-calls.ps1 did; a write fails with `zt: <message>`. A failed `apply` of one model records that text as the model's `error`.

### `model-context`

`model::context` (model-context.ps1). Parameters: `-Staging`*, `-Model`*, `-Out`* (an empty value fails, as PowerShell's mandatory binding did). Reads the Model Card, `get_model`, the book's tree, `owned-g*.txt`, `manifest.json`, and the section files; writes the Markdown pack to `-Out` (UTF-8, CRLF line ends); prints `{"model","group","anchor","sections","parts","context"}`. `model brief` and `model explain` call `context::build` on their own `zt mcp`; a failure fails `brief` with `model-context.ps1 failed for <model>` and leaves `explain` with `上下文包: 无`.

### `list-models`

`model::list` (list-models.ps1). Parameters: `-Staging`*, and `-Groups` (a string such as `1,2,3`) or `-All` with an optional `-CitationKey`; giving both sets fails. Reads `owned-g*.txt`, `list_trees`, the trees in `Sort-Object -Unique` order (culture order, case ignored), and `get_model` per Model Card; prints `{"models":[{"address","title","group","level","form","parts","todo","placeholders","issues","faults"}]}`. `todo`/`placeholders` are the checked unwritten state (a Model Card or part body still holding `待写`), so a partly built model is visible without treating its part count as completion.

### The stage 2 tools

Each keeps its script's parameters, defaults, mandatory parameters, steps, stdout lines, and files; [Digest scripts](digest-scripts.md) describes what each does. What the port changed:

| Tool | One process does | Job-file lines |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `hub` | survey, plan, apply, check, report on one `zt mcp`; zt-check on the reasons and group names in-process. The precheck refuses (`mirror`) a topic hub whose lines only repeat the tree map, judges a Topic body `entries` section (one to three Cards of the tree, each with its reason, refused when the tree already has a topic hub or needs a line hub), and `apply` writes that section instead of a hub; `check` reports `one_book` advice as its own list and `entry_faults` for a section that broke or disappeared; `report` counts `entries_valid`/`entries_invalid`; `plan` skips a tree a valid entry section already satisfies (D166–D168) | `SELF-CHECK: zt-digest hub -Step precheck -Work "…" -File "…"` |
| `bridge` | reads above 40 calls on up to six threads, one `zt mcp` each; zt-check in-process; precheck and report start no `zt mcp`; with `-Apply` the unused preview read is gone. Sources, targets and carriers are Literature-tree Cards: a target in a Topic tree is skipped, and the book's own Literature Idea Cards are Bridge sources, ranked after the Model heads | `SELF-CHECK: zt-digest bridge -Step precheck -Staging '…' -CitationKey '…' -Work '…' -Job br-NNN` |
| `math` | the 13 steps; writes with `-Log owned-gNN.txt -SrcLog src-gNN.txt` in `skeleton` | none (the script wrote no SELF-CHECK line) |
| `event`, `finding` | one shared cycle (`event/layer.rs`); zt-check on the drafts in-process, so precheck starts no `zt mcp` unless an event names an `existing` Card; `plan` also writes the accepted `plan.json` (candidate ids/spans, job-to-group mapping) and `apply` keeps each applied job's answer, entry and refused facts; `events-finish`/`findings-finish` complete the registered layer unit | `SELF-CHECK: zt-digest event\|finding -Step precheck -Staging "…" -Work "…" -Job <id>` |
| `terms` | plan and apply; `get_tree` and one write with `-Apply` | none |
| `skeleton` | the audit in-process (`audit::run`) into `<Work>\audit`, on the same `zt mcp`. It writes the Literature Card index and the structure Card only, no Topic body: `plan` lists no Topics (its last line has no `topics`), an answer that still carries `topics` is the problem `topics: a digest writes no Topic body; Topic trees are zt-idea-grow's`, and key Cards and an event's supports skip Topic addresses | `SELF-CHECK: \`zt-digest skeleton -Step apply -Staging "…" -Work "…"\`（不要加 -Apply）` |
| `organize-survey` | the audit in-process into `<Work>\audit` | none |
| `organize-apply` | each merge and move one `calls::run` call with `-Moves <Work>\moves.jsonl`; later addresses follow every `moved` map. It runs in the Literature scope unless given `-Open`, so a digest's Organize merges no Topic and moves no Topic Card; a Topic merge run by hand (or by a later idea-grow round) passes `-Open` | none |
| `figures` | edits only the Cards in the book's own Literature tree (a Card elsewhere that cites the book, a Topic Card or another book's, is not its to edit); Card reads and writes on one `zt mcp`; `zt scholar keep-figures`, `sql`, `file` still start `zt` once each (no MCP tool keeps all of a document's figures in one call) | none |
| `prepare` | the Literature tree with `-Apply` through `calls::run` (no pwsh helper); `zt scholar` as `figures` | the group files already named `zt-digest call`; the write line is `… \| zt-digest call - -Log "<owned>" -SrcLog "<src>"` (with the note 只写本书的文献树) and no `-TopicGate`, the snapshot line names the Literature-card snapshot, and the default `-Instructions` is `dsh-digest-base.zh.md,dsh-model-cards.zh.md` |
| `prepare-link` | one `export_titles` kept to the Literature trees (`cards-all.md` lists no Topic tree), one bounded bulk text `get_cards` read of every Card, one `list_links` and two `get_register` views in the same `zt mcp` | unchanged (`zt-digest call`, `zt-check`) |
| `snapshot-topics` | every tree of both Workspaces through one `export_titles` on one `zt mcp` | none |

The instruction-file copies replace `{{HELPER}}` with `zt-digest call`; no instruction file holds the token any more, so this changes no file.

### `bridge -Step project`: one selected report's read-only projection

`zt-digest bridge -Step project -Report <proposals.json> -At <address>` (S20, #112, design §10.3; `Bridge::project` in `src/bridge.rs`) is the optional source the GUI's distant slot reads. It takes no `-Staging` and no `-CitationKey`: the report carries its own Citation key. It reads only the selected file, validates it with the same check an apply uses (`report::validate_published`: `schema` 1 and the `r-` content hash over the report without `report_id`/`created_at`), and refuses a missing or edited report as `{"ok":false,"status":"invalid_report"}` with exit code 1.

It then reads the tree list (which trees are agent Literature trees) and each endpoint's own Card through one `zt mcp` (`projection_context`), and compares each with the frozen snapshot (`changed_endpoint`: workspace, tree, kind, type, facet, emphasis, level, form, title, the inferred book, then the authored fingerprint over the same ten fields; generated Reverse links are outside it). It deliberately does not read a whole tree's summaries the way an apply does: a big book tree can exceed the MCP reply bound, and a projection needs only each endpoint's own attribution and fields. A proposal qualifies when the requested Card is its `carrier` or its `target` and both endpoints are unchanged; the answer names the other endpoint as the candidate, with the exact `report`, `proposal`, `relation` (the judged Relation kind), `sentence`, `section`, and which side the requested Card is (`focus: "carrier"` or `"target"`). `status` is `done` (at least one current candidate), `stale` (proposals name the Card but none is current, each with its `why`: a changed field, a changed body, or a missing endpoint), or `not_in_report`. Output is bounded to `PROJECT_MAX` = 20 candidates in report order, with `counts` (`proposals`, `at`, `current`, `stale`, `omitted`) and `bounds.candidates_max`.

It runs no judge, writes no Card, and publishes no report: the published report and the archive are only read. The GUI spawns it with that fixed argument vector, caches its answer by report hash and archive revision, and cancels it after ten seconds ([GUI](gui.md)); the report's own immutable id and the apply path stay authoritative for any accepted Bridge.

### `prepare -Book`: the resolved book

`-Book` takes a Scholar Archive Work id or an exact title and resolves the book through the read-only bridge on the same `zt mcp`: `scholar_archive_read` with `get_work`, `list_works` and `list_work_files`, and `scholar_archive_file` for the document itself. Nothing is written to the Scholar Archive; the document lands in ZET's document cache, and the only file the resolution writes is the caller's `<Out>\preparation.json`. `prepare/resolve.rs` owns it.

| Step | Decision |
| --- | --- |
| Work | a value of only digits is a Work id and must exist (`get_work`); a title resolves only when exactly one search result's title equals it after whitespace collapse and case folding; two or more exact matches, an inexact title, or no match return the candidates (at most 10, each with its files) for a selection |
| Markdown | the Work's present, linked Markdown files are ranked by saved metadata: `display_role = chinese_bilingual_markdown` (1), a bilingual or Chinese file name such as `.zh-bilingual.md` (2), `display_role = ocr_markdown` (3), any other Markdown (4). One file of the best rank resolves; several are a selection, and `-FileId` picks one (it must be a present Markdown of that Work) |
| BibTeX | generated from the saved metadata as `@book{key, author, title, year, publisher, doi}` with the fields the metadata actually has (the venue is the publisher). A given `-CitationKey` becomes the entry key; without one the key is the first author's surname and the year, or `Work<id>` when that is not a valid key. The key is checked against the archive's Cards first: an existing Card or tree refuses (`Citation key ... already names a Card`) |
| range | the scanned `#`/`##` headings propose `start` after the front matter (`Preface`, `前言`, ...), `end` at the first back matter (`参考文献`, `References`, `附录`, ...), and `skip` for exercises, references, indexes and appendices inside the range. Explicit `-StartHeading`, `-EndHeading` and `-SkipHeadings` are the frozen choice, and a heading the document does not have is a fault |
| conflicts | a given `-Source` must be the resolved document (the same file or the same bytes), a given `-BibTeX` must equal the generated entry after whitespace, a given `-BookTitle` must equal the Work's title, and a `-FileId` must be one of the Work's files: anything else is an input error, never a precedence rule |

The resolution freezes the source path, its SHA-256, the key, the book title and the start/end/skip headings in `<Out>\preparation.json` (schema 1, `status: ready`, `work`, `file`, `citation`, `headings` with the top headings, the preamble, and every excluded section with its `before_start`, `skipped` or `after_end` reason, and `frozen`), and records a compact `book_resolution` plus the result's path in `manifest.json`. Both are written before the first section or group file, so no group work exists without them. The Literature Card's Document links come from the resolved file list, not a second archive read.

A choice that stays open writes only the preparation result (`status: needs_selection`, one `selections` entry with `kind`, `reason`, `message` and the candidates), prints it as the last line, and exits **3** with no Card, group file or manifest. A bridge failure is a specific fault that names the missing fact and the explicit `-Source`/`-FileId` fallback: a missing Work, a Work with no present Markdown, an unavailable file list, or an undeliverable document.

### `prepare`: the chapter endnotes

A digested book's collected notes (`注释`, `尾注`, `notes`, ...) stand outside the range, so a definition the book gives only in a note (the missing endnotes of I30) reached no group. `prepare/notes.rs` extracts that section instead of digesting it (design 11.6, R310/R311).

- **Where the notes are.** The section is the frozen end heading when that heading is a notes label, else the first notes-labelled heading after the range. With no end heading the range runs to the end of the book, so a notes heading inside it is the `notes_inside_range` fault: it names the heading and asks for `-EndHeading`, and no notes are extracted twice. The section's own text is never part of `book.zh.md` or of a unit.
- **Chapter mapping.** The digest's chapters are its level-1 headings in reading order, each with a chapter id (`c01`, `c02`, ...) and its number (`第3章` and `第三章` are 3). A notes subsection maps to exactly one chapter by its heading title, then by its chapter number, then by the prefixes of its note labels (`[1.3]` to chapter 1). One chapter is a mapping; several are `notes_ambiguous` (the subsection names them), none is `notes_unmapped`. Both are preparation faults, never a silent drop. A subsection whose body is only blank lines gives an empty block, which maps to no chapter and is skipped.
- **What is written.** `notes/<chapter-id>.md`, or `notes/<chapter-id>-<part>.md` when a chapter's notes exceed `-NoteMaxChars` (6000). A part is a whole number of notes, so a note larger than the budget keeps its own part and is never cut. Each file holds the source path, the original line spans, the note ids and the SHA-256 of its exact note text. `manifest.notes` records the section (heading, line, span, characters, `outside_range`), every chapter (`chapter_id`, `chapter`, `number`, `present`, `files` with path/hash/span/ids, `note_ids`, `spans`, `sha256`, `chars`, `split`, `missing`, `budget`), the notes text, and the faults. Notes create no group and no Card.
- **What the readers get.** Every group file of the chapter, and every model context pack of a group's model, ends with `## 本章注释（只读参考文本…）`: the source lines, the note ids, the notes text inline when it fits the budget, or the labelled part files with their ids when it does not. A note the chapter cites but the book never defines stays visible there (`缺失的注释`) and as the `notes_missing` fault: it is a limitation of the source, never a reason to invent the formula.
- **Where the faults go.** The notes faults are recorded in the run's [source-fault record](#source-faults-the-retained-source-fault-record) under the origin `prepare`, so the person who keeps the Scholar Archive reads one report.

### `source-faults`: the retained source-fault record

`source-faults -Staging <out> [-Json <file>] [-Origin <label>]` (`faults.rs`) keeps the run's source faults and renders `<out>\source-faults.md` from the retained record alone (design 11.6, R312/R313). `prepare` records its notes faults there; the model compiler records each architect plan's `source_faults`; `-Json` takes a list or an object with a `faults` array.

- **A fault entry** names `start_line`/`end_line` (1-based lines of the prepared source), at least one of `chapter`, `equation`, `note`, the `observed` text the source prints there, a `proposed` interpretation, a `confidence` of `high`/`medium`/`low`, and the `affected` Card addresses. An optional `source_sha256`/`file_id` must equal the prepared source's; a different one is refused rather than rebound. An optional `verified` correction needs its `text` and the `evidence` that verified it. `prepare`'s notes faults carry their own kinds and a message.
- **Evidence.** The `observed` text must appear in the source's lines at that span after whitespace folding. A record that does not is kept as an unsupported observation, rendered in its own section and never as a fault about the source, so a stale observation or a historical example from the design notes cannot enter the report as current evidence. Nothing in the tool writes to the Scholar Archive or to a Card.
- **One entry per source location.** A fault's identity is its kind, its lines and its equation/note label; a second observation of the same location merges into the same entry: the first `observed` text stays, every attempt (origin, proposed text, confidence) is kept, and the affected Cards are unioned. A limited notes statement (`notes_missing`, `notes_split`) is identified by its chapter and ids. Re-recording an identical attempt changes nothing.
- **The record and the report.** `<out>\source-faults.json` is the retained record (`schema_version`, the source binding, the faults sorted by their lines). `<out>\source-faults.md` is deterministic: the same record renders byte-identical, without a clock. It lists the source binding and counts, then `## 源码缺陷` (supported source faults, each with its location, observed text, proposed or verified correction, confidence, affected Cards, attempts and origins), `## 未能核对的观察`, `## 准备阶段的映射缺陷` and `## 源码缺失与已声明的限制`. It prints the same counts as one compact JSON line. prepare renders the report once more after it publishes manifest.json, so the file left in the folder names the book; the render still reads the record alone, so a later source-faults render is byte-identical.

### Library functions for the tools

| Function | Does |
|---|---|
| `calls::run(zt, calls, options)` | the helper, returning `Done` lines and an optional `Failure` |
| `calls::invoke(zt, calls)` | `Invoke-Zt`: the answers of the calls whose reply text is a JSON object, in order; a failure is `zt: <message>` |
| `calls::read(zt, tool, arguments)` | one read; its answer |
| `audit::run(zt, params, host)` | the audit; `host` gets the progress lines; `audit::Params::for_card(book, out)` gives the parameters `card` uses |
| `check::Check { .. }.run(zt)` | `Invoke-ZtCheck`: zt-check's lines; fails with `zt-check did not run: …` when no `faults: N` line comes back |
| `util::print_last(value)` | the last line of a compiler step |

## Behaviour

### A tool run

1. `main` takes the tool name (case ignored) and hands the rest of the arguments to the tool.
2. The tool parses them with `Args`; an unknown name or a missing value ends the run with `zt-digest <tool>: <error>` and exit code 1.
3. The tool starts one `Zt` when it needs the archive, and passes it to every `calls::invoke`, `calls::run`, and `Check::run`.
4. It prints its lines; a compiler step prints compact JSON last (`util::print_last`).
5. Dropping the `Zt` closes the server's stdin and waits for it.

### A helper batch (`calls::run`)

For each call from `-From` on: resolve `@@N`; apply the allow list; turn `append_card` into `get_card` and `edit_card`; remove `src`; with `-TopicGate` and `create_topic`, lock the gate file and compare the title with the agent Topics (`list_trees`); send the call (in the Literature scope, without `-Open`, the client refuses a Topic, Topic-tree, Hub or Topic-Link write here, before it reaches the archive, and that refusal is the call's `Failure`); release the gate; on an error stop with a `Failure`; record the address; log `create*` addresses, `src`, and `moved` maps; keep the output line. A transport failure (`zt mcp` closed) is an error of the whole run, not a `Failure`.

## Data and storage

zt-digest keeps no state of its own. The helper appends to the files its options name (`-Log`, `-SrcLog`, `-Moves`: UTF-8 without a BOM, one entry per line) and uses `-TopicGate` only as a lock. The ported compilers keep the work folders, `state.json`, job files, and reports of the scripts they replace (see [Digest scripts](digest-scripts.md)). `register` keeps one staged file, `<Work>\out\register-plan.json`: the accepted body, the terms with their sources and targets, the unresolved rows, the faults, the review summary, the archive identity/revision it was drafted from, its content `plan_id`, and a timestamp. It is never rewritten by `apply`; the published register is the archive's own state, read back through `get_register`.

## Configuration

| Setting | Default | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `ZT_CHECK_ZT` | `zt` on PATH | the program `Zt::start` runs as `<program> mcp` |
| `ZT_DIGEST` | `zt-digest` on PATH | the binary a forwarder script runs (a test of an unreleased build) |
| `ZT_DIGEST_REPO` | the repository zt-digest was built from | where `model` finds the paths its job files name (`scripts\book-digest\…`, `skills\zt\scripts\zt-mcp.ps1`) |
| `ZT_DIGEST_SCRIPTS` | `scripts/book-digest` of the repository the binary was built from | where `card`, `math`, `bridge`, `event`, `finding`, `terms`, `skeleton`, and `prepare` read their instruction files (and the folder job files name for them); their forwarders set it |
| `ZT_RACE_TARGET`, `ZT_RACE_FLAG`, `ZT_RACE_LINE` | unset | a test seam of `event` and `finding` (`event/layer.rs`): before the first write batch that edits the target, make the flag file and append the line to the Card, as a parallel stage would. Inert unless the first two are set |
| `ZT_CHECK_ZT` = `tests\seam-zt-mcp.cmd` | — | the Bridge tests' stand-in for `zt mcp` (`seam-zt-mcp.mjs`): forwards to the real server and injects the faults and races of `ZT_FAIL_*` and `ZT_RACE_*` |
| `-TargetDir` of the install script | `D:\zt-digest-target` | the Cargo target folder (E: is nearly full) |

## Usage

```bash
pwsh -NoProfile -File scripts/install-zt-digest.ps1
cd tools/zt-digest && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=D:/zt-digest-target cargo test --release
```

A DeepSeek run or a script calls the helper as `zt-digest call - -Log <file>` with the batch on stdin.

## Testing

- `cargo test --release` in `tools/zt-digest` runs 352 unit tests (0.10.0). The Literature scope adds `main::scope_tests` (every digest step, `organize-apply` and `call` without `-Open` run in the Literature scope; the idea-grow, hub, register, restate, gate, topic-home, join and publish tools and `-Open`/`-open` keep the open scope), `card::tests::book_links_skip_topic_trees_outside_the_literature_catalogue`, the `book_of` assertion that a Topic-tree Card belongs to no book whatever it links, the `skeleton` check that an answer with `topics` is a problem and that key Cards skip Topic addresses, and the two `grow` tests for `merge_limits` and `grown_by_book`. The scope rules themselves are unit tests of `zt_check::mcp::scope` ([zt-check](zt-check.md)).
- `cargo test` in `tools/zt-digest`: the parameter parser, the helper's `@@N` and Topic-gate title rules, the PowerShell semantics of `ps` (truthiness, `[int]`, `-eq`, UTF-16 cuts, `Join-Path`, `Split-Path`, `ConvertTo-Json`, `Sort-Object` order, hashtable keys), the text functions of `model::text` (chain lines, `set_section`, the explanation and its core, the tables), and the no-main-line plan check of `model::compiler` (`lacks_main_line`: only filed parts count, so a model built only from linked parts may start a main line).
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/model-compiler.tests.ps1` drives every `model` step through the forwarder; `math-compiler.tests.ps1` runs `model validate` on a unit plan; `zt-calls.tests.ps1` runs `list-models` under a GBK console. `model-resume.tests.ps1` (20 assertions) checks the checked-state selection and the partial resume; `model-finish.tests.ps1` (37) checks the checked Model completion through a real group and compiler; `model-dependencies.tests.ps1` (24) checks `X#A` producer dependencies, waiting, cycles and named-part faults.
