- `cargo test` in `tools/zt-digest`: the parameter parser and the helper's `@@N` and Topic-gate title rules; `ps` against values pwsh 7.6 printed (`Sort-Object` orders of 200 and 40 items, `[Random]::new(1)`, `ConvertTo-Json` text); `book_of.rs` with the assertions of `book-of.tests.ps1`; the audit's figure names, quantiles, tallies, and Bridges (an incomplete read leaves `island` null, the assertion `bridge-planning.tests.ps1` made through a faulting helper); `audit.md` lists; `audit::measures`' source sets (unknown, multiple, D071's inheritance), the seeded sample, P8's reach, the exact and near title pairs, the fixed sheet's field-by-field validation, the observation check, the medians and rates, and the comparison's scope guard; the card cycle's kind words, Link kinds, rewrite test, ranking, and job text.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/card-compiler.tests.ps1`, `digest-audit.tests.ps1`, `organize.tests.ps1`, `book-terms.tests.ps1`, `bridge-human.tests.ps1`, and `bridge-planning.tests.ps1`: `audit` and `card` through the forwarders on scratch archives.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/bridge-projection.tests.ps1` (S20, #112; 24 assertions) drives `bridge -Step project` on a scratch archive with a real published report: a current proposal for the carrier and for its target with the direction, the exact report/proposal id and sentence, the counts and the twenty-candidate bound, a Card the report never names (`not_in_report`), a path and an address with shell metacharacters read as one argument, a changed endpoint reported `stale` with the field and current again after the restore, a deleted endpoint reported `stale` as a missing endpoint, an earlier-carrier proposal with its recalculated report id, an edited report, a missing report, an over-long address and a missing `-At` refused as `invalid_report`, and no Card, report byte, or revision change from any projection.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/audit-baseline.tests.ps1` (#94 S01, 55 assertions) drives the reproducible baseline through `digest-audit.ps1` on a scratch archive with two books, a model with a Topic-filed part that has no source of its own, Topic Cards with one, two and no explicit source books, a hub line that crosses books, a hub that copies its anchor's title, a register with an accepted and an over-long row, and title pairs. It checks the evidence (identity, revision, software, thresholds, scope, the seed with its sampled addresses, the missing inputs), the source sets and the derived head, P1's own-source rests-on, P4's tails and its unknown run figures, P7's evidence-only crossing, P8's accepted terms and its two-link outbound reach, P9's heuristic rates, P11's two populations and anchor-title fault, the delta only where the scopes match, the refused scope change, the fixed task sheet and its two faults, the refused unknown sheet column, the refused baseline from another archive, and that the audit changes no Card.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/zt-helper-gate.tests.ps1`: `-SrcLog`, `-TopicGate` (with `-Open`), and two helpers at once, through the forwarder; without `-Open` the helper refuses `create_topic`, a Card in a Topic tree and a Link to a Topic-tree Card (`literature_only: …`) and still writes a Literature tree.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/lock-preflight.tests.ps1` (#137, S14) drives the Lock precheck on a scratch archive and its own service, with real owner Locks taken through the page-token `/api/lock` plan and confirmation: the audit, the model report and an unlocked plan still run; `prepare -Apply`, `skeleton -Step plan`, `terms` and its claimed `-Token` form, `event`, `finding`, `math`, `card`, `model brief`, `figures -Apply`, `prepare-link` and `ledger resume` each stop with the exact owner sentence, the typed `card_locked` JSON, exit 2 and no job file, work folder or Card; a job planned while the tree was free keeps its draft and writes nothing after the Lock; the `ZT_LOCK_TREE` seam commits the run's own first call, locks the tree before its reply and hands the next write to the archive's real refusal, with the earlier receipt, `-Log` line and Card retained; a refused identified chunk writes no Card; and an unlocked fourth book is still judged on its own destination while three trees are locked. `cargo test` covers the message, the refusal value, the Lock parse and the staging key; `calls.rs` covers the structured failure line and the resume line order.
- `scripts/book-digest/tests/grow.tests.ps1` (S15, #138; 78 checks) drives `zt-digest grow` on a scratch archive with real owner Locks taken through `/api/lock` and `/api/unlock`: the section of a Locked source book still plans, a Locked Topic is marked in `topics.md`, its Cards appear only in the read-only context block, and `titles.json` carries each Lock; a Locked `at`, a Locked `same`, an `existing` Locked Topic and a new Topic named like a Locked one are G12 while a Topic away from them passes; the run makes a new Topic and writes its Cards while the source book and another Topic stay Locked; a Topic locked after its answers stops the apply with exit 2 and the typed refusal (tree, Lock, step, address, no bypass in the hint), keeps the answers and the stopped job, writes nothing into the Locked tree, and is named by the report; unlocking the same folder writes the same three Cards and clears the stop. `cargo test --bin zt-digest grow` covers `lock_of`, the G12 messages, the refusal builder and the two closest blocks.

## Decisions and pitfalls

- **Same parameters, same output.** A tool keeps the PowerShell script's parameter names and output lines, so the workflows, the prompts, and the tests change only the program they start.
- **One process, one `zt mcp`.** The helper used to cost a pwsh and a `zt mcp` per batch (about 0.8 s); a tool now reuses one server for every call and check.
- **zt-check as a library.** The check runs on the tool's own `Zt`, and its rules stay in one crate.
- **Key order.** `serde_json` has `preserve_order`, so compact JSON keeps the order zt and the tools wrote, as `ConvertTo-Json` did.
- **PowerShell's order, not Rust's.** `src/ps.rs` copies what the scripts' files depended on: `Sort-Object` is .NET's introsort (equal keys come out in its order, not input order, above 16 items), strings sort by the zh-CN culture ignoring case (Han before Latin letters), `[Random]::new(seed)` is the .NET generator, and JSON is written as `ConvertTo-Json` writes it (two-space indent, CRLF, `1.0` for a whole double). On the card-compiler test flow and on a 635-Card archive, every file `audit` and `card` write equals the script's, byte for byte, except where PowerShell's order was random: the keys of an unordered `@{}` (`limits`, `bridges.out`, `bridges.in`) and ties in the relatedness ranking.
- **Audit inputs.** The audit uses bounded bulk reads in one MCP client. Legacy `-Throttle` and `-Chunk` values are parsed for wrapper compatibility; they do not create workers or override server limits. Read failures stop the audit rather than becoming null rows.
- **The forwarder must read stdin as UTF-8.** It sets `[Console]::InputEncoding`; without it a GBK console garbles the batch.
- **PowerShell semantics are kept on purpose.** The model tools compare text as `-eq` did (case and ignorable characters do not count), look up `@{}` keys without case, count lengths in UTF-16 units, treat `@($null)` as one element, and write doubles where `Measure-Object` gave doubles (`src/ps.rs`). State maps read back from `state.json` are case-sensitive, as `ConvertFrom-Json -AsHashtable` made them.
- **Ties and unordered keys.** Where the scripts' output depended on the enumeration order of an unordered `@{}` (randomised per pwsh process), the tools write insertion order, so their output is now stable: the keys of a new `state.json` of `event`/`finding`, `setup-calls.json` of `prepare`, Bridge plan and report objects, and the order of equal-count word groups in `terms`' job. Ties in the relatedness ranking (the keep-150 or keep-80 cut, equal scores) are broken by text order, as in `card`; the scripts broke them in hashtable order and differed from run to run. This changes a few "most related sections" lines in `prepare`'s group files, some candidate lines of `prepare-link`, and on one source the Bridge plan id. An Int32-keyed hashtable whose order the output showed (`event`'s refused list) is reproduced (`int_hashtable_order`).
- **Loops that never ended now fail.** `-UnitsPerJob` (math) or `-GroupsPerJob` (event, finding) of 0 or less with work to split fails with a message; the scripts looped for ever.
- **The scope is per process, set once in `main`.** A digest step that needs a Topic-tree write cannot get one: the client refuses it before the archive sees it, whatever the caller's allow-list says. A new tool is a digest step (Literature scope) unless it is added to `OPEN_TOOLS`; a hand-run `call` or `organize-apply` that must write a Topic tree passes `-Open`. Test fixtures that build Topic trees and hubs go through `zt-calls.ps1` (`Invoke-Zt` passes `-Open`) or pass `-Open` themselves.
- **A digest reads no Topic tree either.** Every digest catalogue comes from `snapshot::read_literature_trees`, so a Link from or to a Topic-tree Card is outside it: `card` and `link_gap` skip such Links instead of treating them as an incomplete read, and a Link from a Topic Card closes no gap of a Literature Card.
- **Error texts are not PowerShell's.** A PowerShell runtime error (a null hashtable index, a failed `[int]` cast) had a localized message; the tool writes an English one. Errors on stderr are `zt-digest model: <error>`, not a PowerShell error record.

- `2026-10-23` — 0.11.8: a figure of several panels is shown whole. `figures_from_text` records where each image first appears in the book (`Figure::position`); the attach step orders the figures by it and keeps one entry per label and image (`attach_order`), so a Card that names 图 1.1 gets every panel in the book's order, and a later panel of a figure the Card already shows goes right after the earlier one. The heading look-back has no block limit: the nearest heading above an image decides its label. ebrill2001modern's 图 1.1 is two maps; the step kept one image per label (the first by its hash file name) and the second map, more than eight blocks below its heading, got no label, so it was added by hand. Tests: `every_panel_of_a_figure_is_attached_in_source_order`, the far-panel case in `a_figure_without_a_caption_takes_the_figure_heading_above_it`, and a two-panel figure in attach-figures.tests.ps1 (the second panel's path sorts first).

The audit reads the figure labels inside an image alt (`alt_labels`: the attach format `图 2.C.1、2.C.2` and a writer's captioned alt `图 1.1：…`), so a figure shown with a caption is no longer "named but not shown". `ledger report` prints its facts with `usage.missing` and `cleanup.units` cut to eight entries and the rest counted (`missing_omitted`, `units_omitted`; `report_line`): the 138 KB line of ebrill2001modern was summarized by its relay and the cleanup stage reported no run report; run-report.json keeps every entry. - `2026-10-23` — 0.11.5 (finishing ebrill2001modern through the workflow): (1) the `notes` enumeration closes once the latest Organize publication is terminal and no note unit is open (`close_enumerations`); nothing closed it before and no digest run was ever `settled`, so none was content-complete. (2) `patch_compatible`: a tool reads a run prepared under an earlier patch of the same major.minor blueprint version (a 6.1.1 run under the 6.1.2 tools) and never a newer one; the exact match had left every fixed run readable only by the binary with the bugs. (3) `cleanup-finish -Ready` re-verifies a done cleanup unit whose result is a reconciliation and records it complete, with a `reconciled` event, once the facts show it resolved (`reverify_reconciliations`; the line carries `reconciled`). (4) The figures step reports by sha256 every figure a Card of the book already shows (`shown`, read with one `get_tree` with bodies) and every figure the digested `book.zh.md` never holds (`outside`); the Figures stage publication counts the first `shown` and the second `outside` (accepted with a reason, never a gap). (5) A stage publication's scope is its layer and the hash of its checked coverage (`stage_scope`): the same coverage reuses the revision, a changed one publishes a new revision that supersedes it; with no scope a Figures stage run again after its gaps were fixed reused the old revision and kept the gaps. On ebrill2001modern 图 12.1 sat in 45|d and both maps of 图 1.1 in 2|b, and the barcode lies after the end heading, yet all four were reported unmatched. Tests: cleanup-resources (whole-run settlement, reconciliation re-verified), figures and finalize unit tests, `a_patch_release_drives_a_run_of_an_earlier_patch_only`; 367 unit tests.

- `2026-10-22` — 0.9.3: `grow` counts a fault list as a count (`fault_count`, the register check prints lists), and `hubs-plan` seeds the box's field hubs into the hub state (`seed_fields`) so the field writer keeps them. Unit test `a_fault_list_counts_as_its_length`.

- `2026-10-22` — 0.9.2: `grow` gains the hubs round (`-Step hubs-plan`, `hubs-apply`, `hubs-check`, `hubs-report -Register apply|plan|off`). It runs `zt-digest hub` and `zt-digest register` as child processes of the same binary (`sibling`), keeps their long job lists out of relays, and wraps each hub job as a grow job (`gh-NNN`). The gh precheck sums the hub precheck of every file. The limits come from the hub survey (`hub_limits`). A typed `card_locked` refusal ends the step with exit 2.

- `2026-10-22` — (0.9.1) idea-grow 2.4.0: about seven lines per doorway. `validate` reports the new G13 for a second idea of one answer at the same Topic root. `apply` sends a root placement beside the doorway's last line once the doorway has `MAX_LINES` (7) lines (`line_parent`), and records `full` in the receipt. `report` counts `deeper` and lists those Cards in `grow-report.md`. The run-topics block of the section jobs asks for one root placement per Topic. Tests: the unit tests `a_section_places_one_idea_at_a_topic_root` and `a_full_doorway_files_the_next_line_beside_its_last_line`; `grow.tests.ps1` (G13, a doorway with seven lines).
- `2026-10-22` — (0.9.0) Tree Lock S17 (#140): every tool keeps the archive's typed Lock refusal across its own boundary. `lockcheck::stopped_exit(tool, error)` maps the `Stopped` refusal `calls::execute_identified` raises to the refusal's own JSON as the step's last stdout line, the archive's sentence on stderr and exit `2`; `main` consults it for every tool before the ordinary failure path, so a tool that does not handle the refusal itself (`card`, `model`, `terms`, `skeleton`, `event`, `finding`, `figures`, `bridge`, `finalize`, `concepts`, `restate`, `call`, …) no longer fails as exit 1 with the refusal buried in prose, and a Workflow reads `card_locked` without matching English text (R338). `hub`/`register`/`organize-apply` keep their own handlers. Unit test `a_typed_refusal_leaves_a_step_with_the_typed_json_and_exit_2`; no archive is touched. Released as zt-digest 0.9.0.
- `2026-10-22` — (0.8.0) Tree Lock S16 (#139): the Hub, Organize and register tools keep their plans inside Lock and pin limits. `lockcheck` gains the planner's own vocabulary — `Facts`/`facts_in` (a Card's `lock` and `pinned_by`), `lock_in`, `tree_id`/`inside`, `blocked`/`blocked_card`/`blocked_pin`/`blocked_destination` and the `Stopped` error with `stopped_refusal` — and `calls::execute_identified` returns a `card_locked` refusal as that typed error. `organize-apply` asks the archive for each move's plan (so the real Lock, pin, destination and Link-rewrite checks decide), checks a merge's emptied root and its `into` re-pointing before the first write, reports `blocked` decisions with the archive's own refusal, marks a decision that depends on a blocked one (`dependent`/`depends_on`), publishes a complete `preview` (mapping, `into.repointed_in`, `side_compaction`) with `-DryRun`, and stops a mid-run Lock with `applied.json.stopped`, the earlier receipts and exit 2. `organize-survey` adds `locks` and a `## 1b` mark. `hub` marks a Locked tree in its survey, allocates no job for it, skips its hub/field/register writes with typed `blocked` entries and `complete: false`, and stops a mid-run Lock with the typed JSON and exit 2; a pinned, unlocked Hub stays editable. `register` refuses a publication whose `索引` tree is Locked before its first write (`locked_destination`, exit 2). Tests: `organize-lock.tests.ps1`, `hub-lock.tests.ps1`, the seam's new `ZT_LOCK_AFTER_N`, and `tests/tree_lock.rs`'s pinned-Hub case (R336).
- `2026-10-22` — (0.7.0) Tree Lock S15 (#138): `grow` reads and never writes a Locked tree. `Ctx::lock_of` reads the Lock facts the plan's own `export_titles` already carries, so `titles.json` keeps each Card's `lock`; `topics.md` marks a Locked Topic and prints `locked`; the section file lists Locked Topic Cards apart as related context (`closest_blocks`); `validate`, `validate_fix`, `validate_topics` and the G8 near-name check report the new G12 for a Locked destination instead of substituting one. The archive's `card_locked` refusal stops the `apply` (or `topics`) stage: `archive_lock`/`locked_refusal` build the typed refusal (`code`, `tree`, `lock_id`, `mode`, `step`, `at`, plus the archive's `reason`/`cards`/`locks`), `stopped_fact` keeps the job's stop in `state.json`, and the stage exits 2 without retrying or moving the Card. `report` counts and names the stopped writes. Test: `grow.tests.ps1` with real owner Locks, plus `grow`'s new unit tests.
- `2026-10-22` — (0.6.0) Tree Lock S14 (#137): `lockcheck.rs` asks the archive, through the one `zt mcp` client, whether a writing mode's destination Literature tree is Locked, and stops before the first write and before any job file with `the Literature tree <key> is locked by the owner` (typed `card_locked` JSON, exit 2). `prepare -Apply`, `ledger resume`, `terms` (folder and claimed), `skeleton`, `event`, `finding`, `card`, `model`, `math`, `figures -Apply` and `prepare-link` ask for their actual destination only; every read-only step, an unrelated Locked tree and a `model`/`card` step without a book never ask. `calls::Failure` now keeps a typed refusal's JSON and prints it as one `<code> <compact JSON>` line before the `resume:` line, and a refused identified chunk carries the same refusal in its error. Test: `tests/lock-preflight.tests.ps1` (real owner Locks, the `ZT_LOCK_TREE` real-MCP seam, mid-run race) plus `lockcheck` and `calls` unit tests.
- `2026-10-21` — (0.5.3) A planned reference to another model's part (`<model>#<id>`) whose part `split` cut into pieces is met by a Link to any piece, read from that model's map in the compiler state (`compiler::reference_pieces`, used by the close step's `planned_link_entries` and by `model-finish`). On grossman2002integration trial 19 model-finish refused `3|a`: `3|a|a|4` linked a later piece of `2|a`'s split derivation, not `2|a|a|14`. Test `a_reference_to_another_models_split_part_takes_any_piece`. Also: a unit's frozen owned manifest (`frozen_owned`, read by the Link unit) is accepted when its leading whole lines still hash to the frozen value, because a card split appends the new Card to its group's manifest (`card::add_owned`); any other change is still refused (trial 19: the whole-book cycle split a Card of group 8 and `link-start` refused `owned-g08.txt` three times, so `link/g008@1` failed). Test `an_owned_manifest_extended_by_split_cards_keeps_its_frozen_prefix`. Also: `ledger resource` counts `-SweepEvidence` as one of its actions; it was left out of the count, so the cleanup stage's evidence sweep (added on trial 11) was always refused with "resource takes exactly one of …" (trial 19). Covered in `cleanup-resources.tests.ps1`.
- `2026-10-21` — (0.5.2) The close step's closure note names every word that reads as rests-on (依赖、依据、基于、建立在、取自、沿用、承接、用到、借用、代入、推导自) and asks the writer to leave none of them in the rewritten sentence. On grossman2002integration trial 17 close sent `6|a|a` back for three 沿用 sentences resting on whole other models; the writer turned 「时序沿用 [[2|a]]」 into 「信息沿用 [[2|a]]」, still a rests-on sentence, and model-finish refused `6|a` for its closure fault. Test `each_build_fault_becomes_a_note_for_its_own_card`.
- `2026-10-21` — (0.5.1) A planned relation to a split part is authored by a Link to any of its pieces. `split` cuts a Derivation part `D11` into `D11`, `D11~2`, `D11~3` and the last piece holds the result, so a later part that uses the result links the last piece. `model -Step close` (`planned_link_entries`) and `model-finish` both still demanded the Link to `D11` itself: on grossman2002integration trial 16 close sent `7|a|a|19` back for `7|a|a|16`, its writer kept the Link to `7|a|a|18`, and model-finish refused `7|a` and `8|a` (`8|a|a|16` → `8|a|a|15`). Both now share `compiler::split_pieces` (the id and every `id~k` piece; `D1` is no piece of `D11`); a close note for a split target names every piece. Test `a_link_to_any_piece_of_a_split_part_authors_its_planned_link`.
