Unit test `the_job_commands_name_the_tool_and_the_box`.
- `2026-10-20` — `grow` 2.0: `-Books`/`-Book` optional (default: every Literature tree of the box, `books_of`), `box_share` (the share of all books and per book), global section numbers with `book`, a tree with no anchor as one section, the closest notes of other books per section, `book_of`/`book_of_card`/`faults_by_book` for per-book checks, the report's bridges, `left` after 120 sections. Test: `grow.tests.ps1` (the whole box: three books, a tree with no anchor).
- `2026-10-20` — `grow` 1.3: `-Step links` (one gl-NNN job per Topic tree the run wrote in), `validate_links` (G11), the link answers in apply (`append_card`, `linked`), and the report's `linked` count. Test: `grow.tests.ps1` (the link round).
- `2026-10-20` — `grow` 1.1: `-Step topics` (the run Topics from gt-001 through the topic gate, then the section jobs), `validate_topics` and `max_new_topics`, the main-line append for a root-direct idea, and `link_kinds` (a port of `src/model.rs` `RELATION_KINDS`/`read_links`) for G3, G4, G6 and the new G10. Tests: 8 unit tests and `grow.tests.ps1`.
- `2026-10-20` — New tool `grow` (`src/grow.rs`): the idea-grow compiler of the zt-idea-grow workflow ([idea-grow-workflow](idea-grow-workflow.md), `docs/idea-grow-design.md`). Steps plan (sections, snapshot, titles, Topic catalogue, closest Topic Cards, baseline Topic share), precheck (G1–G9 and zt-check on the drafts), apply (topic gate, doorway, chains, Direct-to-Side when the slot is taken, `same`, run-level duplicates, fix edits with the stored text as `base`), check (zt-check `--cards` with the book on the run's Cards, fix jobs per section), report (`grow-report.json`/`.md`, the Topic share before and after). Tests: 6 unit tests and `scripts/book-digest/tests/grow.tests.ps1`.
- `2026-10-20` — `model -Step report` writes the full report to `<work>\report.json` and prints a brief line: per model `parts` (the count), `core` (the core parts) and `moved` (the count), plus `file`. The printed line is relayed by an agent; on grossman2002integration trial 12 a relay cut three part lists to a note. Test: `model-compiler.tests.ps1` (report line and file).

- `2026-10-20` — `card -Step plan` adds D1 (`model_part_duplicates`): an Idea Card whose advisory I6 fault names a Model part (category `model` with a facet) repeats that part's claim, so it becomes a fix target with the note to give it the angle the part lacks and link the part with `对应`, or to propose the merge. D1 exists only in the plan (zt-check never reports it), so it changes no before/after fault count. On grossman2002integration trial 11 `6|f` and the part `6|a|a|13` had the same title (with four near pairs), and the restatement review compares only the run's new Topic Cards, never an Idea Card with a Model part. `dsh-card-instructions.zh.md` says how to repair D1. Test `an_idea_card_near_a_model_part_is_a_duplicate`.

- `2026-10-20` — `skeleton -Step apply` measures each index line's first sentence with the group label it is written after (`第 N 组（chapter）：…`) and reports a problem when it is longer than R2 allows, so the skeleton writer shortens `says` (its precheck is `apply` without `-Apply`). On grossman2002integration trial 11 one index line came to 41 characters, an R2 fault in the Literature Card that the bounded correction may not reopen. Test `an_index_line_is_measured_with_its_group_label`.

- `2026-10-20` — `organize-apply` rewrites the staging manifests that name owned Card addresses through its moved map (`ledger::relocate_manifests`): each `owned-gNN.txt` line that is a moved address and the address before the tab of each `src-gNN.txt` line; `applied.json` lists the rewritten files under `manifests`. The card compiler reads every `owned-g*.txt` for the book's declared Cards, and on grossman2002integration trial 11 a Card Organize had moved kept its old address there, so every later card cycle stopped at "incomplete Card read". `ledger relocate` rewrites the same manifests too; it is not run on a digest, because relocating the recorded outputs after their units completed makes the checked completions disagree (`producer_outputs_mismatch`, and every publication built on them reads `required_unit_missing_or_changed`). New: `ledger resource -List -Counts` (numbers per workspace), `-List -Workspace <path>` (one workspace's ids) and `-SweepEvidence <answers>` (the ledger records the missing evidence itself). Tests `a_recorded_move_rewrites_the_owned_and_source_manifests`, `the_cleanup_listings_carry_no_id_list_through_a_relay`.

- `2026-10-20` — Two cleanup gaps of grossman2002integration trial 10, the first run whose cleanup acted. (1) `cleanup-finish` counts a resource's usage as retained when it holds a resource-wide reading, a per-run reading, or an explicit unavailable record (`usage_retained`); `-EvidenceFor` with `-Evidence` and no `-Usage` records the usage as unavailable ("the run's result stated no token usage", R345) unless the conversation already has a usage record (`conversation_usage`). No manager passed `-Usage`, nothing recorded the usage as unavailable, and cleanup-finish refused every digest cleanup unit as `cleanup_evidence_missing` although each conversation was deleted with evidence. Test `an_unavailable_usage_record_is_retained_usage`. (2) The agent-facing record commands (`ledger resource -RegisterId`, `-Settle`, `-SettleWorkspace`, `-EvidenceFor`) print one short line, their top-level facts and `recorded: true` (`record_brief`), never the resource views: one `-SettleWorkspace` batch printed about 260 KB (the shared hub workspace holds every hub run's conversations), and its cleanup agent reported all six workspaces unrecorded. Test `a_record_command_answers_one_short_line`. The short answer names the recorded conversation (or workspace) and leaves out the prepared run's `run_id`: on trial 11 a brief manager read `run_id` in it and reported that registration had stored the wrong id.

- `2026-10-20` — The model compiler writes each 结构 line's text through `structure_text`, which writes a semicolon as a comma: the line copies the plan's `use` (共用) or title, and on grossman2002integration trial 10 `8|a`'s 共用 line carried the architect's `；`, an R3 fault in a done Model Card the bounded correction may not reopen. Test `a_structure_line_writes_a_semicolon_as_a_comma`; the term-table test now expects a very long title named in full (zt-check counts 《…》 as one name).

- `2026-10-20` — `model -Step close` adds `planned_link_entries`: for each planned part the build wrote (not taken, not existing), every `rests` and `of` reference that resolves to a part of the model and is not a relation from that part in `get_model` goes back once, as a 修正 note, to the part's writer, the same rule `model-finish` applies (`does not author the Link to … its accepted plan requires`). On grossman2002integration trial 10 close reported no fault and model-finish refused `7|a` because `7|a|a|5` lacked its Link to `3|a|a|2`. Test `a_missing_planned_link_is_a_close_entry`.

- `2026-10-20` — `planned_in_batch` (model-deps) decides that a referenced model is planned in the same batch by its `plan.json` in the batch folder and its place in the batch order, not by a plan hash: the compiler records the plan hash only at the fill, after `model-deps` runs, so on grossman2002integration trial 10 every in-batch reference still waited (`3|a`, `4|a`, `7|a` behind `2|a`; `-Step defer` kept every write claimed and the models were built one batch after another). Test `a_reference_inside_the_batch_needs_no_ledger_wait` (with plan files and no plan hash).

- `2026-10-20` — `model -Step defer -Models "a,b"` (`step_defer`, `defer_models`): the models `model-deps` released to wait leave the batch's `order`, which every later step iterates, read `deferred`, and their `plan.json` is renamed `plan-deferred.json` so the batch that claims them again plans them afresh and `model_plan_dir` still finds one accepted plan. `model-deps` partitions a plan's `X#A` references: one to a model the same batch planned (`planned_in_batch`: in the batch order with a plan hash) is the compiler's to resolve and is answered as `in_batch`; only the others register a producer dependency and may wait. `card`'s `DEFAULT_CARD_RULES` adds R22. grossman2002integration trial 9: the first batch released three of four models to wait for `2|a` of the same batch, then the fill held jobs for all four. Tests `deferred_models_leave_the_batch_order`, `a_reference_inside_the_batch_needs_no_ledger_wait`.

- `2026-10-20` — `finalize -Step stage -Out <out> -Layer organize|skeleton|figures|bridge` completes a stage-owned book publication through the same verified boundary as a refresh (`complete_publication`): its declared artifacts (`-Decisions`/`-Applied`, `-Skeleton`, `-Report`) are bound by hash, and its coverage is derived unless `-Coverage` names it: Organize from the registered note outcomes, the skeleton from the group producers, Figures from the prepared figures file and the stage's figures report (`figures_coverage`: `shown` when a Card names the figure by its alt text or `图 ` + a label, else `unmatched` with its reason), Bridge with every proposal of the immutable report `open`. A layer the run excluded answers `excluded`; any other layer is refused. The answer counts the coverage per outcome so the line stays short. Before it, finalize left these revisions to their stages and no stage published them, so on grossman2002integration trial 8 `organize/book@1`, `skeleton/book@1`, `figures/book@1` and `bridge/book@1` stayed ready and no run could be content-complete. Tests `figures_coverage_classifies_every_recognized_figure` and the `stage` block of `finalize.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — `ledger resource -List -Brief` (`resource_brief`) answers the recorded conversations per DeepSeek workspace as ids only: every recorded id (`recorded`), the ones not deleted yet (`open`) and the ones without retained evidence (`bare`); a lost creation response is neither open nor bare. The cleanup stage acts on it: the full listing was about 52 KB for an eight-group paper (trial 8) and came back as prose. Test `a_brief_resource_list_groups_the_ids_by_workspace`.

- `2026-10-20` — `link-finish` asks a kinded Link of an `already_linked` Card only when a writer claimed it (`already_linked_needs_kind`): an owned Card the frozen gap set left out (reason `NO_GAP_REASON`) needs none, because the gap set asks a kinded semantic Link only of an Idea Card. On grossman2002integration trials 7 and 8 a group's Topic root (`363/0`, `366/0`) and its unbuilt Model Cards were never gaps, so no job could link them, yet the finish refused the group for them on every attempt. Test `only_a_claimed_already_linked_must_show_its_kinded_link`.

- `2026-10-20` — `link-finish` reads each Link target in a receipt-owned paragraph as the whole address inside `[[…]]` (`paragraph_targets`), and a note suggestion's target likewise: both cut the target at the first `|` as if it were an alias, but ZT addresses carry `|` and a Link has no alias part. On grossman2002integration trial 7 a paragraph `依赖 [[…/2|a|a|17]] 的均衡条件` was read as a Link to `…/2` and five of eight Link units were refused as carrying no kinded Link. Test `a_paragraph_target_keeps_the_whole_address`.

- `2026-10-20` — Found on grossman2002integration trial 7. `restate -Step prepare` creates the staging folder's own `restate` folder, where it freezes `evidence.json` (and later `judged.json`, `resolution.json`) whatever `-Work` names: the review module passes `<out>eviewestate`, and prepare failed with a missing path; `restate.tests.ps1` no longer pre-creates the folder and runs prepare with that `-Work`. `ledger gate -Brief` decides an empty layer's `complete` from `sealed`: a sealed, settled layer with no unit (the explain layer when every Derivation part already carries 思路 and 解读) is complete; an unsealed empty queue is not. `close` also sends back each part the source review records as `supplied` whose body carries no marker (`supplied_entries`; 8|a part D1 was refused). Tests `a_brief_gate_is_complete_when_its_own_layer_is`, `a_supplied_part_without_a_marker_becomes_a_note`.

- `2026-10-20` — Three repairs of the model compiler found on grossman2002integration trial 7. (1) `refresh_shared` rebuilds, in order, every 共用 line of each Model Card's 结构 list from its plan's taken parts and the current address map (`shared_lines`; a list whose 共用 lines do not match the taken parts one for one is left alone); `split` runs it after its splits, `close` before it reads the faults, `report` before it reports. A split in 2|a renumbered π_m from `2|a|a|12` to `2|a|a|13`, 3|a kept naming `2|a|a|12`, and `model-finish` refused 3|a ("mapped but belongs to no model", and closure faults on the steps that rest on π_m). (2) `close` records each Model Card's 结构 list (`snapshot_structures`, `structure_close` in the state) and `report` puts it back when a writer changed it (`restore_structures`): a close follow-up continues the fill conversation, whose writer can re-send the whole Model Card from its older copy. (3) `close` also sends back each Card whose supplied-step marker is malformed (`marker_entries`: a marker is exactly （补：书中未写） or （补：书中略去）; 2|a was refused for 「（补：书中略去：两式相除）」). `close` prints `refreshed` too. Tests `a_malformed_marker_becomes_a_note_for_its_card`, `shared_lines_follow_the_taken_parts` and the new `close:`/`report:` checks of `model-compiler.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — `zt-digest model -Step close` (`step_close`, `close_entries`, `head_job`, `follow_jobs`, `add_follow`): for each model of the batch, every build fault `get_model` still reports (closure, forward, reach-in, unanchored) becomes one 修正 note for the Card it sits in, sent as a follow-up that continues the conversation that wrote that Card (the Model Card's own fill job for the Model Card, the part's job for a part); the note quotes the sentence and says how to reword it (an outside link starts with 参照/对应/对照/例示; a sentence about a later part puts that part's link first, "[[later]] 用到本卡的……"; an unanchored step rests on an earlier part). It prints `{jobs, faults, unowned}`; a fault in a Card no job of the batch wrote is listed under `unowned`. `step_split` shares the follow-up writer. The Model Card job now says each kept outside link starts with 参照/对应/对照/例示 and never 依赖/基于. On grossman2002integration trial 6, `model-finish` refused all four models for these faults. Tests `each_build_fault_becomes_a_note_for_its_own_card` and the `close:` checks of `model-compiler.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — `ledger hold` (`hold`, `hold_targets`): one bounded command that keeps a list of claims current for up to 540 seconds and ends once none is held, so a layer driver can hold a long block's claims beside its work (grossman2002integration trial 5: four model claims expired while the model compiler was still building them). Test `a_hold_names_each_claim_by_unit_and_token` and the `ledger hold` checks in `ledger-claims.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — Found on grossman2002integration trial 4. `ledger next -Brief` (`brief_units`, `brief_inputs`) keeps of each claimed unit's inputs only its Model address (`header`, `model`), and `ledger status -Held` lists the same brief inputs: a relay dropped the full inputs of four model units. `ledger status -Units` also returns each unit's `input_hash`, which a fault must carry. `ledger gate -Brief` decides `complete` from the gated layer alone (settled, every unit done or no-op, none of its own unresolved units or coverage faults; an empty layer keeps the run-wide decision): the run-wide `complete` also waits on later layers and held every layer gate open (events 8/8 done reported active). Tests `a_brief_gate_is_complete_when_its_own_layer_is`, `a_brief_claim_drops_the_unit_inputs`, `a_held_view_lists_only_the_owners_current_claims`, `a_unit_view_is_the_state_of_the_named_units_only`.

- `2026-10-20` — `ledger status -Held "<owner>"` (`held_view`): the units one claim owner holds (state `claimed`), each with id, owner, token, lease, input hash and inputs, the same entries a claim reply lists. A layer driver whose relayed claim line lost its units array reads them here (grossman2002integration trial 3). Test `a_held_view_lists_only_the_owners_current_claims`.

- `2026-10-20` — Three gate fixes found on grossman2002integration trial 2. (1) The preparation registration check (`preparation_registration`, `registration_deps_ok`) accepts on `cards-book/book@1` and `link-check/book@1` exactly the child prerequisites a digest group's completion registers (#69/#70: `cards/gNNN@1`, `link/gNNN@1`, condition `settled`, units created by a digest unit); before, every real run reported `preparation_registration_mismatch` and could never be content-complete. (2) A gate's `complete` keeps its run-wide meaning (the layer's own completion is `settled` with nothing open, failed or blocked; `cards-finish`, `model-finish`, `explain-finish` and `ledger-group-finish` tests pin this). (3) `ledger gate -Brief` (`gate_brief`) replies with the identity, `sealed`/`settled`/`complete`, the counts without the per-layer table, and the layer's own unresolved units and coverage faults (`layer_faults`): the full status was about 49 KB on an eight-group paper and the relay summarized it. Tests `the_registration_check_accepts_the_child_prerequisites_of_book_units`, `a_brief_gate_lists_only_its_own_layers_coverage_faults`, `a_brief_gate_keeps_the_layer_decision_only`.

- `2026-10-20` — Event and Finding jobs are named by the groups they cover (`event/layer.rs`, `job_id`: `fd-g001`, `fd-g002-g005`, `ev-g012-g013`) instead of their position in one plan (`fd-01`). The step's `state.json` lives across claims, so on grossman2002integration trial 1 the second claim's plan (groups 2–5) took the names `fd-01`–`fd-03` again, read them as already applied, printed no job while declaring `expected_jobs: 3`, and `findings-finish` recorded the units done with no Finding written; a reused name would also have let a new answer file replace an earlier claim's answer. A re-plan of the same groups still recognizes its own applied job. Test `a_job_is_named_by_the_groups_it_covers`.

- `2026-10-20` — `ledger status -Units <comma-separated ids>` (`unit_view`): the named units' state, token, attempts, fault class and result disposition, in the order named (`unknown` for an id the ledger does not hold), so a block driver decides each unit from the ledger. `ledger next -Brief` (`brief_units`) drops the claimed units' frozen inputs from the claim reply. Tests `a_unit_view_is_the_state_of_the_named_units_only` and `a_brief_claim_drops_the_unit_inputs`.

- `2026-10-20` — Plain-argument resource records (`resource_register_id`, `resource_evidence_for`, `resource_settle`, `resource_settle_workspace`; `-Register`/`-Observe` now share `resource_register_input`/`resource_observe_input`): the manager that ran a DeepSeek job records its id, its answer file and its usage itself, and the cleanup agent records its deletions and workspace listings itself, so no relay writes a JSON record that restates another agent's observation. `ledger next` replies with `claim_reply` (identity, layer state, counts, claimed units) instead of the full status. Tests `a_claim_reply_carries_the_units_and_the_scheduling_state_only` and `resource-register-id.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — `ledger reclaim -Release` (`reclaim`, `quiet_claim`, `last_activity_ms`): a stopped run's claims are released before their lease ends when nothing happened to them for the quiet window (a held claim records activity at least every 30 s, or with every manager wait round). The guard, the live-run check and the token fencing are the same as an ordinary reclaim; the `reclaimed` event and result say `released`. On stiglitz2014learning a stopped run held 8 group claims with 22 minutes of lease left, and `ledger resume` refuses while any claim is live. Tests `a_release_takes_only_a_quiet_claim` and the `-Release` checks in `ledger-claims.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-20` — `concepts -Step propose` names only each row's own term (`proposals_from_rows`). It also named the row's words not to use, which `prepare` refuses because the table does not define them (stiglitz2014learning: `学习型社会` named its avoided word `学习社会`); the matched row still carries those words into the review. Test `the_proposals_are_the_books_own_term_rows`.

- `2026-10-20` — The term table Card names its book within the Card limits (`terms.rs`, `term_title`, `book_names`): the full title, else the main title before a colon, else 本书, in both the title (R12) and the first sentence (R2), measured by zt-check's own `title_fits`/`sentence_fits`. The full English title of stiglitz2014learning (86 characters) made the Terms apply refuse its own Card. Test `the_term_table_names_its_book_within_the_card_limits`.

- `2026-10-19` — `ledger status -Summary` (`status_summary`): the run's identity, settings, counts and completion flags, with the units replaced by their number per layer and state (`layers.<layer>.{total,<state>}`). The full status of stiglitz2014learning (171 groups) is about 450 KB; the summary is about 3 KB, small enough for a workflow to read through one relayed output line. Test `a_status_summary_counts_the_units_per_layer`.

- `2026-10-19` — Keep the claim's provider-run lease through the start steps (`ledger.rs`, `start_lease`/`held_lease`): `terms -Step plan`, `group-start` and the Link unit start reset `lease_until` to now + 120 s, cutting the 1500-second lease `ledger next -Lease` had granted, so a Terms job of about three minutes lost its claim before `apply` (`stale_claim` on stiglitz2014learning) and every digest group stayed waiting on the Terms unit. Regression test `a_start_step_keeps_the_longer_claim_lease`.

- `2026-10-20` — #122 S29 (0.5.0): `resolve`, the separately selected historical resolution batch (design §15-phase-6; D377, D378; S29-AC01–AC07). There is no archive-wide migration: the reports `topic-home` and `join` already produced name the old restatements, misfiled Cards and Topic pairs, and this gate resolves only the exact items a person selects out of them. `plan` writes one `rb-` batch: every offered report item with its report/finding/item or plan id, the exact texts or operations the qualified path would write, the current address and authored fingerprint of every Card it touches beside the reviewed one (`stale` names what moved), the archive identity and revision, the explicit earlier-carrier treatment, and — once anything is selected — the approved backup with its `sha256`, bytes and the verification that it is this archive's own readable SQLite backup holding Cards. The bodies it read are retained under `resolve-endpoints\`. `-File` names the selection: a note plus the `topic_home` and `join` blocks in exactly those two steps' own selection shapes, so their own validators decide every item and nothing they would refuse can enter a batch; an item the report did not offer is `ineligible` and never selectable. Silence is never permission: no selection defers the batch (`no-selection`), a selection that accepts nothing declines it (`declined`), and every eligible item keeps its visible `deferred` disposition. `apply` refuses a deferred batch, re-verifies the backup and the protected human/Model scope, and executes the accepted items one at a time through the qualified paths — the address-preserving transfers first, the reviewed moves after them — under a receipt prefix derived from the batch id, so a retry of the same batch replays its receipts instead of writing twice. Each item keeps the qualified path's own verification as its evidence and ends `applied-and-verified`, `unchanged`, `conflict`, `refused` or `pending`; `complete` requires every item verified, `done` follows D378 (every item verified or explicitly unresolved, protected content unchanged), and the result says it fixed no other historical fault. `check` re-reads the archive — applied addresses, statement fingerprints, the old Card's entry and its Link, the moved Cards' authored text, the model reports, the protected digest and the Broken-link count — names any fault and repairs nothing. New coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/resolve.tests.ps1` (93 assertions) and 9 module unit tests.

- `2026-10-19` — #117 S26: the one-book pilot gate. `pilot` has three read-only steps over one prepared run and writes only under `-Work`. `plan` (S26-AC01) verifies and records the source, Citation key, staging, groups, archive identity and revision, the three installed binaries with their SHA-256 and own version (the named `zt-digest` must be this process), the installed frozen Workflow's version and script hash, the caps equal to the prepared settings, the baseline report with its address list, the verified backup, the preserved builds, the earlier run, the approved new-Card statement and the authored fingerprint of every old Card (`pilot-protected.json`), refusing to write a plan that misses any of them; the prepared root and anchors are named as the run's own placeholders. `preflight` (S26-AC02) refuses a spent or moved run, a live foreign claim, a registered Link unit without its group's `digest` **success** prerequisite, an installed binary, frozen script, backup or preserved build that changed, another archive or a moved revision, an edited plan and a changed protected scope, and writes its record with `ok: false` before exiting 1. `qualify` (S26-AC03–AC08) reads only what the run retained — the verified run report, every Card of the run's scope with its group, parent, earlier-filing flag and review outcome, the group's Link completion and quality, the audit's P1–P4 measures and the pilot book's language/layer/orphan counts, the ledger's interruption events and recovered starts, and the old-data proposals — states an unread measure as unknown, sets `verdict` from its own checks, and always records `decision: pending-human` with a representative sample. New coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/pilot.tests.ps1` (53 assertions) and 9 module unit tests. The runbook is `docs/luhmann-comparison-pilot.md`.

- `2026-10-19` — #115 S23: the delivered vertical paths join the integrated digest run. `concepts` gains `-Step propose`, which freezes the run's proposed concepts from its own book term table (`<Staging>\terms.md`, one proposal per row: the book's preferred word as the title, its own meaning as the question, the accepted and rejected spellings as `terms`), collapses a repeated term, bounds the set at 20 with an `omitted` count and, when the run has no term row, prints `proposals: 0` with its reason and writes no file — so no agent invents a concept and a review over an empty set is never reported as cleared. `restate -Step prepare` gains the run's own population: without `-Cards` it reads the staged `owned-g*.txt` manifests the Card cycle enumerates, narrows them to reviewable agent Topic Idea claims from the title catalogue, bounds the selection at `MAX_NEW` in address order and records `population {owned, reviewable, selected, omitted, skipped, skipped_total}` in the evidence; a caller's own `-Cards` list keeps the delivered bound and refusal, and the evidence keeps its old payload in that mode, so a resumed caller's `evidence_id` does not change. Both are covered by module unit tests and by the S23 harness scenarios.

- `2026-10-19` — #113 S21: `topic-home` resolves an approved Topic-home transfer while preserving old addresses (design §11.1, §11.3, §12.3; D262–D274, D301, D305). The Topic-to-Literature `move` refusal stays policy and is never worked around: this path moves no Card. `plan` consumes one reviewed concept report (its own `r-` content id is recomputed), takes its judged `topic-home` findings, reads each Topic's Direct-successor entries and their own-book source Links, and proposes one item per book-bound entry: a plain Literature Idea Card beside a plain anchor with the exact reviewed title and body (source lines included, so the statement fingerprint, every formula and every source Link is checkable), and the old Card's edit as §4.5's explicit `## 同述入口` entry or as an authored distinct interpretation the selection must supply. Every item binds the concept report and finding ids, the archive identity, the entry's, anchor's and book's authored fingerprints, the predicted Side slot, the model context and the treatment of an earlier carrier — a Card of the target tree that already carries the statement is `reuse`d and never copied. Agent writability and the model boundary come first: a Model Card or Model part is `blocked` on a named model decision and no Model part is created. The review is immutable (`th-` content id), reports its bounds, an unread endpoint and a bounded carrier scan as `partial`, and writes no Card. `apply` binds the exact recomputed report id, the offered item, the remedy, the supplied draft (distinct only) and a note, refuses another archive or a blocked item, and needs `-OperationPrefix`, whose stable receipt keys (`create-t<id>`, `rewrite-t<id>`) make a retry replay instead of writing twice. Each item is two identified writes, never one atomic move: the statement first (a lost reply is read back from the anchor) and read back verified; then the old Card with the reviewed authored fingerprint as its base guard, which a changed old Card turns into a `conflict` that preserves the created statement and produces a revised review item. Afterwards it reads back the persisted text, the Links and their actual Relation kinds, the old address's inbound count, the Broken-link count, one substantive home and the `get_model` reports, and records `applied-and-verified`/`unchanged`/`conflict`/`refused`/`pending` with per-item reasons and rollback evidence; an archive change between two reads is a pending quiet window. Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/topic-home.tests.ps1` (77 assertions, including the `seam-zt-mcp` `ZT_RACE_AFTER_CREATE` knob and a lost-reply resume) and the module's 9 unit tests. Released as zt-digest 0.3.0.

- `2026-10-19` — #119 S27 (0.4.2): the review shows each target Card's own text (the first 200 characters and its size) beside the row that names it, and the hub-state publication is an overlay — publishing one plan keeps every tree, field, job and counter it does not name. `publish.tests.ps1` reaches 110 assertions.
- `2026-10-19` — #119 S27 (0.4.1): the gate now binds the exact register plan it reviewed (a plan re-staged since the review makes every accepted row a conflict, never an unreviewed write), refuses at `-Step plan` a staged hub plan whose reviewed authored base has moved, and shows each declared register part's authored fingerprint beside its old body. `publish.tests.ps1` grows to 108 assertions with the stale-plan refusal, the re-staged-plan conflict and the parts review.
- `2026-10-19` — #119 S27: `publish` reviews and publishes the exact existing-register and crossing-hub changes (design §8.5, §11.3, §12.3, §15-phase-3; D191, D371, D372; S27-AC01–AC07). `plan` writes an `xp-` immutable review of the accepted register plan and hub plan: every term with its exact row, targets (Workspace, Zone, type, facet, substantiveness), source rows, sense evidence and current row; the pinyin bands with their readings and the unresolved terms/readings; every part's old and planned body; every staged hub change with its old body and authored fingerprint, the hub it reuses, its crossings and its policy evidence (mirror, one-book, anchor title); the protected human/Model scope; and the policy checks. `apply` binds the review id, archive id, note, verified backup and reviewed readings, records every item it does not name as declined, publishes the accepted hub changes first under `publish/<review>/1/hubNNNN` receipts with their `@@N` references resolved and the state advanced, then re-stages the accepted register rows (`register::restage`) and publishes the parts and the root through `Register::publish_plan`, verifying each body and entry by fresh reads; a changed protected Card stops at a quiet window, a stale base is a conflict with a revised plan, a declined row the register already serves publishes nothing, a retry recognises what its own result already published, and every item ends applied/declined/conflict/pending/refused. `check` reports the post-publication revision and the accepted scope for reader qualification. `hub -Step stage` records the same changes without a write; `register` excludes a term label that holds `：` instead of publishing an unreadable row. Released as zt-digest 0.4.0.
- `2026-10-19` — #112 S20: `bridge -Step project -Report <proposals.json> -At <address>`, the read-only nearby projection of one selected immutable report (design §10.3, D258–D260, D284, D293, D309). It reuses the report id check (`report::validate_published`) and the apply path's endpoint comparison (`changed_endpoint` over `projection_context`), names the exact report/proposal/relation/section and the requested Card's side, bounds its answer to 20 candidates with `counts` and `bounds`, and distinguishes `done`, `stale`, `not_in_report`, and the `invalid_report` refusal. It writes no Card and publishes no report. The first real-archive run (a 10.9 MB report from the Stiglitz run, 57 proposals) found that an apply's whole-tree read can exceed the MCP reply bound on a big book tree, so the projection reads only the tree list and each endpoint's own Card; it then reported the report's edited carriers `stale` and its unchanged ones `done`. New coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/bridge-projection.tests.ps1` (24 assertions) and the `changed_endpoint` unit test.
- `2026-10-19` — #109 S22: `join` reviews existing Topic pairs and applies one approved join with fresh mappings (design §7.3, §11.2, §11.3, §12.3; D155–D159, D269–D274, D301, D305). `plan` compares every agent Topic pair with the concept review's cheap comparison, keeps the strongest pairs, and for each lists the full meanings, the source mix, the evidence-based keeper (a tie is `unresolved`; the Card count is never the reason), the best joining Card and the concrete alternatives: keep both, keep both with a crossing through an existing Hub level, or move one identified agent subtree. Each move plan is one real `move_card` preview of a movable entry chain (a Topic root is never an operation), carrying every mapped address, the Link rewrite count and the finished-model effect; a human Link rewrite, a model boundary or an over-bound plan is recorded refused beside its nonmoving alternative. The review is immutable (`tj-` content id) and `partial` over a missed read. `apply` binds the report id, finding and plan ids, fingerprints and notes, refuses another archive or a refused plan, then applies one move at a time: it re-reads the moving Cards, re-derives the destination, compares the fresh plan with the reviewed one, checks the model boundary, confirms, reads the mapping and every persisted address back, verifies the authored text, Relation kinds, Broken links and model reports, refreshes the pending addresses, and records `applied-and-verified`/`unchanged`/`conflict`/`refused`/`pending` with rollback evidence; a revision change in the interval is a pending quiet window. Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/topic-join.tests.ps1` (79 assertions) and the module's unit tests. Released as zt-digest 0.2.0.
- `2026-10-19` — #107 S08: `restate` resolves a new restatement without rewriting a finished model (design §4.5, §5.4; D083-D087, D109-D116). `prepare` compares each new Topic Idea Card with its own book's anchors and Model Cards/parts and with earlier Topic Idea Cards on a bounded read set, carrying the TF-IDF relatedness score, the normalized title similarity, exact shared-formula evidence and the archive's exact statement fingerprint, and shortlists at most ten pairs per Card; `judge` accepts one `same`/`different`/`uncertain` verdict per pair, and a `same` verdict carries one accepted rewrite of the new Card (a distinct implication, an explicit `## 同述入口` entry whose one Link is the candidate's terminal substantive Card, or an explicit pending existing-Card change) that keeps the source evidence; `apply` writes only the listed new Cards through the identified receipt path with their frozen authored base, reads every output back, checks it, confirms the watched existing statements keep their authored fingerprints, and records `resolved-by-rewrite`, `pending-existing-card-change`, `recommended` or `unverified` per finding, with a Card-cycle merge verdict staying pending until a rewrite covers it and never counted as an applied merge; `report` renders the statuses with one pending and one verified example and a content id. The `card` report adds the resolution's counts as `restate` when the staging folder holds it, and the audit's I6 title context now carries each title's `restatement` role so an entry is no duplicate-title candidate. Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/restate.tests.ps1` and the module's unit tests.\n- `2026-10-19` — #105 S12: the `hub` stage crosses books and stops writing a hub that only repeats the tree map (design §8.1, §8.2; D160-D169, D340; S12-AC01–AC08). `precheck` judges a draft's `topic.entries` (one to three Cards of the tree, each with its reason; `oversize` above three, `entry` for a Card outside the tree, the tree root, a Hub Card, a repeated Card, a tree that already has a topic hub, or a tree that needs a line hub, `bare` with no reason) and refuses a topic hub whose lines are its own tree in address order (`mirror`), telling the writer to write `entries` instead; `mirror` uses the same seguito order as `get_hub` (`address_order`, a deliberate copy of `model::address_order`). `apply` writes the accepted section into the Topic Card's `## 入口` group (`text::replace_entries`, replacing any existing one, keeping every other group) and creates no topic hub; an existing topic hub is reported, never deleted. `check` reports the `one_book` advice `get_hub` returns as its own list (never a fix job, so no relation is demanded) and `entry_faults` for a section that broke or disappeared; `report` adds `entries`, `entries_valid`, `entries_invalid`; `plan` skips a tree whose accepted section already satisfies the entry requirement and its job file teaches the `entries` alternative and the `one-book` advice. The hub instructions (`dsh-hub-instructions.zh.md`) state both. Tests: a `precheck` unit test for the mirror rule (`a_topic_hub_in_address_order_is_a_mirror`) and 20 new assertions in `hub-compiler.tests.ps1`.
- `2026-10-19` — #104 S11: `register` publishes stable register parts with reviewed pinyin grouping and replay (design §8.4, §8.5; D176-D191, D276, D290, D298, D306, D339; AC01-AC09). `plan` gains `-Readings <file>` (default `out\register-readings.json`, schema `{"schema_version":1,"readings":{"学习":{"reading":"xué xí","initial":"x"}}}`): it validates each reading's shape (Latin letters, tone marks, digits, spaces, apostrophes; one letter or digit as the initial), records the accepted ones, and keeps a missing, `null`-initial, or `uncertain` reading in `其他` and in the unresolved-reading report. It never transliterates: `ps::culture_cmp` is not pinyin and stays out of this. A register over 120 rows, or one already split, is staged as `词条分册` parts grouped by the accepted initials (`A–F`, `G–L`, `M–R`, `S–Z`, then `0–9`, then `其他`), ordered by the base letters of the accepted reading and split contiguously at 120 rows. The plan carries each part's exact body, the root body with one `{{part:N}}` per new part, the identified calls of the parts batch, and the root call; `apply` publishes and verifies the parts first and the root last, replays receipts instead of duplicating a created part (`calls::invoke_identified_staged`), reuses addresses by label then position, keeps an unneeded part declared and empty, and marks the publication complete only after a fresh exact read of the root, parts, labels, addresses, targets, duplicates, and revision. A register whose declared parts are moved, missing, a Hub, listed twice, nested, or unlisted is refused before any write, and so is a plan that keeps more than 120 rows on the root. `check` reports the published layout and readings beside the missing/diverged/extra terms. Tests: 8 new module tests, `register-parts.tests.ps1` (30 assertions), the rewritten `register-paging.tests.ps1` (14 assertions, nine parts), and the readings cases in `register-compiler.tests.ps1` (38 assertions).
- `2026-10-19` — Re-verify the concept register (#101, S10; §4.1, §8.3-8.5, D058-D062, D170-D175, D276, D289, D296, D297, D299; AC01–AC09 and the Verification cases). Two defects were found and fixed, and the missing explicit human-target selection was added. **(1) The whole catalog, not one page.** `apply` used to read only the first `get_register` page (200 entries) and then compare it with the staged plan, so a register with more than 200 terms was published and immediately reported as not verifying; `check` reported every term beyond the first page as missing. Both now follow `entry_next_offset` at `entry_limit` 1,000 until null, require one revision, one register, contiguous offsets and entries that add up to `entry_total`, and retry once on a moving revision (`Merge::Changing` → one changing-view fault). **(2) S11's parts are refused before any write.** A register that already declares `词条分册` parts made `apply` publish and then fail its own verification over the parts' rows; `plan` and `apply` now refuse with a message naming S11 (#104), leaving the accepted plan file and the register body untouched. **(3) A reviewed human Card is a read-only target (D174, AC03).** `plan` reads the human Workspace's titles too; a `same` finding whose `entry` is a human Card is a tier-1 target listed in the term's `read_only_targets`; an entry in neither Workspace is `excluded` as `reviewed_entry_missing`. `apply` accepts a planned target in either Workspace, reports `read_only`, and never writes the human Workspace; the register only names the Card. New coverage: `register-human-target.tests.ps1` (19 assertions) and `register-paging.tests.ps1` (15 assertions) on scratch archives; the module's unit tests add the read-only selection, the missing reviewed entry, and the page merge (one catalog, a crossed revision, non-contiguous or short pages). Verified: `cargo test` in `tools/zt-digest` (190 passed), `register-compiler.tests.ps1` (30 assertions, unchanged and passing).

- `2026-10-19` — Re-verify S06 (#103, reverification of D097–D108, D304; AC01–AC08) on the current main. Two defects kept the slice from reaching the live workflow. (1) `group-finish` never checked the place-beside list it had published: a draft could make the listed earlier Card's continuation `direct` and the command accepted it, creating `0/2` in the earlier topic tree — a Direct successor the writer's helper would have refused. The command now reads the job's `artifacts.place_beside` before its preview and refuses a `create_successor` at a listed Card other than the group anchor unless `kind: side`; a unit that is already done reads nothing and keeps its retained accepted plan. (2) The econ and math workflows build their prompt from `dsh-digest-base.zh.md`, not `dsh-instructions.zh.md`, and the base still carried the old fixed Link quota (`父卡以外`, `至少一半`) with none of the filing decision or the `-Beneath` rule. The base now states the five-step filing order, the local statement rule, the first-sentence relation, the `-Beneath` call, the inline-Link rule and source/formula fidelity. `standalone-placement.tests.ps1` gained the assembled-prompt assertions and the group-draft refusal (22 → 28 assertions), each red before its fix.

- `2026-10-19` — Re-verify S05 (#98, reverification of D088–D096, D286, AC01–AC08) on the tree that carries #99–#106. The pack code needed no change: the four-group scratch fixture now drives every case the issue's Verification names through the real `group-start` — more than twenty candidates (a twenty-Card pack with `below_selection`), sparse matches (a photosynthesis group with six candidates, `enough: false` and no padded Card), truncated excerpts (a 1 441-character body cut to 800 with its locator), Model heads and parts (a Model part marked `model·假设`), human targets (a human Topic and Idea as read-only references), an accepted register entry whose own target leads the next pack, a duplicated register reported as a fault while the pack still builds, a reply-limit refusal that halves the read, changing revisions (retried once, then one `read_view_changed` fault), a refused start when the read cannot deliver an earlier Card, and resumed startup with the frozen pack byte for byte. The MCP trace pins one title catalogue, one register read and one bounded body batch with no read above 200 addresses, and the test also reads the writer instructions' read-the-whole-candidate rule. 44 assertions; the module unit tests are unchanged.

- `2026-10-19` — Re-verify S04 (#97, reverification of D076–D082, D302) on the tree that carries #98–#106. `concepts report` now takes `completeness.unread` from the frozen evidence's own unread record as well as from the judged pairs, so a Card whose read the archive answered as missing before it could become a candidate keeps the report `partial` and is named in `reasons` and `unresolved`; before this, such a report could publish `complete` with `unread: []`. `unread` is sorted and deduplicated before `unresolved` is written, so the partial report is deterministic. Public coverage: `concepts.tests.ps1` gained the hidden Topic root that never becomes a candidate (every shortlisted pair judged, the review still `partial` and naming `13/0`) and shares one canned-answer builder between its two evidence sets; the module's unit tests gained `a_card_the_review_could_not_read_keeps_the_report_partial` and `a_long_endpoint_body_is_retained_within_its_bound`, which pins the retained endpoint body at `ENDPOINT_BODY` plus the ellipsis.

- `2026-10-19` — File a standalone claim beside earlier thought (#103, S06; design §5.2, §5.3; D097–D108, D304). `group-start` now publishes `<claim>/place-beside.txt` — the earlier-context pack's agent Regular Cards in Topic trees, each read in full, never a Topic root or a Literature Card — in the job inputs and artifacts, and names it in the prompt. `zt-digest call -Beneath <file>` then permits one `create_successor` with `kind: side` beneath those Cards and nothing else: a Direct successor, an edit there, an unlisted Card and a human parent stay refused, the earlier Card keeps its authored body (only its generated Reverse section grows), and the group owns the new Card by its receipt through `group-finish`. The writer prompt gains the ordered filing decision, the local statement rule, the first-sentence relation and the inline-Link rule with no quota or appended association paragraph; the Card-review prompt gains the semantic rule for unlinked book-bound claims and refuses a kind-word substitution. Public coverage: `standalone-placement.tests.ps1`.

- `2026-10-19` — Reverify the concept-meaning Topic gate (#100, S07; D141–D154, D302, D337; AC01–AC08). The gate now takes the run's **own** Topic gate file `<Staging>\topic-gate.lock` (`gate::run_gate`) instead of a second `<Staging>\gate\topic-gate.lock` beside it, so a managed group writer (`-TopicGate`, `ledger` group-apply) and this gate actually serialize; `gate-report.md` now carries the missing or ambiguous concept inputs, which only the JSON did before; and `complete` is real (D141, AC02): an absent register or term table is a fact and stays complete, while an unreadable term table (`failed`, which `read_terms` now distinguishes from `absent`) and two registers (`ambiguous`) leave `complete: false`, set `outcome: incomplete` and make `create` refuse `incomplete_evidence` (exit 3) with the missing inputs, so no new Topic is cleared from evidence the gate could not read (D302). New public coverage in `gate.tests.ps1` (33 → 69 assertions): a creation waits for the run gate while another writer holds it and writes nothing until released; two concurrent managed writers leave exactly one Topic and the regenerated decision names it; a changed-payload replay is refused by its operation key; the ten-Topic bound is exercised with twelve term-named Topics; a third learning sense is kept apart and created after an unrelated Card edit while the fingerprint holds; a bare archive reports an absent register and term table, an unreadable term table reports `failed`, and two registers report `ambiguous` as `incomplete` in both report files; the TF-IDF order is proven to reach a Topic naming no term row; a repeated JSON key in the answer is refused; and the helper replays its committed Topic and refuses a title the decision did not clear. A new unit test pins the competing raw-creation report and the unreadable term table. Verified: `cargo test` in `tools/zt-digest` (185 passed).

- `2026-10-19` — Add the concept-meaning Topic gate (#100, S07; design §7.1, §7.2; D139–D154, D302, D337). `zt-digest gate` has four steps: `prepare` freezes the shortlist evidence (fast exact/near title check, TF-IDF over titles and questions with a score floor, term- and register-named candidates, representative reads, labelled definitions), `decide` validates the judge's `same`/`different`/`uncertain` answer against that evidence, `create` resolves a committed receipt before any new decision, takes the archive-shared Topic gate lock, revalidates the concept-input fingerprint, refuses a stale decision (exit 3) or an exact-title Topic and writes through the identified path while holding the lock, and `report` renders the frozen outcome. `calls` accepts `-ConceptGate <decision.json>` beside `-OperationPrefix` (and refuses it anywhere else), so a managed Topic creation is receipt-owned, and the legacy `-TopicGate` options stay refused there. `Relatedness` gains `rank_text_scored` so the shortlist can keep only a meaningful match. Public coverage: `gate.tests.ps1` and the module's unit tests.

- `2026-10-19` — Publish the bounded earlier-Card context pack at group startup (#98, S05; design §5.1; D088–D096, D286, D304; AC01–AC08). `group-start` now writes `earlier-context.json` and `earlier-context.md` in the claim folder before it freezes the job. `src/earlier.rs` selects at most twenty earlier Cards from the startup snapshot's own title export and one bounded text read: a title-vocabulary stage over both Workspaces (with `Relatedness::overlap` for a corpus too uniform for the weighted ranking) then a body stage over the sixty-Card pool plus the target Cards of accepted register terms the group's text names. It excludes the group's own anchor and every group of this book that has not completed, and Literature roots; it keeps Model heads and parts, Topic roots and standalone Cards; a human Card is offered as a read-only reference and never a destination. Each candidate carries its decision evidence — type, facet, Workspace, `destination`, the checker's source evidence, an excerpt of at most 800 Unicode characters, the full text's `text_chars` and `text_sha256`, the `get_card` locator, and why it was selected — with a 16 000-character pack budget and honest omission and sufficiency facts. A read set that crosses archive revisions is retried once and then reported as `read_view_changed`. The pack is frozen in `inputs.earlier_context` and the job artifacts, handed to the writer job's `files`, and named in the prompt, so a resumed start reuses it byte for byte. `Relatedness` gains `rank_text` (score a text that is not a document without changing any weight) and `overlap` (raw shared terms for a homogeneous corpus), both with unit tests. Public coverage: `scripts/book-digest/tests/earlier-context.tests.ps1` (21 assertions, including the new `ZT_DRIFT_MIN_ADDRESSES` seam knob) and the new unit tests of `src/earlier.rs` and `card/relatedness.rs`.

- `2026-10-19` — The audit's layer block now splits advice by the published `(advisory)` text as well as by its advisory rules, so I7's unknown-source line is counted in `layer.advisory` instead of `layer.faults` and `layer.cards_with_faults` (D138). Before this, only rules whose every line is advice (I1, I6, E5, L3) were split, so a layer advisory that shares a hard rule with other faults was miscounted as blocking. Pinned by the layer-advice assertions of `scripts/book-digest/tests/standalone-and-source.tests.ps1`, which compare `layer.faults`/`layer.advisory` with the exact advisory lines in `audit-faults.txt`.

- `2026-10-19` — Add the concept register's plan and publication (#101, S10; design §4.1, §8.3-8.5; D058-D062, D170-D175, D276, D289, D296, D297, D299). `register` has three steps. `plan` reads every agent book term table through zt-check's `check::term_table` and every model-head name, groups each row's preferred word with its `不用` words as the accepted spellings of one concept, ranks one to three entry Cards per term (a definition/statement, a whole Model Card, then a standalone Topic claim), records a term with no defensible target as an unresolved source row, reports two meanings for one spelling as ambiguous, and stages `out\register-plan.json` with the exact body and a content id. `-Review <concept-review.json>` is read-only evidence: a `same` finding supplies its accepted entry, a `different` finding keeps its candidate sense out, a reviewed entry outside the agent Workspace is excluded rather than used, and the plan records the report id. `apply` refuses a plan from another archive or one naming a Card that no longer exists, creates the Topic `索引` and its register when the Workspace has none, otherwise edits that one register while keeping the groups it does not own, writes nothing when the body is already published, and verifies the result through `get_register`. `check` compares the staged plan with the published entries. Public coverage: `register-compiler.tests.ps1` and the module's unit tests.
- `2026-10-19` — Add the concept review of a prepared run (#97, S04; design §4.2, §4.4, §12; D063–D066, D076–D082, D287, D288, D300, D302). `concepts` has four explicit steps — `prepare`, `judge`, `report`, `decision` — and no Card-write path: `prepare` reads the book's term table through zt-check's new public `check::term_table` (all four columns, R6 unchanged), shorts at most ten Topic candidates per proposed concept and ten restatement candidates per proposed statement (deduplicated by unordered pair, with the unselected counts in the evidence), and freezes `evidence.json`, one retained endpoint body per address and the Chinese judge job; `judge` validates canned answers (schema, repeated JSON keys, unknown verdicts, contradictory pairs, missing evidence, unread endpoints) and writes `judged.json`; `report` renders the immutable `concept-review.json` and `concept-review.md` in the staging folder from the frozen files alone, so it starts no client and cannot write a Card; `decision` re-computes the report id and accepts only known findings, the finding's own verdict, one of its remedies and the reviewed fingerprints. A report with unread endpoints or unjudged candidates is `partial` and names them; a view that keeps moving is a changing-view fault after one retry. The evidence carries no binary float, so another reader's parse cannot change its id. Public coverage: `concepts.tests.ps1` and the module's unit tests.
- `2026-10-19` — #93 S02/S03. `audit` passes its already-read complete title projection to zt-check as the bundle `context`, so I6 compares every checked Idea title with its own Workspace without a second read; each audited entry now names `workspace`, `tree`, its kinded `relations` (I7), and `book`, and the style block splits the standalone advice (`advisory`, `advice_by_rule`, summary `style_advisory`) from blocking faults. `card::DEFAULT_CARD_RULES` carries R20, R21, and I7, and `cards-finish` still accepts a report written with the previous complete set. The hub precheck refuses a new line or topic hub that copies its anchor's title (`anchor-title`).
- `2026-10-19` — #93 S03 re-verification: `hub -Step precheck` anchors a new topic-hub draft to both Cards `get_hub` reports `anchor-title` against — its tree's Topic Card `<n>/0` and the Card apply files it under (the parent of the existing topic hub, else `<n>/1`) — so a draft the service would report is refused before it is written, and a draft the precheck refuses is one the report names. `address_parent` mirrors zt's `parent_location` for those addresses.
- `2026-10-18` — Accept the JSON arguments the frozen workflows pass (#90). `prepare -RunOptions` and `ledger fail -Fault` now take JSON text as well as a file (`canon::read_json_arg`): the frozen prepare stage writes `-RunOptions '{"terms":false,…}'` and `zt-digest-lib.js`'s `failUnit` writes `-Fault '<document text>'`, and before this change both commands failed with `Cannot find path …` because they read the value only as a path. The 64 KiB fault bound still applies to either form, and a missing path reports the same `Cannot find path` error as before. Public coverage: `ledger-preparation.tests.ps1` (a prepare given text freezes the declared switches into the run contract) and `ledger-claims.tests.ps1` (a failure given text records the same state as a file, and a stale token with text is refused as `stale_claim`), plus the `canon` unit test for both forms.
- `2026-10-18` — Resume the whole unresolved closure once without rebuilding done work (#87, R274–R285). Add `ledger resume -Out <out>`: it verifies the frozen identity/source/range/contract/options/anchors and the live archive (a moved anchor is `anchor_moved`), refuses a legacy staging folder, a live foreign claim (`run_active`) and a spent budget (`resume_used`), reclaims a lost owner through the guarded path, and opens invocation 2 in one transaction — `resume_count` spent, only the unresolved units' attempts reset (each with an `attempt_reset` event carrying the prior attempts), every blocked unit back to `dependency_evaluation`, done builds untouched — then reports the reclaimed/reset/reevaluated units, the retained done set, the prerequisite and downstream closure and the ready count per layer. `nothing_to_resume` spends nothing. `group-start` recovers a group whose accepted plan already committed: it verifies the plan against the archive receipts, publishes a job with `expected_jobs: 0` and a draft rebound to the current claim, records `group_recovered`, and `group-finish` completes it with no DeepSeek job and no new Card. `Prepared::read` now names a moved prepared anchor precisely instead of a generic missing read. Public coverage: `ledger-resume.tests.ps1` (43 assertions) and two ledger unit tests (`closure_of`, the resume invocation surface).
- `2026-10-18` — Finish with one bounded audit-fix-refresh-final-audit path (#86, R299/R300). Add `finalize` (`src/finalize.rs`): `-Step plan` publishes the scheduling audit revision and derives one immutable audit plan (per-fault disposition `fix`/`conflict`/`blocked`/`stale`/`advisory`, the branch `no_fix`/`fix`/`conflict`/`unresolved`, the single permitted cycle with its exact Cards and rules, the refresh layers and their reasons); `-Step scope` records the one cycle's exact changed-Card/touched-tree manifest from the audited and current authored projections and refuses a second cycle; `-Step refresh` republishes an affected publication at a new revision whose declared scope is that manifest, reusing it for identical changes, and names the layers whose own stage owns their content instead of republishing a stale artifact; `-Step final` runs the read-only audit again (a report at or before the scheduling revision is refused), publishes the next audit revision and writes the final report — new graph revision, refresh facts and every residual hard/source/infrastructure fault as `partial`. The Card compiler gains `-Cards <file>` so the one cycle picks exactly the plan's Cards. Public coverage: `finalize.tests.ps1` (all four branches, the scoped cycle, the reused revision, the refused stale final audit and second scope) and the module's unit tests.

- `2026-10-18` — Settle owned DeepSeek resources and scratch cleanup from ledger evidence (#83, R231, R322–R330). Add the `cleanup` layer's own surface: `ledger resource -Register|-Observe|-List` records the returned conversation ids with the run/unit/token label the moment a manager has them (retaining the accepted answer under `cleanup/evidence/`), creates one cleanup unit per (unit, attempt) with a `settled` dependency on its owner, merges a second conversation of the same workspace, recovers a lost creation response only through this run's own label, and refuses a superseded attempt token. `cleanup-finish` (and its `ledger done` dispatch) verifies terminal state, hash-bound evidence, per-run usage and the confirmed or already-absent deletion of every recorded id, applies the shared workspace rule (removed only when every recorded conversation is absent and the listing shows no unrecorded member), removes only registered scratch inside the staging root after its retained copies exist, and completes with `content_complete` reported apart from `cleanup_complete`. A transient fact refuses as retryable; an unrecorded workspace member or an unrecovered creation response is a recorded `partial` reconciliation. The run-level `cleanup/book@1` is registered at preparation with a `settled` dependency on the last content layer, `ledger status` reports the two completion halves, and the layer closes once every unit outside it is terminal. Public coverage: `cleanup-resources.tests.ps1` and two ledger unit tests (`content_facts`, the label and protected-file rules).

- `2026-10-18` — The managed run's settings, readiness wait and provider cap (#82, R265–R273). `prepare -Variant -MaxAttempts <1..10> -DshRunCap <60..3600> -Apply` validates both finite bounds before writing and persists the effective values in the run contract as `settings`; `ledger status` reports them so a resume reuses the frozen values. `ledger next` gains `-Wait <0..600>` (a bounded tool-side readiness wait that spends no attempt), and `ledger job` records the provider start and the frozen cap and reports `elapsed_seconds`, `cap_remaining_seconds`, `cap_exceeded` and `action` on register, observe, cancel and list. A write-capable step sets `ZT_EDIT_PATIENCE=180` for its child `zt mcp` (`util::writes_cards`); `zt mcp` reads 1–300 seconds and defaults to 20. Public coverage: `ledger-settings.tests.ps1` (settings persistence and refusal, the readiness wait's real duration and its bound, the cap facts, the cancellation observation and the retry), the `writes_cards` unit test and the `zt` `edit_patience` unit test. The Link draft transport remains open.

- `2026-10-18` — Run one whole-book Card cycle with source-review coverage (R217, R247, R296–R298): the model compiler records the architect's checked `<slug>/source-review.json` per model (one reviewed source span and outcome per written part, bound to the accepted plan and the brief's context pack; `validate` prints it and faults a part with no span, an unknown outcome, a `supplied` entry without a note or an unresolvable span), and `model-finish` requires a complete artifact, refusing a supplied part without one of the two established markers. The card cycle gains the deterministic group-covered Idea sample (`-Sample`/`-Seed`, recorded population, addresses, spans, per-group coverage and `proves: "sample"`) and `-ModelReview`, which reviews the Model drafts no model chain owns and records each outcome with its fingerprint. Add `book-cards-finish` (and its `ledger done` dispatch) for a `cards-book/book@N` revision: it re-derives the deterministic pass from the recorded checked Cards, compares it with the recorded fault list, refuses a changed checked Card, requires one reviewed owner per Model draft (the chain's artifact or the cycle's review, and a named `unfinished_chain` for a settled failure), checks the sample record, and records every residual hard fault of its own rule set as an explicit `final-fix` obligation. `-ModelReview` also refuses a malformed supplied-step marker at precheck and apply.

- `2026-10-18` — Supply the chapter endnotes and keep the source faults: `prepare` extracts the notes section outside the digest range into one read-only file per chapter (`prepare/notes.rs`), records each chapter id, original line span, note id and hash in `manifest.json`, maps subsections by explicit headings and note labels, makes an ambiguous or unmapped mapping a preparation fault, splits notes over `-NoteMaxChars` at whole notes and lists the parts with their ids, and appends the chapter's notes (or their labelled parts and the missing-note warning) to every group file and model context pack. Add `source-faults`: the retained record of the architects' structured observations and the preparation's notes faults, validated against the prepared source, deduplicated to one entry per source location with the original observation and every attempt kept, a proposed correction kept apart from a verified one, an unsupported observation kept out of the faults, and a deterministic `<out>\source-faults.md`. `model -Step validate` (and `apply`) records a plan's `source_faults`; `source-notes.tests.ps1` drives it all.

- `2026-10-18` — Resolve a book from the Scholar Archive: `prepare -Book <Work id|title>` (`prepare/resolve.rs`) reads the Work, its ranked Chinese/bilingual Markdown, its BibTeX and its heading range through the read-only bridge, freezes the source hash and every excluded section in `<Out>\preparation.json` and `manifest.json` before group work, returns candidates and exit code 3 when a choice stays open, refuses a disagreeing explicit argument, and names the explicit-source fallback for every missing bridge fact. The digest base 2.4.0 adds `args.book` and the prepare module v4 passes `-Book` alone.

- `2026-10-18` — Report verified coverage, placeholders and exact per-run usage (R331–R346): add `ledger report`, which renders `<out>/run-report.json` and `<out>/run-report.md` from the ledger plus one verified placeholder read (identity, per-layer and per-invocation counts, the four completeness flags, every gap with its unit/address/reason, current placeholder addresses, wall/queue/tool/external timing with one critical path, exact per-run usage and the supported resume line) and is idempotent. Extend `ledger status` with the same facts and `-Verify`. Record per-run usage keyed by provider run id and request id, with labelled estimates, an explicit unavailable reason and data faults, and remove the account-total differencing from the workflows: each stage records the numbers a run's own result reports, and the cleanup stage renders the report after every stage settles.

- `2026-10-18` — Add the checked Model unit and its dependencies: `model-finish` (and its `ledger done` dispatch) completes a `models/gNNN-mIIII@1` unit from the compiler's accepted plan hash, step evidence, exact part map, authored Cards and build checks, refuses any `待写` placeholder, retains structure/Link/note/tree facts and registers the exact explanation needs as `explain/<key>@1`. `model-deps` turns every `X#A` plan reference into a producer dependency before fill, waits without spending an attempt, detects cycles with the real closed path, and refuses a missing or changed named part. `model -Step resume` finishes a partly built model from its first missing step and reuses written parts by their current authored fingerprint; `list-models` reports the checked `todo`/`placeholders` beside the part count.

- `2026-10-18` — Recover lost owners without a second writer: add `ledger reclaim` (per-unit OS guard, expired-lease re-check under `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, forward token fencing) and the `ledger job` register/observe/cancel surface for external runs, refuse reclaim and completion while a registered run is not terminal, and renew a held claim from a 30-second internal heartbeat in every claim-holding command, with the process id as the recorded fact. Windows sharing violations on the unit guard join the retryable `lock_busy` class.

- `2026-10-19` — #110: the Link pass works only the **receipt-owned semantic gap set**. `prepare-link` drops every remaining ownership inference (a Card in the book's own Literature tree is no longer assigned from its address; only `owned-gNN.txt` and the anchor count) and classifies each owned Card into a gap or a skipped Card: an Idea Card with no kinded semantic Link outside its group (parent, source places, register membership and Reverse text never close a gap), a substantive orphan, or an anchor whose `在全书中的位置` paragraph is missing, duplicated or unlinked. It publishes the gap file, the frozen `link-gap-gNN.json` and the gap-only allow-list, and reports `pending`, `no_gap`, `gap_total` and `skipped_total`. New `link-start` freezes the prepared scope against the unit's owned manifest, writes the one job file with its binding and SELF-CHECK, and starts no provider job when the gap set is empty. `link-finish` requires an outcome for every gap Card only, gives each skipped owned Card a verified `already_linked` outcome, refuses a call outside the gap set, requires an anchor paragraph to start with `在全书中的位置` (never a second copy), records `no_link`/`none` with its reason and adds the gap/skipped/counts/L1-L2 quality to its completion. A conflicting receipt is a preparation fault; a lost final reply re-reads the retained accepted plan instead of re-classifying changed Cards. The workflow modules (`zt-link-prepare` v5, `zt-link-pass` v4, `zt-digest-report` v11) and the frozen base 3.6.0 / econ 4.6.0 / math 3.6.0 carry `link_gaps` and the per-group outcomes; `link-gaps.tests.ps1` covers the boundaries.

- `2026-10-18` — #92: `prepare-link` assigns ownership only by receipt (the group's `owned-gNN.txt`, its anchor, and a Card of the book's own Literature tree under its anchor); the `names_section`/`cites_section` inference is removed, so no Card of another book or of an existing Topic tree can reach a link job. A group is pending only when its `digest/gNNN@R` unit is `done`; every other state is reported in `blocked` with an empty allow-list. `ledger next -Lease <1..7200>` makes a claim's lease cover the provider run it starts, and the claimed chain renews it with every 90-second wait round.

Close a dynamic child layer's enumeration when its producer enumeration finishes, recording one closure fact and event.

- `2026-10-18` — Complete the `link/gNNN@1` unit through its exact owned enumeration: add `link-finish` with per-Card kinded-Link or checked-disposition coverage, receipt-owned paragraphs, the Link SELF-CHECK over every owned Card and retained coverage/accepted/SELF-CHECK/completion evidence. Refuse silent omission, invented Cards, raw edits, unkinded or reasonless Links and stale evidence; recover a lost ledger commit from receipts and publish the Link-check revision that consumes the checked result.

- `2026-10-17` — Derive checked aggregate publication revisions from a sorted input manifest at the ledger boundary. Seal each publication layer when its revision registers, make a changed manifest supersede every older open revision, select the latest required revision in `status`, complete a revision only when the whole manifest is accounted for with hash-bound compiler artifacts, verify organize/skeleton/figure/Bridge coverage sets, register one unit per note suggestion with an archive-checked outcome, declare a hub refresh's touched tree with its upper hubs and Register, and reconcile published Card locators from a recorded structural mapping. Every publication reads its groups' Model candidates too, so a repaired model derives the next downstream revision.

Add public interruption, conflict and registration evidence; owner reclamation remains separate.

Remove the global-audit fallback from Card planning/check/report.
